\lambda^{\circ}=\sup\supp\mu_{{\bm{s}}{\bm{t}}}
.
∎

D.4
Specialization to our setting

Our choice of
T
shall be given by
Equation

4.1a
with the function
{\mathcal{T}}
therein satisfying
Item

(A5)
.
This implies
\sup\supp\mu_{{\mathsf{T}}}<1
, and also that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{T}}}{{\mathsf{T}}-1}\right]=\frac{1}{\gamma}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\right]=0,

(D.34)

where
{\mathsf{T}}={\mathcal{T}}({\mathsf{Y}})
(recall
{\mathsf{Y}}
from
Equation

3.10
).
In what follows, in light of
Lemma

D.2
, we apply
Theorem

D.9
with the choices

\displaystyle\mu_{{\bm{s}}}

\displaystyle=\mu_{{\mathsf{\Sigma}}_{n}},

\displaystyle\mu_{{\bm{t}}}

\displaystyle=\mu_{{\mathsf{T}}}.

(D.35)

Note that under these choices, all of
Items

(A21)
,
(A22)
,
(A23)
and
(A24)
required in
Section

D.3
have been covered by
Items

(A2)
and
(A5)
.
In particular, by recalling
{\mathsf{\Sigma}}_{n}
from
Equation

D.5
and its relation to
{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}
in
Equation

3.8
, one may identify
\widetilde{\mu}_{{\bm{s}}}
in
Item

(A22)
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{{\bm{s}}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\law({\mathsf{\Lambda}}^{2}),&\delta\leq 1\\
\delta^{-1}\law({\mathsf{\Lambda}}^{2}),&\delta>1\end{cases}.

Theorem D.15
.

Let
\overline{\psi}(a)=\psi\left(\max\left\{a,a^{\circ}\right\}\right)
.
Then the following three conditions are equivalent.

1.

a^{\circ}<1
;

2.

\overline{\psi}(a^{\circ})<\overline{\psi}(1)
, or more explicitly,
\lambda^{\circ}<\overline{\kappa}_{2}
;

3.

\overline{\psi}^{\prime}(1)>0
, or more explicitly,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}+\delta\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{T}}^{2}}{({\mathsf{T}}-1)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle<1.

(D.36)

Proof.

Notice that
\psi
is eventually increasing by
Item

4
of
Lemma

D.8
.
On the other hand since
a^{\circ}
is the largest critical point, it follows that
\psi
is increasing in
[a^{\circ},\infty)
.
Hence the equivalence of
a^{\circ}<1
,
\overline{\psi}(a^{\circ})<\overline{\psi}(1)
and
\overline{\psi}^{\prime}(1)>0
holds.

For the explicit form of
Item

2
, we note that when
a^{\circ}<1
, by the definition of
\overline{\psi}
, it holds that
\overline{\psi}(1)=\psi(1)
.
We next compute the value of
\psi(1)
. Since

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{{\bm{t}}}{{\bm{t}}-1}\right\rangle=(1\wedge\delta)\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{T}}}{{\mathsf{T}}-1}\right]=0,

we have
1\in{\mathcal{S}}_{{\bm{t}}}
hence
\omega
has a pole at
1
. Thus, by
Equation

D.15
we have
\psi(1)=\langle{\bm{s}}\rangle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Sigma}}_{n}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]=\overline{m}_{2}=\overline{\kappa}_{2}
.

For the explicit form of
Item

3
, note that when
a^{\circ}<1
, it holds that
\overline{\psi}^{\prime}(1)=\psi^{\prime}(1)
.
We finally compute
\psi^{\prime}(1)
by taking the limit
a\to 1
in
Equation

D.17
. For the first term, we use
|\omega(a)|\to\infty
to get

\displaystyle\lim_{a\to 1}\left\langle\frac{{\bm{t}}}{({\bm{t}}-a)^{2}}\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{{\bm{s}}}{({\bm{s}}-\omega(a))^{2}}\right\rangle^{-1}\left\langle\frac{{\bm{s}}^{2}}{({\bm{s}}-\omega(a))^{2}}\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{1}{{\bm{t}}-a}\right\rangle^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\frac{{\bm{t}}}{({\bm{t}}-1)^{2}}\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{1}{{\bm{t}}-1}\right\rangle^{-1}\lim_{a\to 1}\left\langle\frac{{\bm{s}}\omega(a)^{2}}{({\bm{s}}-\omega(a))^{2}}\right\rangle^{-1}\left\langle\frac{{\bm{s}}^{2}\omega(a)^{2}}{({\bm{s}}-\omega(a))^{2}}\right\rangle=\left\langle\frac{{\bm{t}}}{({\bm{t}}-1)^{2}}\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{1}{{\bm{t}}-1}\right\rangle^{-1}\frac{\langle{\bm{s}}^{2}\rangle}{\langle{\bm{s}}\rangle}.

For the second term, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{a\to 1}\left\langle\frac{\omega(a){\bm{s}}}{{\bm{s}}-\omega(a)}\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{1}{{\bm{t}}-a}\right\rangle^{-2}\left\langle\frac{1}{({\bm{t}}-a)^{2}}\right\rangle=-\langle{\bm{s}}\rangle\left\langle\frac{1}{{\bm{t}}-1}\right\rangle^{-2}\left\langle\frac{1}{({\bm{t}}-1)^{2}}\right\rangle.

Thus we conclude

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}(1)=\langle{\bm{s}}\rangle\left\langle\frac{1}{{\bm{t}}-1}\right\rangle^{-2}\left(\frac{\langle{\bm{s}}^{2}\rangle}{\langle{\bm{s}}\rangle^{2}}\left\langle\frac{{\bm{t}}}{({\bm{t}}-1)^{2}}\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{1}{{\bm{t}}-1}\right\rangle+\left\langle\frac{1}{({\bm{t}}-1)^{2}}\right\rangle\right).

Note that
\langle{\bm{t}}/({\bm{t}}-1)\rangle=0
implies

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{1}{{\bm{t}}-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-1,

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{1}{({\bm{t}}-1)^{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=1+\left\langle\frac{{\bm{t}}}{({\bm{t}}-1)^{2}}\right\rangle=1+\left\langle\frac{{\bm{t}}^{2}}{({\bm{t}}-1)^{2}}\right\rangle,

from which we can further simplify the last expression as

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}(1)=\langle{\bm{s}}\rangle\left(\left(-\frac{\langle{\bm{s}}^{2}\rangle}{\langle{\bm{s}}\rangle^{2}}+1\right)\left\langle\frac{{\bm{t}}^{2}}{({\bm{t}}-1)^{2}}\right\rangle+1\right).

Recalling the definitions of
{\bm{s}}
and
{\bm{t}}
from
Equation

D.35
, we compute

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}(1)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Sigma}}_{n}\right]\left(\left(-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Sigma}}_{n}^{2}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Sigma}}_{n}\right]^{2}}+1\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{T}}^{2}}{({\mathsf{T}}-1)^{2}}\right]+1\right)

\displaystyle=\overline{m}_{2}\left(1-\left(\frac{\overline{m}_{4}-\overline{m}_{2}^{2}}{\overline{m}_{2}^{2}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{T}}^{2}}{({\mathsf{T}}-1)^{2}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\overline{\kappa}_{2}\left(1-\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}+\delta\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{T}}^{2}}{({\mathsf{T}}-1)^{2}}\right]\right).

Therefore,
\psi^{\prime}(1)>0
is equivalent to
Equation

D.36
.
∎

Appendix E
Proof of
Lemma

B.3

Recall from
Equation

C.1
the linearized GVAMP iteration

\displaystyle u^{t}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}XX^{\top}-I_{n}\right)\left(\widetilde{G}-I_{n}\right)u^{t-1},

(E.1)

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}\coloneqq\diag(\overline{g}(y))+I_{n}.

(E.2)

With the notation

\displaystyle\beta_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\|u^{t}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\widehat{u}^{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{u^{t}}{\beta_{t}},

(E.3)

we can write
Equation

E.1
as

\displaystyle\frac{\beta_{t}}{\beta_{t-1}}\widehat{u}^{t}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}XX^{\top}-I_{n}\right)\left(\widetilde{G}-I_{n}\right)\widehat{u}^{t-1}.

(E.4)

Lemma E.1
.

Let
\beta_{t}
be defined in
Equation

E.3
.
The following limits hold:

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle u^{t},u^{t-1}\right\rangle}{\left\|u^{t-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}}=\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\beta_{t}}{\beta_{t-1}}

\displaystyle=\gamma,

(E.5)

where
\gamma
is defined in
Equation

4.2
.

The proof of
Lemma

E.1
is deferred to
Section

E.1
.

Adding
(\widetilde{G}-I_{n})\widehat{u}^{t}
to both sides of
Equation

E.4
, we have

\displaystyle\left[\left(\frac{\beta_{t}}{\beta_{t-1}}-1\right)I_{n}+\widetilde{G}\right]\widehat{u}^{t}

\displaystyle=\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}XX^{\top}\left(\widetilde{G}-I_{n}\right)\widehat{u}^{t-1}+\left(\widetilde{G}-I_{n}\right)\left(\widehat{u}^{t}-\widehat{u}^{t-1}\right).

Before proceeding, recall from the definitions
Equation

E.2
of
\widetilde{G}
that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\beta_{t}}{\beta_{t-1}}-1\right)I_{n}+\widetilde{G}

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta_{t}}{\beta_{t-1}}I_{n}+\diag(\overline{g}(y)).

(E.6)

By the second identity of
Equation

E.5
and the first assumption on
\overline{g}
in
Equation

4.3
, the above matrix is strictly positive definite with probability
1
for all sufficiently large
n
.
Now, let us multiply both sides by
X^{\top}\left(\widetilde{G}-I_{n}\right)\left[\left(\frac{\beta_{t}}{\beta_{t-1}}-1\right)I_{n}+\widetilde{G}\right]^{-1}
:

\displaystyle X^{\top}\left(\widetilde{G}-I_{n}\right)\widehat{u}^{t}

\displaystyle=\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}X^{\top}\left(\widetilde{G}-I_{n}\right)\left[\left(\frac{\beta_{t}}{\beta_{t-1}}-1\right)I_{n}+\widetilde{G}\right]^{-1}XX^{\top}\left(\widetilde{G}-I_{n}\right)\widehat{u}^{t-1}

\displaystyle\quad+X^{\top}\left(\widetilde{G}-I_{n}\right)^{2}\left[\left(\frac{\beta_{t}}{\beta_{t-1}}-1\right)I_{n}+\widetilde{G}\right]^{-1}\left(\widehat{u}^{t}-\widehat{u}^{t-1}\right).

Further define

\displaystyle\widehat{v}^{t+1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq X^{\top}\left(\widetilde{G}-I_{n}\right)\widehat{u}^{t},

(E.7)

and rewrite the above iteration as:

\displaystyle\widehat{v}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}X^{\top}\left(\widetilde{G}-I_{n}\right)\left[\left(\gamma-1\right)I_{n}+\widetilde{G}\right]^{-1}X\widehat{v}^{t}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}X^{\top}\left(\widetilde{G}-I_{n}\right)\left\{\left[\left(\frac{\beta_{t}}{\beta_{t-1}}-1\right)I_{n}+\widetilde{G}\right]^{-1}-\left[\left(\gamma-1\right)I_{n}+\widetilde{G}\right]^{-1}\right\}X\widehat{v}^{t}}_{\widehat{e}_{2}^{t}}

(E.8)

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{X^{\top}\left(\widetilde{G}-I_{n}\right)^{2}\left[\left(\frac{\beta_{t}}{\beta_{t-1}}-1\right)I_{n}+\widetilde{G}\right]^{-1}\left(\widehat{u}^{t}-\widehat{u}^{t-1}\right)}_{\widehat{e}_{1}^{t}}.

(E.9)

Recalling the definition of
D
in
Equation

4.1a
, the form of
{\mathcal{T}}
in
Equation

4.2
and the notation
\widetilde{G}
in
Equation

E.2
, we recognize

\displaystyle\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}X^{\top}\left(\widetilde{G}-I_{n}\right)\left[\left(\gamma-1\right)I_{n}+\widetilde{G}\right]^{-1}X

\displaystyle=\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}D.

So we arrive at

\displaystyle\widehat{v}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}D\widehat{v}^{t}+\widehat{e}_{2}^{t}+\widehat{e}_{1}^{t}.

We shift the spectrum of
\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}D
to the right so that the resulting matrix is positive definite (with probability
1
for all sufficiently large
n
).
Take
\ell>0
to be a sufficiently large constant such that
\mathbb{P}\left(\lambda_{d}(\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}D+\ell I_{d})>0\right)=1
for all sufficiently large
d
.
Then, the iteration can be re-written as

\displaystyle\widehat{v}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}D+\ell I_{d}}{1+\ell}}_{\widetilde{D}}\widehat{v}^{t}+\underbrace{\frac{\ell}{1+\ell}\left(\widehat{v}^{t+1}-\widehat{v}^{t}\right)+\frac{1}{1+\ell}\left(\widehat{e}_{2}^{t}+\widehat{e}_{1}^{t}\right)}_{\widehat{e}_{3}^{t}}.

(E.10)

We note that
D
and
\widetilde{D}
have the same eigenvectors.
Unrolling the above iteration for
s
steps, we get

\displaystyle\widehat{v}^{t+s}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{D}^{s}\widehat{v}^{t}+\widehat{e}^{t,s},

(E.11)

where

\displaystyle\widehat{e}^{t,s}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}\widetilde{D}^{s-1-i}\widehat{e}_{3}^{t+i}.

(E.12)

Let us examine the limiting squared norm of both sides of
Equation

E.11
.
For the left-hand side, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{v}^{t+s}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{v}^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\|X^{\top}\left(\widetilde{G}-I_{n}\right)u^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{\left\|u^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}\left\|v^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{\left\|u^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}\left(\chi_{t+1}^{2}\rho+\omega_{t+1}^{2}\right)}{\nu_{t}^{2}\sigma^{2}+\tau_{t}^{2}}=\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\chi_{t+1}^{2}}{\nu_{t}^{2}}\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}\left(\rho+\frac{\omega_{t+1}^{2}}{\chi_{t+1}^{2}}\right)}{\sigma^{2}+\frac{\tau_{t}^{2}}{\nu_{t}^{2}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}w_{4}^{2}\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}\left(\rho+w_{2}\right)}{\sigma^{2}+w_{1}}\in(0,\infty),

(E.13)

where the equality
(a)
is obtained by recalling the update rules for
v^{t}
in the linearized GVAMP dynamics
Equation

C.1
,
(b)
follows from
Lemmas

C.1
and
C.2
, and
(c)
from
Equation

B.5
.

We then turn to the right-hand side of
Equation

E.11
.
The following lemma, whose proof is deferred to
Section

E.2
, shows that the error term
\widehat{e}^{t,s}
is asymptotically negligible.

Lemma E.2
.

The error vector
\widehat{e}^{t,s}
defined in
Equation

E.12
satisfies

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{e}^{t,s}\right\|_{2}^{2}=0.

By
Lemma

E.2
and the spectral decomposition of
\widetilde{D}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widetilde{D}^{s}\widehat{v}^{t}+\widehat{e}^{t,s}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\lim_{s\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widetilde{D}^{s}\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{s\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(\widetilde{D})^{2s}\left\langle v_{1}(D),\widehat{v}^{t}\right\rangle^{2}+\sum_{i=2}^{d}\lambda_{i}(\widetilde{D})^{2s}\left\langle v_{i}(D),\widehat{v}^{t}\right\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{s\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widetilde{D}^{s}\Pi\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\widetilde{D}^{s}\Pi^{\perp}\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2},

(E.14)

where

\displaystyle\Pi

\displaystyle\coloneqq v_{1}(D)v_{1}(D)^{\top},

\displaystyle\Pi^{\perp}

\displaystyle\coloneqq I_{d}-\Pi.

The second term in
Equation

E.14
can be upper bounded as

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{D}^{s}\Pi^{\perp}\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left(\widetilde{D}\Pi^{\perp}\right)^{s}\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\sigma_{1}\left(\left(\widetilde{D}\Pi^{\perp}\right)^{s}\right)^{2}=\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lambda_{1}\left(\widetilde{D}\Pi^{\perp}\right)^{2s}=\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lambda_{2}(\widetilde{D})^{2s},

(E.15)

where we have used the positive definiteness of
\widetilde{D}\Pi^{\perp}
in the penultimate equality.

By
Lemma

B.2
,

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{2}(D)

\displaystyle=\lambda^{\circ}<\overline{\kappa}_{2},

where the strict inequality holds under the assumption
Equation

4.13
.
In view of the definition of
\widetilde{D}
in
Equation

E.10
, this implies

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{2}(\widetilde{D})

\displaystyle=\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}\lambda^{\circ}+\ell}{1+\ell}<1.

Getting back to
Equation

E.15
, using the above bound and the boundedness of
\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}
shown in
Equation

E.13
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widetilde{D}^{s}\Pi^{\perp}\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\left(\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)\left(\lim_{s\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{2}(\widetilde{D})^{2s}\right)

\displaystyle=0.

(E.16)

Using
Equation

E.16
in
Equation

E.14
yields

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widetilde{D}^{s}\widehat{v}^{t}+\widehat{e}^{t,s}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{s\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(\widetilde{D})^{2s}\left\langle v_{1}(D),\widehat{v}^{t}\right\rangle^{2}.

(E.17)

Finally, combining
Equations

E.17
and
E.13
brings us to

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\frac{\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(\widetilde{D})^{2s}\left\langle v_{1}(D),\widehat{v}^{t}\right\rangle^{2}}{\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}},

implying

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(\widetilde{D})

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle v_{1}(D),\widehat{v}^{t}\right\rangle^{2}}{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=1.

Recalling the definitions of
\widetilde{D}
in
Equation

E.10
and noting from
Equation

E.7
that
\widehat{v}^{t}=\overline{\kappa}_{2}v^{t}
, we conclude:

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(D)

\displaystyle=\overline{\kappa}_{2},

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle v_{1}(D),v^{t}\right\rangle^{2}}{\left\|v^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=1,

as claimed in
Lemma

B.3
.

E.1
Proof of
Lemma

E.1

Recalling the state evolution representation in
Lemma

C.2
, let

\displaystyle n_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{N}}_{t}{\mathsf{N}}_{t-1}\right]=\frac{1}{\tau_{t}\tau_{t-1}}\left(\Sigma_{t+1,t}-\nu_{t}\nu_{t-1}\sigma^{2}\right).

(E.18)

We first show

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}n_{t}

\displaystyle=1.

(E.19)

To this end, we begin by deriving a recursion for
n_{t}
.
From the state evolution recursions
Equations

C.9
and
C.10
,

\displaystyle\Sigma_{t+1,t}

\displaystyle=\overline{\kappa}_{2}\left(\chi_{t}\chi_{t-1}\rho+\Omega_{t,t-1}\right)+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\rho\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]\left(\nu_{t-1}\chi_{t-1}+\nu_{t-2}\chi_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\tau_{t-1}{\mathsf{N}}_{t-1}\cdot\tau_{t-2}{\mathsf{N}}_{t-2}\right]+\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{6}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]^{2}\right)\rho\nu_{t-1}\nu_{t-2},

\displaystyle\Omega_{t+1,t}

\displaystyle=\delta\left\{\rho\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]^{2}\right)\nu_{t}\nu_{t-1}+\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\tau_{t}{\mathsf{N}}_{t}\cdot\tau_{t-1}{\mathsf{N}}_{t-1}\right]\right\}.

Using the definition
Equation

E.18
of
n_{t}
, we obtain

\displaystyle n_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{t}\tau_{t-1}}\bigg\{\overline{\kappa}_{2}\left(\chi_{t}\chi_{t-1}\rho+\Omega_{t,t-1}\right)+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\rho\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]\left(\nu_{t-1}\chi_{t-1}+\nu_{t-2}\chi_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]\tau_{t-1}\tau_{t-2}n_{t-1}+\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{6}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]^{2}\right)\rho\nu_{t-1}\nu_{t-2}-\sigma^{2}\nu_{t-1}\nu_{t}\bigg\},

\displaystyle\Omega_{t+1,t}

\displaystyle=\delta\left\{\rho\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]^{2}\right)\nu_{t}\nu_{t-1}+\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]\tau_{t}\tau_{t-1}n_{t}\right\}.

Eliminating
\Omega_{t,t-1}
, we get

\displaystyle n_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{t}\tau_{t-1}}\bigg\{\overline{\kappa}_{2}\chi_{t}\chi_{t-1}\rho+\delta\overline{\kappa}_{2}\rho\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]^{2}\right)\nu_{t-1}\nu_{t-2}+\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]\tau_{t-1}\tau_{t-2}n_{t-1}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\rho\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]\left(\nu_{t-1}\chi_{t-1}+\nu_{t-2}\chi_{t}\right)+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]\tau_{t-1}\tau_{t-2}n_{t-1}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{6}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]^{2}\right)\rho\nu_{t-1}\nu_{t-2}-\sigma^{2}\nu_{t-1}\nu_{t}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{t}\tau_{t-1}}\bigg\{\overline{\kappa}_{2}\chi_{t}\chi_{t-1}\rho+\rho\left(\left(\delta\overline{\kappa}_{2}+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]+\left(\delta\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{6}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]^{2}\right)\nu_{t-1}\nu_{t-2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\delta+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]\tau_{t-1}\tau_{t-2}n_{t-1}+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\rho\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]\left(\nu_{t-1}\chi_{t-1}+\nu_{t-2}\chi_{t}\right)-\sigma^{2}\nu_{t-1}\nu_{t}\bigg\}.

(E.20)

Recalling
w_{1},w_{2},w_{3},w_{4}
, and
\gamma
from
Lemma

B.1
and noting that
w_{3}w_{4}=\gamma
, we have the following identities for every sufficiently large
t
:

\displaystyle\frac{\chi_{t-1}\chi_{t}}{\tau_{t-1}\tau_{t}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\chi_{t-1}\chi_{t}}{\nu_{t-1}\nu_{t}}\frac{\nu_{t-1}\nu_{t}}{\tau_{t-1}\tau_{t}}=w_{3}^{-2}w_{1}^{-1},

\displaystyle\frac{\nu_{t-2}\nu_{t-1}}{\tau_{t-1}\tau_{t}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\nu_{t-2}\nu_{t-1}}{\nu_{t-1}\nu_{t}}\frac{\nu_{t-1}\nu_{t}}{\tau_{t-1}\tau_{t}}=\gamma^{-2}w_{1}^{-1},

\displaystyle\frac{\tau_{t-2}}{\tau_{t}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau_{t-2}}{\nu_{t-2}}\frac{\nu_{t-2}}{\nu_{t-1}}\frac{\nu_{t-1}}{\nu_{t}}\frac{\nu_{t}}{\tau_{t}}=\gamma^{-2},

\displaystyle\frac{\chi_{t-1}\nu_{t-1}}{\tau_{t-1}\tau_{t}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\chi_{t-1}}{\nu_{t-1}}\frac{\nu_{t-1}}{\tau_{t-1}}\frac{\nu_{t-1}}{\nu_{t}}\frac{\nu_{t}}{\tau_{t}}=w_{3}^{-1}w_{1}^{-1}\gamma^{-1},

\displaystyle\frac{\chi_{t}\nu_{t-2}}{\tau_{t-1}\tau_{t}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\chi_{t}}{\nu_{t-1}}\frac{\nu_{t-1}}{\tau_{t-1}}\frac{\nu_{t-2}}{\nu_{t-1}}\frac{\nu_{t-1}}{\nu_{t}}\frac{\nu_{t}}{\tau_{t}}=w_{4}w_{1}^{-1}\gamma^{-2},

\displaystyle\frac{\nu_{t-1}\nu_{t}}{\tau_{t-1}\tau_{t}}

\displaystyle=w_{1}^{-1}.

Dividing both sides of
Equation

E.20
by
\tau_{t}\tau_{t-1}
and using the above identities, we have

\displaystyle n_{t}

\displaystyle=\rho\overline{\kappa}_{2}w_{3}^{-2}w_{1}^{-1}+\rho\left\{\left(\delta\overline{\kappa}_{2}+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]+\left(\delta\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{6}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]^{2}\right\}\gamma^{-2}w_{1}^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\delta+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]\gamma^{-2}n_{t-1}+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\rho\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]\left(w_{4}w_{1}^{-1}\gamma^{-2}+w_{3}^{-1}w_{1}^{-1}\gamma^{-1}\right)-\sigma^{2}w_{1}^{-1},

\displaystyle=An_{t-1}+B,

(E.21)

where

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(\delta+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]\gamma^{-2}=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]}{\left(\delta+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]^{2}},

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\coloneqq\rho\overline{\kappa}_{2}w_{3}^{-2}w_{1}^{-1}+\rho\left\{\left(\delta\overline{\kappa}_{2}+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]+\left(\delta\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{6}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]^{2}\right\}\gamma^{-2}w_{1}^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\rho\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]\left(w_{4}w_{1}^{-1}\gamma^{-2}+w_{3}^{-1}w_{1}^{-1}\gamma^{-1}\right)-\sigma^{2}w_{1}^{-1}.

Note that
A\in(0,1)
since the criticality condition
Equation

4.13
holds.

Recall that we would like to prove
Equation

E.19
. We will show that:

\displaystyle 1\leq\underline{n}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\liminf_{t\to\infty}n_{t}\leq\limsup_{t\to\infty}n_{t}\eqqcolon\overline{n}\leq 1.

To prove the right-most inequality, we take
\limsup
on both sides of
Equation

E.21
and apply sub-additivity of
\limsup
:
\overline{n}\leq A\overline{n}+B
, implying
\overline{n}\leq B/(1-A)
.
Using the definitions of
A,B
, it can be verified with a lengthy calculation that
B/(1-A)=1
.
Therefore
\overline{n}\leq 1
.
Similarly, super-additivity of
\liminf
allows us to show the left-most inequality
\underline{n}\geq 1
.
Therefore
Equation

E.19
holds.

Now consider the first limit in
Equation

E.5
.
Using the definition
Equation

E.18
of
n_{t}
, its limit in
Equation

E.19
and
Lemma

B.1
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle u^{t},u^{t-1}\right\rangle}{\left\|u^{t-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\Sigma_{t+1,t}}{\Sigma_{t,t}}=\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\tau_{t}\tau_{t-1}n_{t}+\nu_{t}\nu_{t-1}\sigma^{2}}{\tau_{t-1}^{2}+\nu_{t-1}^{2}\sigma^{2}}=\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\frac{\tau_{t}\tau_{t-1}}{\nu_{t}\nu_{t-1}}n_{t}+\sigma^{2}}{\frac{\nu_{t-1}}{\nu_{t}}\frac{\tau_{t-1}^{2}}{\nu_{t-1}^{2}}+\frac{\nu_{t-1}}{\nu_{t}}\sigma^{2}}=\frac{w_{1}+\sigma^{2}}{\gamma^{-1}w_{1}+\gamma^{-1}\sigma^{2}}=\gamma,

as claimed.

Turning to the second limit in
Equation

E.5
, again using
Lemma

B.1
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\left\|u^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{\left\|u^{t-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\nu_{t}^{2}\sigma^{2}+\tau_{t}^{2}}{\nu_{t-1}^{2}\sigma^{2}+\tau_{t-1}^{2}}=\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\nu_{t}^{2}}{\nu_{t-1}^{2}}\frac{\sigma^{2}+\frac{\tau_{t}^{2}}{\nu_{t}^{2}}}{\sigma^{2}+\frac{\tau_{t-1}^{2}}{\nu_{t-1}^{2}}}=\gamma^{2}\frac{\sigma^{2}+w_{1}}{\sigma^{2}+w_{1}}=\gamma^{2},

implying, by the positivity of
\gamma
(see
Item

(A5)
) that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\left\|u^{t}\right\|_{2}}{\left\|u^{t-1}\right\|_{2}}

\displaystyle=\gamma,

as claimed in the second equality of
Equation

E.5
.

E.2
Proof of
Lemma

E.2

First, by the definition
Equation

E.8
, we can write
\widehat{e}_{2}^{t}
as

\displaystyle\widehat{e}_{2}^{t}

\displaystyle=\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}\left(\gamma-\frac{\beta_{t}}{\beta_{t-1}}\right)X^{\top}\left(\widetilde{G}-I_{n}\right)\left[\left(\frac{\beta_{t}}{\beta_{t-1}}-1\right)I_{n}+\widetilde{G}\right]^{-1}\left[\left(\gamma-1\right)I_{n}+\widetilde{G}\right]^{-1}X\widehat{v}^{t}.

Then we immediately have

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{e}_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=0,

(E.22)

since by
Lemma

E.1
,

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\beta_{t}}{\beta_{t-1}}

\displaystyle=\gamma,

and by
Equation

E.13
and the invertibility of
Equation

E.6
,

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}X^{\top}\left(\widetilde{G}-I_{n}\right)\left\{\left[\left(\frac{\beta_{t}}{\beta_{t-1}}-1\right)I_{n}+\widetilde{G}\right]^{-1}\left[\left(\gamma-1\right)I_{n}+\widetilde{G}\right]^{-1}\right\}X\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle<\infty.

Next, we claim that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{u}^{t}-\widehat{u}^{t-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=0.

(E.23)

Expanding the square on the left-hand side, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{u}^{t}-\widehat{u}^{t-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=2-2\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle u^{t},u^{t-1}\right\rangle}{\left\|u^{t}\right\|_{2}\left\|u^{t-1}\right\|_{2}},

(E.24)

since
\widehat{u}^{t}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{n-1}
by the definition
Equation

E.3
.
Invoking
Lemma

E.1
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle u^{t},u^{t-1}\right\rangle}{\left\|u^{t}\right\|_{2}\left\|u^{t-1}\right\|_{2}}

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\beta_{t-1}}{\beta_{t}}\frac{\left\langle u^{t},u^{t-1}\right\rangle}{\left\|u^{t-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}}=1,

which implies
Equation

E.23
.

In view of the definition of
\widehat{e}_{1}^{t}
in
Equation

E.9
and
\widehat{v}^{t}
in
Equation

E.7
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{e}_{1}^{t}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{v}^{t+1}-\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=0.

(E.25)

Recalling the definition of
\widehat{e}_{3}^{t}
in
Equation

E.10
, by
Equations

E.25
and
E.22
, we then have

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{e}_{3}^{t}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=0.

(E.26)

Finally, consider
\widehat{e}^{t,s}
defined in
Equation

E.12
.
Using
Equation

E.26
and the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{e}^{t,s}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=0.

Appendix F
Proof of
Theorem

4.2

Proof of
Item

2
.

Consider any spectral estimator identified with a function
\overline{g}
satisfying
Equation

4.3
such that the criticality condition
Equation

4.13
holds.
By
Equation

4.3
and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\left(\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}-1\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\iint p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z)Q(y\,|\,z)\overline{g}(y)\left(\sigma^{-2}z^{2}-1\right)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[Q(y\,|\,{\mathsf{Z}})\left(\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}-1\right)\right]}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[Q(y\,|\,{\mathsf{Z}})\right]}}\overline{g}(y)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[Q(y\,|\,{\mathsf{Z}})\right]}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[Q(y\,|\,{\mathsf{Z}})\left(\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}-1\right)\right]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[Q(y\,|\,{\mathsf{Z}})\right]}\,\mathrm{d}y\right)^{1/2}\left(\int\overline{g}(y)^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[Q(y\,|\,{\mathsf{Z}})\right]\,\mathrm{d}y\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\left(\int\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[Q(y\,|\,{\mathsf{Z}})\left(\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}-1\right)\right]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[Q(y\,|\,{\mathsf{Z}})\right]}\,\mathrm{d}y\right)^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

Using this in
Equation

4.13
, we deduce the following inequality:

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle<\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}+\delta\right)\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]}\leq\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}+\delta\right)\int\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[Q(y\,|\,{\mathsf{Z}})\left(\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}-1\right)\right]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[Q(y\,|\,{\mathsf{Z}})\right]}\,\mathrm{d}y,

which is equivalent to
Equation

4.15
, as desired.

Proof of
Item

1
.

Assume that
Equation

4.15
holds and consider
\overline{g}
given by
Equation

4.16
.
We can compute:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\iint p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z)Q(y\,|\,z)\frac{\int p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z^{\prime})\left(\frac{z^{\prime 2}}{\sigma^{2}}-1\right)Q(y\,|\,z^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}}{\int p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z^{\prime\prime})Q(y\,|\,z^{\prime\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime\prime}}\frac{z^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\int p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z^{\prime})\left(\frac{z^{\prime 2}}{\sigma^{2}}-1\right)Q(y\,|\,z^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}}{\int p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z^{\prime\prime})Q(y\,|\,z^{\prime\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime\prime}}\left[\int p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z)Q(y\,|\,z)\frac{z^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}z\right]\,\mathrm{d}y,

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\int p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z^{\prime})\left(\frac{z^{\prime 2}}{\sigma^{2}}-1\right)Q(y\,|\,z^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}}{\int p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z^{\prime\prime})Q(y\,|\,z^{\prime\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime\prime}}\left[\int p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z)Q(y\,|\,z)\left(\frac{z^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}-1\right)\,\mathrm{d}z+\int p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z)Q(y\,|\,z)\,\mathrm{d}z\right]\,\mathrm{d}y,

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\left[\int p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z^{\prime})\left(\frac{z^{\prime 2}}{\sigma^{2}}-1\right)Q(y\,|\,z^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\right]^{2}}{\int p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z^{\prime\prime})Q(y\,|\,z^{\prime\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}y+\iint p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z^{\prime})\left(\frac{z^{\prime 2}}{\sigma^{2}}-1\right)Q(y\,|\,z^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}-1\right)Q(y\,|\,{\mathsf{Z}})\right]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[Q(y\,|\,{\mathsf{Z}})\right]}\,\mathrm{d}y,

(F.1)

where
Equation

F.1
follows since
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}^{2}\right]=\sigma^{2}
, therefore the second term in the previous step vanishes. We also have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\int p_{\mathsf{Y}}(y)\frac{\left[\int p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z^{\prime})\left(\frac{z^{\prime 2}}{\sigma^{2}}-1\right)Q(y\,|\,z^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\right]^{2}}{\left[\int p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z^{\prime\prime})Q(y\,|\,z^{\prime\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime\prime}\right]^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\left[\int p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z^{\prime})\left(\frac{z^{\prime 2}}{\sigma^{2}}-1\right)Q(y\,|\,z^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\right]^{2}}{\int p_{\mathsf{Z}}(z^{\prime\prime})Q(y\,|\,z^{\prime\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}y=\int\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}-1\right)Q(y\,|\,{\mathsf{Z}})\right]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[Q(y\,|\,{\mathsf{Z}})\right]}\,\mathrm{d}y.

(F.2)

By the above expressions, the condition
Equation

4.15
is equivalent to
Equation

4.13
.

Next, we verify the validity of
Equations

4.3
and
4.4
for the choice of
\overline{g}
in
Equation

4.16
.
The middle identity of
Equation

4.3
holds by the tower property of conditional expectation:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}-1\,|\,{\mathsf{Y}}\right]\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]-1=0.

For the third inequality of
Equation

4.3
, recall from
Equation

F.1
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}-1\,|\,{\mathsf{Y}}\right]^{2}\right],

which is positive by the assumption
Equation

4.17
.

For the first inequality in
Equation

4.3
, observe that
\overline{g}(y)=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\,|\,{\mathsf{Y}}=y\right]-1\geq-1
for any
y
.
Since
\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]
by
Equations

F.1
and
F.2
, we have

\displaystyle\overline{g}(y)

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]}>-\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}+\delta\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]=-\gamma,

where the strict inequality is by
Equation

4.13
.

Finally, we move to
Equation

4.4
.
We claim that
\supp\mu_{{\mathsf{T}}}
is finite.
Indeed, for any
y\in\supp{\mathsf{Y}}
, we have
{\mathcal{T}}(y)=1-\frac{\gamma}{\overline{g}(y)+\gamma}\leq 1
since the second term is positive.
If
{\mathsf{T}}
has a point mass at
\supp\mu_{{\mathsf{T}}}
, then
Equation

4.4
holds.
Otherwise, we can perturb
{\mathsf{T}}
by adding a small point mass at
\supp\mu_{{\mathsf{T}}}
while preserving other properties required by
Item

(A5)
.
As long as the perturbation is sufficiently small, the perturbed preprocessing function still satisfies
Equation

4.13
and therefore achieves positive limiting overlap.
This type of argument has been employed in multiple prior works (see e.g.
[
70
, Proof of Theorem 2]
or
[
105
, Section C]
) and we omit the details.

Appendix G
Proof of
Theorem

4.3

The proof of the state evolution result for spectrally initialized GVAMP (
Theorem

4.3
) consists of
3
major steps.
First,
Theorem

G.1
below provides a state evolution result for GVAMP with an ‘oracle’ initialization that is independent of the data
(X,y)
.
Some technical assumptions required by this result are then relaxed in
Theorem

G.2
.
Note that both theorems above exclude spectral initialization which correlates with
(X,y)
.
This issue is resolved in
Section

G.3
by simulating spectrally initialized GVAMP with a two-phase GVAMP algorithm.

During the finalization of this work, we became aware of the recent independent work
[
28
]
which contains in Appendix B a state evolution result for a general Orthogonal Approximate Message Passing (OAMP) algorithm.
A general OAMP takes a similar shape to
Equation

4.19
, with a distinction that it does not assume any underlying statistical model such as
Equation

3.4
.
On the other hand, a general OAMP in
[
28
]
allows the vector denoisers
f_{t},g_{t}
to depend on all iterates up to time
t
(subject to divergence-free conditions with respect to all arguments).
Due to this flexibility, we acknowledge the possibility of proving
Theorem

G.1
by reducing GVAMP to general OAMP.
However, treating spectral initialization promised by
Theorem

4.3
still requires techniques beyond those in
[
28
]
for spiked matrix models.
We therefore opt to proceed with our proof from first principles.
Furthermore, it is straightforward to extend our results to allow memory in the vector denoisers as in
[
28
]
.
Since such extensions are not necessary for
Section

4.3
, we do not pursue them so as to keep the notation more succinct.

Let us start with
Theorem

G.1
which requires additional assumptions listed below.

(A25)

For every
t\geq 0
, both
\max\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}\right],\mathrm{Var}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})\right]\right\}>0
and
\max\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}\right],\mathrm{Var}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})\right]\right\}>0
hold.

(A26)

\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}
is not equal to
\pm{\mathsf{B}}_{*}
almost surely and
\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}
is not equal to
\pm{\mathsf{Z}}
almost surely.
For every
t\geq 0
, there do not exist constants
\beta_{0},\cdots,\beta_{t},\alpha_{0},\cdots,\alpha_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that

either

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t+1}-\widetilde{b}_{t+1}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\beta_{s}\left(\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{s}-\widetilde{b}_{s}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right)

or

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t+1}-\widetilde{a}_{t+1}{\mathsf{Z}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\alpha_{s}\left(\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{s}-\widetilde{a}_{s}{\mathsf{Z}}\right)

holds almost surely.

(A27)

For every
t\geq 1
, the function
f_{t}\in\mathrm{PG}_{(2+d^{\prime})\to 1}
is weakly differentiable in the first argument and continuous in other arguments.
The weak derivative of
r\mapsto f_{t}(r;b,\theta)
, denoted by
f_{t}^{\prime}(r;b,\theta)
, is also in
\mathrm{PG}_{(2+d^{\prime})\to 1}
, and
f_{t}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{t};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})
is continuous with probability
1
.

For every
t\geq 1
, the function
g_{t}\in\mathrm{PG}_{(3+n^{\prime})\to 1}
is weakly differentiable in the first argument and continuous in other arguments.
The weak derivative of
p\mapsto g_{t}(p;z,e,\xi)
, denoted by
g_{t}^{\prime}(p;z,e,\xi)
, is also in
\mathrm{PG}_{(3+n^{\prime})\to 1}
, and
g_{t}^{\prime}({\mathsf{P}}_{t};{\mathsf{Z}},{\mathsf{E}},{\mathsf{\Xi}})
is continuous with probability
1
.

The initializers and side information are required to satisfy the following assumption.

(A28)

\Theta,\Xi
are independent of each other and of
O,Q
from
Item

(A2)
.
The initializers
\widetilde{r}^{0},\widetilde{p}^{0}
take the form

\displaystyle\widetilde{r}^{0}

\displaystyle=f_{0}(\beta_{*},\Theta),

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}^{0}

\displaystyle=g_{0}(z,\varepsilon,\Xi)

for some continuous functions
f_{0}\in\mathrm{PG}_{(1+d^{\prime})\to 1}
and
g_{0}\in\mathrm{PG}_{(2+n^{\prime})\to 1}
, and satisfy

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{r}^{0}&\beta_{*}&\Theta\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}&{\mathsf{B}}_{*}&{\mathsf{\Theta}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{p}^{0}&z&\varepsilon&\Xi\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}&{\mathsf{Z}}&{\mathsf{E}}&{\mathsf{\Xi}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

(G.1)

where the random variables on the right sides are independent of
{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n},{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}
.

Theorem G.1
.

Consider the GVAMP iteration in
Equation

4.19
with initialization
Equation

G.1
.
Let
Items

(A28)
,
(A7)
,
(A8)
,
(A25)
,
(A26)
and
(A27)
hold.
Then for any fixed
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}r^{1}&\cdots&r^{t+1}&\widetilde{r}^{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{r}^{t+1}&\beta_{*}&\Theta\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{R}}_{1}&\cdots&{\mathsf{R}}_{t+1}&\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t+1}&{\mathsf{B}}_{*}&{\mathsf{\Theta}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

(G.2a)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}p^{1}&\cdots&p^{t+1}&\widetilde{p}^{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{p}^{t+1}&z&\varepsilon&\Xi\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{P}}_{1}&\cdots&{\mathsf{P}}_{t+1}&\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t+1}&{\mathsf{Z}}&{\mathsf{E}}&{\mathsf{\Xi}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

(G.2b)

where the random variables on the right-hand sides are defined in
Equation

4.29
.
Furthermore, the covariance matrices of
({\mathsf{J}}_{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t},({\mathsf{K}}_{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t}
are invertible for any
t\geq 0
.

The full proof of
Theorem

G.1
is given in
Section

G.1
.

The next result relaxes
Items

(A25)
and
(A27)
required in
Theorem

G.1
to
Item

(A10)
, at the cost of concluding Wasserstein convergence of a lower order.

Theorem G.2
.

Consider the GVAMP iteration in
Equation

4.19
with initialization
Equation

G.1
.
Let
Items

(A28)
,
(A7)
,
(A8)
,
(A10)
and
(A9)
hold.
Then for any fixed
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}r^{1}&\cdots&r^{t+1}&\widetilde{r}^{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{r}^{t+1}&\beta_{*}&\Theta\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W_{2}}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{R}}_{1}&\cdots&{\mathsf{R}}_{t+1}&\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t+1}&{\mathsf{B}}_{*}&{\mathsf{\Theta}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

(G.3a)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}p^{1}&\cdots&p^{t+1}&\widetilde{p}^{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{p}^{t+1}&z&\varepsilon&\Xi\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W_{2}}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{P}}_{1}&\cdots&{\mathsf{P}}_{t+1}&\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t+1}&{\mathsf{Z}}&{\mathsf{E}}&{\mathsf{\Xi}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

(G.3b)

where the random variables on the right-hand sides are defined in
Equation

4.29
.

See
Section

G.2
for a proof.

To conclude
Theorem

4.3
, it remains to treat spectral initialization which is detailed in
Section

G.3
.

G.1
Proof of
Theorem

G.1

To derive the state evolution result in
Theorem

G.1
, we reparametrize the GVAMP iteration in
Equation

4.19
into a form of an abstract VAMP algorithm presented in
Equation

G.5
below.
We then prove a state evolution result for this instance of the abstract VAMP,
Theorem

G.3
, which implies
Theorem

G.1
.

Let us introduce an abstract iteration driven by
(O,Q)\sim\haar({\mathbb{O}}(n))\otimes\haar({\mathbb{O}}(d))
.
We will soon configure this iteration to implement the original GVAMP algorithm in
Equation

4.19
.
Let
r_{\mathrm{in}},r_{\mathrm{out}},s_{\mathrm{in}},s_{\mathrm{out}},k_{\mathrm{in}},k_{\mathrm{out}},\ell_{\mathrm{in}},\ell_{\mathrm{out}}
be non-negative integers that are fixed relative to
n,d
.
Let
w_{p,\mathrm{in}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times r_{\mathrm{in}}},w_{p,\mathrm{out}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times r_{\mathrm{out}}},w_{q,\mathrm{in}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times s_{\mathrm{in}}},w_{q,\mathrm{out}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times s_{\mathrm{out}}}
be side information that is independent of
O,Q
.
Suppose that we are given the following functions for each
t\geq 0
:

\displaystyle F_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}

\displaystyle\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell_{\mathrm{in}}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell_{\mathrm{out}}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{s_{\mathrm{in}}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{\mathrm{in}}},

\displaystyle F_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}

\displaystyle\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell_{\mathrm{in}}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell_{\mathrm{out}}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{s_{\mathrm{out}}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{\mathrm{out}}},

(G.4a)

\displaystyle F_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{\mathrm{in}}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{\mathrm{out}}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{r_{\mathrm{in}}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell_{\mathrm{in}}},

\displaystyle F_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{\mathrm{in}}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{\mathrm{out}}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{r_{\mathrm{out}}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell_{\mathrm{out}}}.

(G.4b)

Consider the iteration: for
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle q_{\mathrm{in}}^{t}

\displaystyle=Q^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times\ell_{\mathrm{in}}},

\displaystyle q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t}

\displaystyle=O^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times\ell_{\mathrm{out}}},

(G.5a)

\displaystyle f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}

\displaystyle=F_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}(q_{\mathrm{in}}^{t},q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t},w_{q,\mathrm{in}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times k_{\mathrm{in}}},

\displaystyle f_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}

\displaystyle=F_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}(q_{\mathrm{in}}^{t},q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t},w_{q,\mathrm{out}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times k_{\mathrm{out}}},

(G.5b)

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=Qf_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times k_{\mathrm{in}}},

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=Of_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times k_{\mathrm{out}}},

(G.5c)

\displaystyle f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=F_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}(p_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},p_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},w_{p,\mathrm{in}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times\ell_{\mathrm{in}}},

\displaystyle f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=F_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}(p_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},p_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},w_{p,\mathrm{out}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times\ell_{\mathrm{out}}},

(G.5d)

initialized with
f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times\ell_{\mathrm{in}}},f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times\ell_{\mathrm{out}}}
independent of
O,Q
.

The functions in
Equation

G.4
are understood to apply to their matrix inputs row-wise in the updates
Equations

G.5b
and
G.5d
.
Note that these functions may take input matrices with a varying number of rows.
If the number of rows of the output matrix is larger than that of some of the input matrices, the function is understood to apply to the shorter input matrices appended with additional zero rows.
Otherwise, the taller input matrices are truncated before applying the function row-wise.
These operations ensure that in both cases the column dimension of all input matrices equals that of the output matrix.
For instance, consider the first equation in
Equation

G.5b
.
For any
i\in[d]
, according to the above convention, we have that if
d>n
,

\displaystyle f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}F_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}((q_{\mathrm{in}}^{t})_{1,:},(q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t})_{1,:},(w_{q,\mathrm{in}})_{1,:})^{\top}\\
\vdots\\
F_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}((q_{\mathrm{in}}^{t})_{n,:},(q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t})_{n,:},(w_{q,\mathrm{in}})_{n,:})^{\top}\\
F_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}((q_{\mathrm{in}}^{t})_{n+1,:},0_{\ell_{\mathrm{out}}\times 1},(w_{q,\mathrm{in}})_{n+1,:})^{\top}\\
\vdots\\
F_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}((q_{\mathrm{in}}^{t})_{d,:},0_{\ell_{\mathrm{out}}\times 1},(w_{q,\mathrm{in}})_{d,:})^{\top}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times k_{\mathrm{in}}},

and otherwise if
d\leq n
,

\displaystyle f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}F_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}((q_{\mathrm{in}}^{t})_{1,:},(q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t})_{1,:},(w_{q,\mathrm{in}})_{1,:})^{\top}\\
\vdots\\
F_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}((q_{\mathrm{in}}^{t})_{d,:},(q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t})_{d,:},(w_{q,\mathrm{in}})_{d,:})^{\top}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times k_{\mathrm{in}}}.

We now design the functions
F_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t},F_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t},F_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},F_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
suitably so that the iteration
Equation

G.5
implements (a slight generalization of)
Equation

4.19
.
To this end, let us first fix any
\Gamma\geq 0
as an upper bound on the time index.
Recall from
Item

(A2)
that
O\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n},Q\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
are independent Haar matrices collecting the left, right singular vectors of
X
and
\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\wedge d}
is an entry-wise non-negative vector collecting the singular values of
X
.
We define the vectors
\lambda_{n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}_{\geq 0}
and

\lambda_{d}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\geq 0}
as:

\displaystyle\lambda_{n}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\lambda,&n\leq d\\
\begin{bmatrix}\lambda^{\top}&0_{n-d}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}^{\top},&n>d\end{cases},

\displaystyle\lambda_{d}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\begin{bmatrix}\lambda^{\top}&0_{d-n}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}^{\top},&n<d\\
\lambda,&n\geq d\end{cases}.

(G.6)

These vectors have Wasserstein limits
\lambda_{n}\xrightarrow{W}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n},\lambda_{d}\xrightarrow{W}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}
where the limiting random variables are defined in
Equation

3.8
. Consider
4
sequences (indexed by
(n,d)
) of vectors
(\phi_{t})_{0\leq t\leq\Gamma}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{d},(\widetilde{\phi}_{t})_{0\leq t\leq\Gamma}\subset{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}^{n\wedge d},(\psi_{t})_{0\leq t\leq\Gamma}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n},(\widetilde{\psi}_{t})_{0\leq t\leq\Gamma}\subset{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}^{n\wedge d}
, with
\widetilde{\phi}_{t,d},\widetilde{\psi}_{t,n}
denoting vectors of length
d,n
, respectively, obtained by truncating or expanding
\widetilde{\phi}_{t}
and
\widetilde{\psi}_{t}
.
These sequences of vectors are subject to the following assumption.

(A29)

(\phi_{t})_{t\geq 0},(\widetilde{\phi}_{t})_{t\geq 0},(\psi_{t})_{t\geq 0},(\widetilde{\psi}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
are independent of
O,Q,\varepsilon,\beta_{*},\Theta,\Xi
.
For any fixed
\Gamma\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{d}&\phi_{0}&\cdots&\phi_{\Gamma}&\widetilde{\phi}_{0,d}&\cdots&\widetilde{\phi}_{\Gamma,d}\end{bmatrix}&\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}&{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{\Gamma}&\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{\Gamma}\end{bmatrix},\\
\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{n}&\psi_{0}&\cdots&\psi_{\Gamma}&\widetilde{\psi}_{0,n}&\cdots&\widetilde{\psi}_{\Gamma,n}\end{bmatrix}&\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}&{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\Gamma}&\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{\Gamma}\end{bmatrix}.\end{split}

(G.7)

Furthermore, for each
t\geq 0
, the following conditions hold:
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{t}\right]=0
,
\max\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t}^{2}\right],\mathrm{Var}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t}\right]\right\}>0
,
\max\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{t}^{2}\right],\mathrm{Var}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{t}\right]\right\}>0
.

The above sequences of vectors along with the pair of independent Haar matrices
(O,Q)
can be used to define an iteration that slightly generalizes the GVAMP iteration in
Equation

4.19
:

\displaystyle r^{t+1}

\displaystyle=Q\diag(\phi_{t})Q^{\top}\widetilde{r}^{t}+Q\diag_{d\times n}(\widetilde{\phi}_{t})O^{\top}\widetilde{p}^{t},

\displaystyle\widetilde{r}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=f_{t+1}(r^{t+1};\beta_{*},\Theta),

(G.8a)

\displaystyle p^{t+1}

\displaystyle=O\diag(\psi_{t})O^{\top}\widetilde{p}^{t}+O\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\psi}_{t})Q^{\top}\widetilde{r}^{t},

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=g_{t+1}(p^{t+1};z,\varepsilon,\Xi),

(G.8b)

initialized with
\widetilde{r}^{0},\widetilde{p}^{0}
from
Item

(A28)
.
The above iteration is driven by
4
sequences (indexed by
t\geq 0
) of random matrices
Q\diag(\phi_{t})Q^{\top}
,
O\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\phi}_{t})Q^{\top}
,
O\diag(\psi_{t})O^{\top}
,
O\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\psi}_{t})Q^{\top}
.
These matrices share common eigen-/singular spaces but
Item

(A29)
allows them to have arbitrarily correlated spectra across time.
Clearly, the matrices
\Phi_{t}(X^{\top}X),\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}(X),\Psi_{t}(XX^{\top}),\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(X)
in
Equation

4.19
are a special case, hence the GVAMP iteration in
Equation

4.19
is a special case of
Equation

G.8
. Indeed, if we set

\displaystyle\phi_{t}

\displaystyle=\Phi_{t}(\lambda_{d}\circ\lambda_{d}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{t}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}(\lambda),

\displaystyle\psi_{t}

\displaystyle=\Psi_{t}(\lambda_{n}\circ\lambda_{n}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}_{t}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(\lambda),

(G.9)

where all functions are applied component-wise,
Equation

G.8
reduces to
Equation

4.19
.
The additional flexibility offered by
Item

(A29)
will be useful for technical purposes in
Theorem

G.2
.

Before diving into further specifications of
Equation

G.5
, we condition on a sequence (indexed by
(n,d)
) of
\lambda
,
(\phi_{t})_{0\leq t\leq\Gamma}
,
(\widetilde{\phi}_{t})_{0\leq t\leq\Gamma}
,
(\psi_{t})_{0\leq t\leq\Gamma}
,
(\widetilde{\psi}_{t})_{0\leq t\leq\Gamma}
satisfying
Items

(A2)
and
(A29)
throughout the rest of the this section.

Theorem

G.3
below is a state evolution result for
\Gamma
steps of
Equation

G.5
conditioned on the above vectors. Choosing these vectors according to
Equation

G.9
gives
Theorem

G.1
.

Back to the abstract VAMP iteration
Equation

G.5
, we fix the dimensions to be:

\displaystyle k_{\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle k_{\mathrm{out}}

\displaystyle=2,

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=2,

\displaystyle\ell_{\mathrm{out}}

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle r_{\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=1+d^{\prime},

\displaystyle r_{\mathrm{out}}

\displaystyle=1+n^{\prime},

\displaystyle s_{\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=1+2(\Gamma+1),

\displaystyle s_{\mathrm{out}}

\displaystyle=1+2(\Gamma+1).

Let the side information be given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}w_{q,\mathrm{in}}&=\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{d}&\phi_{0}&\cdots&\phi_{\Gamma}&\widetilde{\phi}_{0,d}&\cdots&\widetilde{\phi}_{\Gamma,d}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times(2(\Gamma+1)+1)},\\
w_{q,\mathrm{out}}&=\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{n}&\psi_{0}&\cdots&\psi_{\Gamma}&\widetilde{\psi}_{0,n}&\cdots&\widetilde{\psi}_{\Gamma,n}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times(2(\Gamma+1)+1)},\\
w_{p,\mathrm{in}}&=\begin{bmatrix}\beta_{*}&\Theta\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times(1+d^{\prime})},\qquad w_{p,\mathrm{out}}=\begin{bmatrix}\varepsilon&\Xi\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times(1+n^{\prime})}.\end{split}

(G.10)

We will use
w_{q,\mathrm{in},\lambda},w_{q,\mathrm{in},t},w_{q,\mathrm{in},\widetilde{t}},w_{q,\mathrm{out},\lambda},w_{q,\mathrm{out},t},w_{q,\mathrm{out},\widetilde{t}}
to respectively refer to
\lambda_{d},\phi_{t},\widetilde{\phi}_{t,d},\lambda_{n},\widetilde{\psi}_{t},\widetilde{\psi}_{t,n}
, for any
0\leq t\leq\Gamma
.
We will also use the notation
w_{p,\mathrm{in},1},w_{p,\mathrm{in},-}
to respectively refer to
\beta_{*},\Theta
, and
w_{p,\mathrm{out},1},w_{p,\mathrm{out},-}
to respectively refer to
\varepsilon,\Xi
.
The iterates are specialized as follows.
For
t=0
, let

\displaystyle f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{r}^{0}-\widetilde{b}_{0}\beta_{*}&\beta_{*}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times 2},

\displaystyle f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{p}^{0}-\widetilde{a}_{0}z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},

(G.11)

where
\widetilde{r}^{0},\widetilde{p}^{0}
are the initializers of
Equation

4.19
given in
Equation

G.1
and
\widetilde{b}_{0},\widetilde{a}_{0}
are defined in
Equation

4.29e
.
For
t\geq 0
, we define the functions in
Equation

G.4
as follows. For inputs

q_{\mathrm{in}}=\begin{bmatrix}q_{\mathrm{in},1}&q_{\mathrm{in},2}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
,

q_{\mathrm{out}}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

p_{\mathrm{in}}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

p_{\mathrm{out}}=\begin{bmatrix}p_{\mathrm{out},1}&p_{\mathrm{out},2}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
,

w_{q,\mathrm{in}}=\begin{bmatrix}w_{q,\mathrm{in},\lambda}&w_{q,\mathrm{in},0}&\cdots&w_{q,\mathrm{in},\Gamma}&\cdots&w_{q,\mathrm{in},\widetilde{0}}&\cdots&w_{q,\mathrm{in},\widetilde{\Gamma}}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2(\Gamma+1)+1}
,

w_{q,\mathrm{out}}=\begin{bmatrix}w_{q,\mathrm{out},\lambda}&w_{q,\mathrm{out},0}&\cdots&w_{q,\mathrm{out},\Gamma}&\cdots&w_{q,\mathrm{out},\widetilde{0}}&\cdots&w_{q,\mathrm{out},\widetilde{\Gamma}}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2(\Gamma+1)+1}
,
the outputs of the functions are given by

\displaystyle F_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}(q_{\mathrm{in}},q_{\mathrm{out}},w_{q,\mathrm{in}})

\displaystyle=w_{q,\mathrm{in},t}(q_{\mathrm{in},1}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t}q_{\mathrm{in},2})+w_{q,\mathrm{in},\widetilde{t}}(q_{\mathrm{out}}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t}w_{q,\mathrm{in},\lambda}q_{\mathrm{in},2})-{\mathfrak{b}}_{t+1}q_{\mathrm{in},2}\in{\mathbb{R}},

(G.12a)

\displaystyle F_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}(q_{\mathrm{in}},q_{\mathrm{out}},w_{q,\mathrm{out}})

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}w_{q,\mathrm{out},t}(q_{\mathrm{out}}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t}w_{q,\mathrm{out},\lambda}q_{\mathrm{in},2})+w_{q,\mathrm{out},\widetilde{t}}(q_{\mathrm{in},1}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t}q_{\mathrm{in},2})-{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+1}w_{q,\mathrm{out},\lambda}q_{\mathrm{in},2}&w_{q,\mathrm{out},\lambda}q_{\mathrm{in},2}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},

(G.12b)

\displaystyle F_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}(p_{\mathrm{in}},p_{\mathrm{out}},w_{p,\mathrm{in}})

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}f_{t+1}(p_{\mathrm{in}}+{\mathfrak{b}}_{t+1}w_{p,\mathrm{in},1};w_{p,\mathrm{in}})-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1}w_{p,\mathrm{in},1}&w_{p,\mathrm{in},1}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},

(G.12c)

\displaystyle F_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}(p_{\mathrm{in}},p_{\mathrm{out}},w_{p,\mathrm{out}})

\displaystyle=g_{t+1}(p_{\mathrm{out},1}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+1}p_{\mathrm{out},2};p_{\mathrm{out},2},w_{p,\mathrm{out}})-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1}p_{\mathrm{out},2}\in{\mathbb{R}},

(G.12d)

where the coefficients
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t},\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t},{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+1},{\mathfrak{b}}_{t+1}
are defined in
Equations

G.14q
and
G.14k
below.
These coefficients are designed in such a way that for every
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t},z\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t},\beta_{*}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle p_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},z\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle p_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},\beta_{*}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0,

which will be formally justified in the proof of
Theorem

G.3
.

We now clarify the connection between the GVAMP iteration in
Equation

4.19
and the abstract VAMP iteration in
Equation

G.5
with initialization
Equation

G.11
, side information
Equation

G.10
and denoising functions in
Equation

G.4
specialized to
Equation

G.12
.
As shall be seen from the proof of
Theorem

G.1
below (in particular
Item

1
therein),
under the specification in
Equation

G.9
,
it holds that
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t}=\widetilde{a}_{t},\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t}=\widetilde{b}_{t},{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+1}=a_{t+1},{\mathfrak{b}}_{t+1}=b_{t+1}
for all
t\geq 0
, where the quantities on the right are defined in
Equations

4.29f
and
4.29e
.
Given this, by induction on
t
, one can easily verify that
Equation

G.5
reduces to the following iteration:

\displaystyle\begin{split}q_{\mathrm{in}}^{t}&=\begin{bmatrix}Q^{\top}(\widetilde{r}^{t}-\widetilde{b}_{t}\beta_{*})&Q^{\top}\beta_{*}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times 2},\\
q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t}&=O^{\top}(\widetilde{p}^{t}-\widetilde{a}_{t}z)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\\
f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}&=\Phi_{t}(\Lambda^{\top}\Lambda)Q^{\top}\widetilde{r}^{t}+\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}(\Lambda)^{\top}O^{\top}\widetilde{p}^{t}-b_{t+1}Q^{\top}\beta_{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\\
f_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}&=\begin{bmatrix}\Psi_{t}(\Lambda\Lambda^{\top})O^{\top}\widetilde{p}^{t}+\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(\Lambda)Q^{\top}\widetilde{r}^{t}-a_{t+1}\Lambda Q^{\top}\beta_{*}&\Lambda Q^{\top}\beta_{*}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times 2},\\
p_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&=r^{t+1}-b_{t+1}\beta_{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\\
p_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&=\begin{bmatrix}p^{t+1}-a_{t+1}z&z\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times 2},\\
f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&=\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{r}^{t+1}-\widetilde{b}_{t+1}\beta_{*}&\beta_{*}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times 2},\\
f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&=\widetilde{p}^{t+1}-\widetilde{a}_{t+1}z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\end{split}

(G.13)

from which the equivalence between
Equations

G.5
and
4.19
immediately follows.

Let us denote by
q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t},f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t},p_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1},f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}
the first columns of
q_{\mathrm{in}}^{t},f_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t},p_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
, and by
q_{\mathrm{in},2},f_{q,\mathrm{out},2},p_{\mathrm{out},2},f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}
their second columns (since the latter terms are time invariant).

At a high level, the state evolution result in
Theorem

G.3
shows that the joint empirical distribution of the iterates converges to a multivariate Gaussian whose covariance matrix can be tracked recursively over time.
For instance, the empirical distribution of the rows of
\begin{bmatrix}q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&\cdots&q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t}\end{bmatrix}
converges in Wasserstein to
{\mathcal{N}}(0_{t+1},\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})
.
Similar results hold for
(q_{\mathrm{out}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t},(p_{\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{1\leq s\leq t+1},(p_{\mathrm{out},1}^{s})_{1\leq s\leq t+1}
with corresponding covariance matrices
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t},\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
.
We now define these covariance matrices along with other state evolution parameters associated with the abstract VAMP iteration
Equation

G.5
.
For
t=0
, they are identified with the initial conditions:

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&w_{q,\mathrm{in},\lambda}&w_{q,\mathrm{in},0}&\cdots&w_{q,\mathrm{in},\Gamma}&w_{q,\mathrm{in},\widetilde{0}}&\cdots&w_{q,\mathrm{in},\widetilde{\Gamma}}&w_{p,\mathrm{in},1}&w_{p,\mathrm{in},-}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad=\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{r}^{0}-\widetilde{b}_{0}\beta_{*}&\lambda_{d}&\phi_{0}&\cdots&\phi_{\Gamma}&\widetilde{\phi}_{0,d}&\cdots&\widetilde{\phi}_{\Gamma,d}&\beta_{*}&\Theta\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}-\widetilde{b}_{0}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}&{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}&{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{\Gamma}&\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{\Gamma}&{\mathsf{B}}_{*}&{\mathsf{\Theta}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\eqqcolon\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in},\lambda}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in},0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in},\Gamma}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in},\widetilde{0}}&\cdots&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in},\widetilde{\Gamma}}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in},-}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

(G.14a)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}&w_{q,\mathrm{out},\lambda}&w_{q,\mathrm{out},0}&\cdots&w_{q,\mathrm{out},\Gamma}&w_{q,\mathrm{out},\widetilde{0}},&\cdots&w_{q,\mathrm{out},\widetilde{\Gamma}}&w_{p,\mathrm{out},1}&w_{p,\mathrm{out},-}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad=\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{p}^{0}-\widetilde{a}_{0}z&\lambda_{n}&\psi_{0}&\cdots&\psi_{\Gamma}&\widetilde{\psi}_{0,n}&\cdots&\widetilde{\psi}_{\Gamma,n}&\varepsilon&\Xi\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}-\widetilde{a}_{0}{\mathsf{Z}}&{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}&{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\Gamma}&\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{\Gamma}&{\mathsf{E}}&{\mathsf{\Xi}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\eqqcolon\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out},\lambda}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out},0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out},\Gamma}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out},\widetilde{0}},&\cdots&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out},\widetilde{\Gamma}}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out},1}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out},-}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

(G.14b)

where the existence of the limiting random variables on the right is guaranteed by
Items

(A28)
and
(A29)
, and the constants
\widetilde{a}_{0},\widetilde{b}_{0}
are defined as per
Equation

4.29e
.
We will also use the notation

{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in},-}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}
,

{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out},1}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out},-}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}
,

{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in},\lambda}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in},0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in},\Gamma}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in},\widetilde{0}}&\cdots&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in},\widetilde{\Gamma}}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}
,
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out},\lambda}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out},0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out},\Gamma}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out},\widetilde{0}},&\cdots&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out},\widetilde{\Gamma}}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}
.
For notational convenience, let the random vector
{\mathsf{A}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{4(\Gamma+1)+3+d^{\prime}+n^{\prime}}
collect:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{\Gamma}&\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{\Gamma}&{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\Gamma}&\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{\Gamma}&{\mathsf{\Lambda}}&{\mathsf{B}}_{*}&{\mathsf{E}}&{\mathsf{\Theta}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{\Xi}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}.

Using these random variables, we define

\displaystyle\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}-\widetilde{b}_{0}{\mathsf{B}}_{*})^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0})^{2}\right]\in{\mathbb{R}},

\displaystyle\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}-\widetilde{a}_{0}{\mathsf{Z}})^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0})^{2}\right]\in{\mathbb{R}}.

(G.14c)

Note that

\displaystyle\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}

\displaystyle>0,

\displaystyle\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{0}

\displaystyle>0,

(G.14d)

since by the definitions of
\widetilde{b}_{0}
in
Equation

4.29e
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}-\widetilde{b}_{0}{\mathsf{B}}_{*})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}^{2}\right]-2\widetilde{b}_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right]+\widetilde{b}_{0}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}^{2}\right]-\widetilde{b}_{0}^{2}\rho,

and similarly,
\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}-\widetilde{a}_{0}{\mathsf{Z}})^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}^{2}\right]-\widetilde{a}_{0}\sigma^{2}
.
By
Item

(A28)
and the equality condition of Cauchy–Schwarz, the random variables
\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0},\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}
satisfy the following inequalities strictly:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle<\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}^{2}\right]=\rho\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}^{2}\right],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}{\mathsf{Z}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle<\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}^{2}\right]=\sigma^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}^{2}\right],

implying the claimed positivity.

Now, we recursively define the state evolution parameters
{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+1},{\mathfrak{b}}_{t+1}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(t+1)\times(t+1)}
,
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1},\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(t+2)\times(t+2)}
.
Let

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{a}_{0},

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{b}_{0}.

(G.14e)

For
t\geq 0
, given
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t},\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}
, let

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}

\displaystyle\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(0,\rho\right)\otimes{\mathcal{N}}(0_{t+1},\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}),

(G.14f)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}

\displaystyle\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{t+1},\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t})

(G.14g)

be two mutually independent Gaussian vectors that are also independent of
{\mathsf{A}}
,
({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t-1}
,
({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t-1}
.
For every
0\leq s\leq t
, denote
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s}=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}
.
Let

\displaystyle{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}

\displaystyle=F_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t},{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t},{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})

\displaystyle={\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})+\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})-{\mathfrak{b}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2},

(G.14h)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t}

\displaystyle=F_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t},{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t},{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})

\displaystyle={\mathsf{\Psi}}_{t}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})+\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{t}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})-{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2},

(G.14i)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}

\displaystyle=F_{q,\mathrm{out},2}^{t}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t},{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t},{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})={\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2},

(G.14j)

where the constants
({\mathfrak{a}}_{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t},({\mathfrak{b}}_{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t}
are defined as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{a}}_{r+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{r}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{r}+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{r}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right]\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{r}\right),

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{b}}_{r+1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{r}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{r}.

(G.14k)

Let
{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}
.
Using these random variables, define covariance matrices

\displaystyle\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0:t}({\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0:t})^{\top}\right]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(t+1)\times(t+1)},

\displaystyle\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{0:t}({\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{0:t})^{\top}\right]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(t+1)\times(t+1)}.

(G.14l)

Next, define two mutually independent Gaussian vectors with the above covariances:

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{1}&\cdots,{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}

\displaystyle\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(0_{t+1},\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\right),

(G.14m)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Z}}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{1}&\cdots&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}

\displaystyle\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(0,\sigma^{2}\right)\otimes{\mathcal{N}}\left(0_{t+1},\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\right),

(G.14n)

that are independent of
({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t},({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t},{\mathsf{A}}
.
We will also use the notation
{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}={\mathsf{Z}}
and
{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s+1}=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{s+1}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}
, for every
0\leq s\leq t
.
Let

\displaystyle{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=F_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})=f_{t+1}({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}+{\mathfrak{b}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{B}}_{*};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{B}}_{*},

(G.14o)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=F_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},{\mathsf{E}},{\mathsf{\Xi}})=g_{t+1}({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1,}^{t+1}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2};{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2},{\mathsf{E}},{\mathsf{\Xi}})-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2},

(G.14p)

where the constants
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1},\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1}
are defined as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1}&=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[g_{t+1}({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2};{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2},{\mathsf{E}},{\mathsf{\Xi}}){\mathsf{Z}}\right],\\
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1}&=\frac{1}{\rho}\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t+1}({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}+{\mathfrak{b}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{B}}_{*};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}}){\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right].\end{split}

(G.14q)

Let also
{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}={\mathsf{B}}_{*}
and
{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}
.
Use these random variables to define covariance matrices:

\displaystyle\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0:t+1}({\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0:t+1})^{\top}\right]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(t+2)\times(t+2)},

\displaystyle\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0:t+1}({\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0:t+1})^{\top}\right]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(t+2)\times(t+2)}.

(G.14r)

Note that by the definitions in
Equations

G.14l
and
G.14r
, for every
t\geq 0
,
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t},\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t},\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
are the leading principal minors (of order
t+1
) of
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+2},\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}
, respectively.
As a notational convention, we index elements in
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t},\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}
with
(i,j)\in\left\{0,1,\cdots,t\right\}^{2}
and index elements in
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
with
(i,j)\in\left\{1,2,\cdots,t+1\right\}^{2}
, so that we conveniently have, e.g.,
(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})_{r,s}=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s}\right]
and
(\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1})_{r+1,s+1}=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{r+1}{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1}\right]
for any
0\leq r,s\leq t
.

To summarize, for any
t\geq 0
,
Equation

G.14
defines random variables
{\mathsf{A}}
,
({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
({\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
({\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
({\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t+1}
,
({\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t+1}
.
The main technical result of this section,
Theorem

G.3
below, asserts that these random variables identify the Wasserstein limits of the iterates in
Equation

G.5
.

(A30)

For every
t\geq 0
, there do not exist constants
\beta_{0},\cdots,\beta_{t},\alpha_{0},\cdots,\alpha_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that

either

\displaystyle{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\beta_{s}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{s}

or

\displaystyle{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\alpha_{s}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{s}

holds almost surely.

For any
t\geq 0
, let
{\bm{q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t}\coloneqq\begin{bmatrix}q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&\cdots&q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times(t+1)}
,
\bm{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t}\coloneqq\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1\times(t+1)}
and other notation including
{\bm{f}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}
,
{\bm{p}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
,
{\bm{f}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}
,
{\bm{q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t}
,
{\bm{f}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t}
,
{\bm{p}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}
,
{\bm{f}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
,
\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}
,
\bm{{\mathsf{P}}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
,
\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}
,
\bm{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t}
,
\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t}
,
\bm{{\mathsf{P}}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}
,
\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
is similarly defined.

Theorem G.3

(State evolution of abstract VAMP)
.

Consider the abstract VAMP iteration
Equation

G.5
with initialization
Equation

G.11
, side information
Equation

G.10
and denoising functions in
Equation

G.4
specialized to
Equation

G.12
.
Let
Items

(A28)
,
(A29)
,
(A27)
,
(A8)
and
(A30)
hold.
Then for any fixed
t\geq 0
,
