\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}q_{\mathrm{in},2}&{\bm{q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t}&{\bm{f}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}&{\bm{p}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&{\bm{f}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&w_{p,\mathrm{in}}&w_{q,\mathrm{in}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}&\bm{{\mathsf{P}}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

(G.15)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t}&f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&{\bm{f}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t}&p_{\mathrm{out},2}&{\bm{p}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}&{\bm{f}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&w_{p,\mathrm{out}}&w_{q,\mathrm{out}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}\bm{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{P}}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}.

(G.16)

Since the abstract VAMP in
Equation

G.5
is configured to simulate the GVAMP dynamics in
Equation

4.19
(see
Equation

G.13
), the state evolution result in
Theorem

G.3
for the former iteration can be translated to that for the latter, as stated in
Theorem

G.1
.

Proof of
Theorem

G.1
.

We specialize the vectors
\phi_{t},\widetilde{\phi}_{t},\psi_{t},\widetilde{\psi}_{t}
(for every
t\geq 0
) according to
Equation

G.9
.
Due to the regularity of the functions
\Phi_{t},\widetilde{\Phi}_{t},\Psi_{t},\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}
guaranteed by
Item

(A25)
,
Proposition

K.2
implies the Wasserstein convergence required in
Item

(A29)
.
In particular, we can identify for every
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\Phi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\Psi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}).

The trace-free property of
\Phi_{t},\Psi_{t}
in
Item

(A7)
implies the zero mean condition of
{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t},{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{t}
required in
Item

(A29)
.
Therefore,
Theorem

G.3
is applicable to the present setting.

By induction on
t\geq 0
, we will prove the following statements which allow us to translate the state evolution result in
Theorem

G.3
to
Theorem

G.1
.

1.

\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t}=\widetilde{a}_{t},\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t}=\widetilde{b}_{t},{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+1}=a_{t+1},{\mathfrak{b}}_{t+1}=b_{t+1}
.

2.

The joint law of

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{R}}_{1}&\cdots&{\mathsf{R}}_{t+1}&\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t+1}&{\mathsf{B}}_{*}&{\mathsf{\Theta}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}

(G.17)

is equal to that of

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\mathfrak{b}}_{1}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}+{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{1}&\cdots&{\mathfrak{b}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}+{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&\cdots&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in},-}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}.

(G.18)

The joint law of

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{P}}_{1}&\cdots&{\mathsf{P}}_{t+1}&\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t+1}&{\mathsf{Z}}&{\mathsf{E}}&{\mathsf{\Xi}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}

(G.19)

is equal to that of

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}+{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{1}&\cdots&{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}+{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}&\cdots&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out},1}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out},-}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}.

(G.20)

Recall
{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}={\mathsf{Z}},{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}={\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}={\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out},1}={\mathsf{E}},{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in},-}={\mathsf{\Theta}},{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out},-}={\mathsf{\Xi}}
.
We identify the random variables
({\mathsf{K}}_{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t},({\mathsf{J}}_{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t}
with the following change of variables:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{K}}_{s+1}

\displaystyle={\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{J}}_{s+1}

\displaystyle={\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{s+1}.

(G.21)

Using this and comparing the definitions
Equation

4.29g
with
Equations

G.14o
and
G.14p
, we further identify
(\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{s})_{0\leq s\leq t+1},(\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{s})_{0\leq s\leq t+1}
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{s}

\displaystyle={\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{s}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{s}{\mathsf{B}}_{*},

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{s}

\displaystyle={\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{s}{\mathsf{Z}}.

(G.22)

To conclude the convergence result in
Equation

G.2
, it remains to verify that the joint distribution of the random variables on the left-hand sides above is consistent with the descriptions in
Equation

4.29
.

Base case.

For
t=0
, we have
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}=\widetilde{a}_{0},\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}=\widetilde{b}_{0}
by definition
Equation

G.14e
.
Comparing
Equations

4.29f
and
G.14k
then gives
{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}=a_{1},{\mathfrak{b}}_{1}={\mathfrak{b}}_{1}
.
Therefore
Item

1
holds at
t=0
.
Given
Item

1
together with
Equations

G.14a
and
G.14b
, it remains to verify
{\mathsf{R}}_{1}=b_{1}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}+{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{1},{\mathsf{P}}_{1}=a_{1}{\mathsf{Z}}+{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{1}
which, upon recalling
Equations

G.14o
,
G.14p
and
G.22
and comparing them with
Equation

4.29g
, then implies
Item

2
for
t=0
.
To this end, we note from
Equations

G.14m
and
G.14n
that
{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{1},{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{1}
are centered Gaussians independent of
{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{Z}}
.
So we only need to check that their covariances
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{1},\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{1}
equal
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{1}^{2}\right],\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{1}^{2}\right]
given in
Equations

4.29d
and
4.29c
, respectively.
We evaluate the former quantities according the their definitions in
Equation

G.14l
.
For
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{1}=\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0})^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\Phi_{0}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})+\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})-{\mathfrak{b}}_{1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{0}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}\right]\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}\Phi_{0}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}-{\mathfrak{b}}_{1}\right)^{2}\right]\rho+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}\right]\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{0}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}^{2}\right]-b_{1}^{2}\rho+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}\right]\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}^{2}\right]-\widetilde{a}_{0}^{2}\sigma^{2}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]\widetilde{a}_{0}^{2}\rho+2\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{0}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\widetilde{a}_{0}\widetilde{b}_{0}\rho.

The penultimate line is taken from
Equation

G.47
in the proof of
Theorem

G.3
.
The last line is obtained by using
Item

1
for
t=0
just shown, plugging in the definitions
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}=\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}^{2}\right]-\widetilde{a}_{0}^{2}\rho
and
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{0}=\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}^{2}\right]-\widetilde{b}_{0}\sigma^{2}
from
Equation

G.14c
, and simplifying.
The result coincides with
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{1}^{2}\right]
in
Equation

4.29d
, as desired.
For
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{1}
, similar calculations starting from
Equation

G.48
lead to the claimed result
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{1}=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{1}^{2}\right]
.
This proves
Item

2
for
t=0
.

Induction step.

Assuming the validity of
Items

1
and
2
for time up to
t-1
, we verify their validity for time
t
.
Using the induction hypothesis in
Item

2
and comparing the definitions
Equations

4.29e
and
G.14q
, we immediately get
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t}=\widetilde{a}_{t},\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t}=\widetilde{b}_{t}
.
In view of
Equations

4.29f
and
G.14k
, this in turn implies
{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+1}=a_{t+1},{\mathfrak{b}}_{t+1}=b_{b+1}
, thereby justifying
Item

1
for time
t
.
In the sequel, we will therefore replace all occurrences of
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{s},\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{s},{\mathfrak{a}}_{s+1},{\mathfrak{b}}_{s+1}
with
\widetilde{a}_{s},\widetilde{b}_{s},a_{s+1},b_{s+1}
, for any
0\leq s\leq t
.

According to the identification
Equation

G.21
and the definition
Equations

G.14m
and
G.14n
of the right-hand side thereof, we have that
({\mathsf{K}}_{s})_{1\leq s\leq t+1}
and
({\mathsf{J}}_{s})_{1\leq s\leq t+1}
are two independent centered Gaussian processes that are also independent of
{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n},{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d},{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{Z}},{\mathsf{E}},{\mathsf{\Theta}},{\mathsf{\Xi}}
.
So comparing the description
Equation

4.29
of the laws of
Equations

G.17
and
G.19
with the description
Equation

G.14
of the laws of
Equations

G.18
and
G.20
, we see that to prove
Item

2
for time
t
, we only need to examine the covariance structures of the Gaussian processes
({\mathsf{K}}_{s})_{1\leq s\leq t+1}
and
({\mathsf{J}}_{s})_{1\leq s\leq t+1}
.
The covariance of
({\mathsf{J}}_{s})_{1\leq s\leq t+1}
is given as follows.
For any
0\leq r,s\leq t
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{r+1}{\mathsf{J}}_{s+1}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{r+1}{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{s+1}\right]=(\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1})_{r+1,s+1}=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{r}{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{s}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\Big[\Big(\Psi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}+\widetilde{a}_{r}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})+\widetilde{\Psi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}+\widetilde{b}_{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})-a_{r+1}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Big(\Psi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{a}_{s}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})+\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}+\widetilde{b}_{s}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})-a_{s+1}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\Psi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}+\widetilde{a}_{r}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{a}_{s}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]

(G.23)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})\right]\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}+\widetilde{b}_{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}+\widetilde{b}_{s}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]+a_{r+1}a_{s+1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}^{2}\right]

(G.24)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}+\widetilde{a}_{r}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}+\widetilde{b}_{s}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]

(G.25)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\widetilde{\Psi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{a}_{s}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}+\widetilde{b}_{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]

(G.26)

\displaystyle\quad-a_{r+1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{a}_{s}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}){\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}+\widetilde{b}_{s}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}){\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\right]\right)

(G.27)

\displaystyle\quad-a_{s+1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}+\widetilde{a}_{r}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}){\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}+\widetilde{b}_{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}){\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\right]\right),

(G.28)

where the second line in the above display follows from the definition
Equation

G.14i
of
{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t}
.
We will separately examine the terms in
Equations

G.23
,
G.24
,
G.25
,
G.26
,
G.27
and
G.28
.

We first note several facts that will be used in the calculations:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}\right]

\displaystyle=(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t})_{r,s}=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{r}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{s}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{r}-\widetilde{a}_{r}{\mathsf{Z}}\right)\left(\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{s}-\widetilde{a}_{s}{\mathsf{Z}}\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{r}\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{s}\right]-\widetilde{a}_{r}\widetilde{a}_{s}\sigma^{2},

(G.29)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}\right]

\displaystyle=(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})_{r,s}=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{r}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{s}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{r}-\widetilde{b}_{r}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right)\left(\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{s}-\widetilde{b}_{s}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{r}\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{s}\right]-\widetilde{b}_{r}\widetilde{b}_{s}\rho,

(G.30)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\overline{m}_{2}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}^{2}\right]=\overline{\kappa}_{2}\rho=\sigma^{2}.

(G.31)

In the above, we have used the
the relation
Equation

G.22
and
the definitions of
\widetilde{a}_{t},\widetilde{b}_{t}
in
Equation

4.29e
.

Now we simplify
Equation

G.23
to:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\Psi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}+\widetilde{a}_{r}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{a}_{s}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\Psi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}\right]+\widetilde{a}_{r}\widetilde{a}_{s}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\Psi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\Psi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\right]\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{r}\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{s}\right]-\widetilde{a}_{r}\widetilde{a}_{s}\sigma^{2}\right)+\widetilde{a}_{r}\widetilde{a}_{s}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\Psi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\rho,

(G.32)

where we have used the independence between
({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
and
({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
, and the fact
Equation

G.29
.

The second expectation in
Equation

G.24
can be simplified as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}+\widetilde{b}_{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}+\widetilde{b}_{s}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}\right]+\widetilde{b}_{r}\widetilde{b}_{s}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{r}\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{s}\right],

(G.33)

where we have used the independence between

and

(see

), and the fact
Equation

G.30
.

We then move to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}+\widetilde{a}_{r}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}+\widetilde{b}_{s}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{a}_{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}+\widetilde{b}_{s}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]=\widetilde{a}_{r}\widetilde{b}_{s}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right]\rho.

(G.34)

Similarly,
Equation

G.26
evaluates to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\widetilde{\Psi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{a}_{s}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}+\widetilde{b}_{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{a}_{s}\widetilde{b}_{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\widetilde{\Psi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right]\rho.

(G.35)

Finally, consider the terms in the parentheses in
Equation

G.28
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}+\widetilde{a}_{r}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}){\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}+\widetilde{b}_{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}){\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{a}_{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}^{2}\right]+\widetilde{b}_{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho}{\delta}\left(\widetilde{a}_{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]+\widetilde{b}_{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\right)=\overline{\kappa}_{2}\rho a_{r+1}=\sigma^{2}a_{r+1},

where the second line follows since both
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}
and
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}
are independent of
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}
.
Similarly, the terms in the parentheses in
Equation

G.27
can be simplified to
\sigma^{2}a_{s+1}
.

Putting all the terms together, we conclude that
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{r+1}{\mathsf{J}}_{s+1}\right]
evaluates to
Equation

4.29c
.

We use similar steps to check the covariance structure of
({\mathsf{K}}_{r+1})_{0\leq r\leq t}
.
For any
0\leq r,s\leq t
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{r+1}{\mathsf{K}}_{s+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{r+1}{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1}\right]=(\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1})_{r+1,s+1}=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{r}{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{s}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\Big[\left(\Phi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}+\widetilde{b}_{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})+\widetilde{\Phi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}+\widetilde{a}_{r}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})-b_{r+1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\left(\Phi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}+\widetilde{b}_{s}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})+\widetilde{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{a}_{s}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})-b_{s+1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\right)\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\Phi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\right]\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}+\widetilde{b}_{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}+\widetilde{b}_{s}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]

(G.36)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})\widetilde{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}+\widetilde{a}_{r}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{a}_{s}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]+b_{r+1}b_{s+1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}^{2}\right]

(G.37)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\widetilde{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}+\widetilde{b}_{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{a}_{s}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]

(G.38)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\widetilde{\Phi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}+\widetilde{b}_{s}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}+\widetilde{a}_{r}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]

(G.39)

\displaystyle\quad-b_{r+1}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{a}_{s}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}){\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\right]-b_{s+1}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}+\widetilde{a}_{r}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}){\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\right],

(G.40)

where we have used the trace-free property of
\Phi_{t}
(see
Item

(A7)
) in
Equation

G.40
.

We examine each term in
Equations

G.36
,
G.37
,
G.38
,
G.39
and
G.40
separately.
The second expectation in
Equation

G.36
has been evaluated in
Equation

G.33
.
The first term in
Equation

G.37
can be computed similarly to
Equation

G.32
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})\widetilde{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}+\widetilde{a}_{r}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{a}_{s}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})\widetilde{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})\right]\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{r}\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{s}\right]-\widetilde{a}_{r}\widetilde{a}_{s}\sigma^{2}\right)+\widetilde{a}_{r}\widetilde{a}_{s}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})\widetilde{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]\rho.

The terms in

and
G.39
can be computed similarly to

and

:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\widetilde{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}+\widetilde{b}_{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{a}_{s}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{a}_{s}\widetilde{b}_{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\widetilde{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\rho,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\widetilde{\Phi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}+\widetilde{b}_{s}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}+\widetilde{a}_{r}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{a}_{r}\widetilde{b}_{s}\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\widetilde{\Phi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\rho.

The first expectation in
Equation

G.40
evaluates to:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{a}_{s}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}){\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{a}_{s}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\rho=b_{s+1}\rho,

where the last step is by the definition of

in

.
Similarly the second expectation in
