is:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{r}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}+\widetilde{a}_{r}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}){\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\right]

\displaystyle=b_{r+1}\rho.

Putting all the terms back, we obtain
Equation

4.29d
as the desired expression for
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{r+1}{\mathsf{K}}_{s+1}\right]
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

G.3
.

The proof is by induction on
t\geq 0
.
We will inductively show the following statements which imply the conclusion of
Theorem

G.3
:

1.

For every
t\geq 0
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t}\right]=0
.

2.

For every
t\geq 0
,
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t},\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t},\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
are non-singular.

3.

For every
t\geq 0
, there exist Gaussian random variables
{\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t},{\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}
with zero mean and strictly positive variances, independent of each other and of everything else such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t}&=\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}\left((\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t-1})^{-1}(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})_{0:t-1,t}\right)_{s}+{\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t},\\
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t}&=\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}\left((\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t-1})^{-1}(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t})_{0:t-1,t}\right)_{s}+{\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}.\end{split}

(G.41)

There exist Gaussian random variables
{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
with zero mean and strictly positive variances, independent of each other and of everything else such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&=\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1}\left((\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t})^{-1}(\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1})_{1:t,t+1}\right)_{s+1}+{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},\\
{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}&=\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{s+1}\left((\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t})^{-1}(\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1})_{1:t,t+1}\right)_{s+1}+{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}.\end{split}

(G.42)

4.

Equations

G.15
and
G.16
hold.

Base case.

We take
t=0
as the base case and verify the validity of
Items

1
,
4
,
2
and
3
for
t=0
.

By the choice of
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}
in
Equation

G.14e
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}){\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right]=0
which proves the first statement in
Item

1
.

The non-singularity of
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0},\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}
in
Item

2
reduces to positivity at
t=0
, which follows from
Item

(A28)
, as noted in
Equation

G.14d
.

We now verify
Equations

G.15
and
G.16
in
Item

4
for
t=0
.
Recall that
f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}=w_{p,\mathrm{in},1}=\beta_{*}
.
The joint convergence of

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&w_{p,\mathrm{in}}&w_{q,\mathrm{in}}\end{bmatrix}

and

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}&w_{p,\mathrm{out}}&w_{q,\mathrm{out}}\end{bmatrix}

(G.43)

holds due to the initial condition
Equation

G.14
.
We then show joint convergence of the iterates involved in the left of
Equations

G.15
and
G.16
(for
t=0
) by examining the updates
Equations

G.5a
,
G.5b
,
G.5c
and
G.5d
sequentially.
The first update
Equation

G.5a
generates

\displaystyle q_{\mathrm{in}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}Q^{\top}(\widetilde{r}^{0}-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}\beta_{*})&Q^{\top}\beta_{*}\end{bmatrix}

and

\displaystyle q_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}

\displaystyle=O^{\top}(\widetilde{p}^{0}-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}z)=O^{\top}\widetilde{p}^{0}-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}\Lambda Q^{\top}\beta_{*}

whose joint convergence with vectors in
Equation

G.43
is guaranteed by
Proposition

K.5
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\begin{bmatrix}f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&w_{p,\mathrm{in}}&w_{q,\mathrm{in}}&q_{\mathrm{in},2}&q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}\end{bmatrix}&\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}\end{bmatrix},\\
\begin{bmatrix}f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}&w_{p,\mathrm{out}}&w_{q,\mathrm{out}}&q_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}\end{bmatrix}&\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}\end{bmatrix},\end{split}

(G.44)

where

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\\
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(\begin{bmatrix}0\\
0\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}\rho&0\\
0&\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}\end{bmatrix}\right)

(G.45)

is independent of
{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2},{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0},{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in}},{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}
, and
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{0})
is independent of
{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0},{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out}},{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}
.
By positivity of
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}
and
\rho
(see
Equation

G.14d
and
Item

(A1)
), the covariance matrix of
\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}
is strictly positive definite.
Note that
\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}
and
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}
are also independent of each other.
This proves
Equation

G.41
in
Item

3
for
t=0
.

The second update
Equation

G.5b
generates

\displaystyle f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\diag(\phi_{0})Q^{\top}\widetilde{r}^{0}+\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\phi}_{0})^{\top}O^{\top}\widetilde{p}^{0}-{\mathfrak{b}}_{1}Q^{\top}\beta_{*},

\displaystyle f_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\diag(\psi_{0})O^{\top}\widetilde{p}^{0}+\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\psi}_{0})Q^{\top}\widetilde{r}^{0}-{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\Lambda Q^{\top}\beta_{*}&\Lambda Q^{\top}\beta_{*}\end{bmatrix},

whose joint convergence with vectors in
Equation

G.44
is guaranteed by
Propositions

K.5
and
K.2
,

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&w_{p,\mathrm{in}}&w_{q,\mathrm{in}}&q_{\mathrm{in},2}&q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}\end{bmatrix},

(G.46a)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}&w_{p,\mathrm{out}}&w_{q,\mathrm{out}}&q_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}&f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{0}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{0}\end{bmatrix},

(G.46b)

where
{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0},{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{0},{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}
are defined in
Equations

G.14h
,
G.14i
and
G.14j
, respectively.
Now
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{1},\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}
can be computed as per
Equation

G.14l
.
We argue that they must be strictly positive, thereby justifying
Item

2
for
t=0
.
For
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{1}
, we write it as

\displaystyle\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0})^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{0}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})+\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{0}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})-{\mathfrak{b}}_{1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{0}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}+\left(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{0}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}-{\mathfrak{b}}_{1}\right){\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}+\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{0}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}\right\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{0}^{2}\right]\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{0}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\right)^{2}\right]\rho+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{0}^{2}\right]\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{0},

(G.47)

which can be seen positive since at least one of the first and last terms above is positive by
Equation

G.14d
and
Item

(A29)
.
Similarly, the positivity of

\displaystyle\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{0}^{2}\right]\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{0}+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{0}-{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right)^{2}\right]\rho+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{0}^{2}\right]\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}

(G.48)

follows from the positivity of
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{0},\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}
and at least one of
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{0}^{2}\right],\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{0}^{2}\right]
.

Moving on, the third update
Equation

G.5c
generates

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{in}}^{1}

\displaystyle=Q\diag(\phi_{0})Q^{\top}\widetilde{r}^{0}+Q\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\phi}_{0})^{\top}O^{\top}\widetilde{p}^{0}-{\mathfrak{b}}_{1}\beta_{*}=r^{1}-{\mathfrak{b}}_{1}\beta_{*},

\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{out}}^{1}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}O\diag(\psi_{0})O^{\top}\widetilde{p}^{0}+O\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\psi}_{0})Q^{\top}\widetilde{r}^{0}-{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}O\Lambda Q^{\top}\beta_{*}&O\Lambda Q^{\top}\beta_{*}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}p^{1}-{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}z&z\end{bmatrix}.

To understand their joint convergence, we condition on all vectors on the left of
Equation

G.46
.
The laws of
Q,O
are then conditioned on the events
Qq_{\mathrm{in}}^{0}=f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{0},Oq_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}=f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}
, respectively.
The Wasserstein convergence of
q_{\mathrm{in}}^{0}
and
q_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}
in
Equation

G.44
implies
d^{-1}(q_{\mathrm{in}}^{0})^{\top}q_{\mathrm{in}}^{0}\to\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}&0\\
0&\rho\end{bmatrix}\succ 0_{2\times 2}
(see
Equation

G.45
) and
n^{-1}(q_{\mathrm{out}}^{0})^{\top}q_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}\to\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{0}>0
(see
Equation

G.14d
).
In particular, the left-hand sides are invertible for all sufficiently large
n,d
.
Using
Proposition

K.6
, for all sufficiently large
n,d
, we can write the conditional law of
p_{\mathrm{in}}^{1},p_{\mathrm{out}}^{1}
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left.p_{\mathrm{in}}^{1}\,|\,\left\{Qq_{\mathrm{in}}^{0}=f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{0}\right\}\right.&=\left.Qf_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}\,|\,\left\{Qq_{\mathrm{in}}^{0}=f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{0}\right\}\right.\overset{d}{=}f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{0}((q_{\mathrm{in}}^{0})^{\top}q_{\mathrm{in}}^{0})^{-1}(q_{\mathrm{in}}^{0})^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}+\Pi_{(f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{0})^{\perp}}\widetilde{Q}\Pi_{(q_{\mathrm{in}}^{0})^{\perp}}^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0},\\
\left.p_{\mathrm{out}}^{1}\,|\,\left\{Oq_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}=f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}\right\}\right.&=\left.Of_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{0}\,|\,\left\{Oq_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}=f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}\right\}\right.\overset{d}{=}f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}((q_{\mathrm{out}}^{0})^{\top}q_{\mathrm{out}}^{0})^{-1}(q_{\mathrm{out}}^{0})^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{0}+\Pi_{(f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0})^{\perp}}\widetilde{O}\Pi_{(q_{\mathrm{out}}^{0})^{\perp}}^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{0},\end{split}

(G.49)

where
(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{O})\sim\haar({\mathbb{O}}(d-2))\otimes\haar({\mathbb{O}}(n-1))
are independent of everything else.
We claim that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}(q_{\mathrm{in}}^{0})^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}

\displaystyle\to\begin{bmatrix}0\\
0\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}(q_{\mathrm{out}}^{0})^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{0}

\displaystyle\to\begin{bmatrix}0&0\end{bmatrix},

(G.50)

entry-wise.
To show the first claim in
Equation

G.50
, using the joint convergence result
Equation

G.46
, zero mean of
{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{0}
and the definition of
{\mathfrak{b}}_{1}
in
Equation

G.14k
, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}q_{\mathrm{in},2}^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\left\{{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{0}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})+\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{0}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})-{\mathfrak{b}}_{1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{0}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}^{2}\right]+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}^{2}\right]-{\mathfrak{b}}_{1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\rho\left\{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]-{\mathfrak{b}}_{1}\right\}=0,

(G.51)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}(q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0})^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}\left\{{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{0}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})+\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{0}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})-{\mathfrak{b}}_{1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{0}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0})^{2}\right]=0.

(G.52)

The second claim in
Equation

G.50
can be similarly proved using the joint convergence result
Equation

G.46
and zero mean of
{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{0}
.
Therefore, by
Propositions

K.4
and
K.5
we have that
\left.p_{\mathrm{in}}^{1}\,|\,\left\{Qq_{\mathrm{in}}^{0}=f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{0}\right\}\right.
and
\left.p_{\mathrm{out}}^{1}\,|\,\left\{Oq_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}=f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}\right\}\right.
have Gaussian limits due to the second terms on the right of
Equation

G.49
, denoted by
{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
({\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{1})^{\top}=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{out},1}^{1}&{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{out},2}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, respectively, independent of everything else.
In formulas,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\begin{bmatrix}f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&w_{p,\mathrm{in}}&w_{q,\mathrm{in}}&q_{\mathrm{in},2}&q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}&p_{\mathrm{in}}^{1}\end{bmatrix}\\
&\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}&{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{1}\end{bmatrix},\\
&\begin{bmatrix}f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}&w_{p,\mathrm{out}}&w_{q,\mathrm{out}}&q_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}&f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{0}&p_{\mathrm{out},1}^{1}&p_{\mathrm{out},2}\end{bmatrix}\\
&\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{0}&{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{out},1}^{1}&{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{out},2}\end{bmatrix}.\end{split}

(G.53)

We then argue that
{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{1},{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{out},1}^{1},{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{out},2}
are precisely
{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{1},{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{1},{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}
by verifying that the joint distributions between the former random variables with others on the right of
Equation

G.53
are consistent with the definition
Equations

G.14m
and
G.14n
.
By the first claim in
Equation

G.50
,
{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{1}
is Gaussian with variance:

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\left\|p_{\mathrm{in}}^{1}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\left\|f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{1},

independent of all other random variables on the right of
Equation

G.53
.
Similarly, by the second claim in
Equation

G.50
and the convergence result
Equation

G.46
,
({\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{out},1}^{1},{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{out},2})
are jointly Gaussian with covariance:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}(p_{\mathrm{out}}^{1})^{\top}p_{\mathrm{out}}^{1}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}(f_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{0})^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{0}=\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{1}&0\\
0&\sigma^{2}\end{bmatrix},

independent of all other random variables on the right of
Equation

G.53
.
The bottom-right entry of the covariance matrix above follows since
\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2})^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}^{2}\right]=\overline{\kappa}_{2}\rho=\sigma^{2}
.
The off-diagonal entries follow from

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{0}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]-{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\rho+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{0}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right]\rho-{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\overline{\kappa}_{2}\rho=0,

(G.54)

and the choices of
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{0},\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{0}
in
Equation

G.14e
and of
{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}
in
Equation

G.14k
.
This proves
Equation

G.42
in
Item

3
for
t=0
.
Also,
Equation

G.54
above proves the second statement in
Item

1
for
t=0
.

Finally, the fourth update
Equation

G.5d
generates

\displaystyle f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{1}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}f_{1}(r^{1};\beta_{*},\Theta)-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{1}\beta_{*}&\beta_{*}\end{bmatrix}

and

\displaystyle f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{1}

\displaystyle=g_{1}(p^{1};z,\varepsilon,\Xi)-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{1}z,

whose joint convergence with all preceding iterates is guaranteed by
Propositions

K.2
and
K.5
.
In formulas, we have the desired result:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\begin{bmatrix}q_{\mathrm{in},2}&q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}&p_{\mathrm{in}}^{1}&f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{1}&w_{p,\mathrm{in}}&w_{q,\mathrm{in}}\end{bmatrix}\\
&\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{1}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{1}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}\\
&\begin{bmatrix}q_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}&f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{0}&p_{\mathrm{out},2}&p_{\mathrm{out},1}^{1}&f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}&f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{1}&w_{p,\mathrm{out}}&w_{q,\mathrm{out}}\end{bmatrix}\\
&\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{0}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{1}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{1}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}.\end{split}

(G.55)

Induction step.

Now assuming the validity of
Items

1
,
4
,
2
and
3
for time up to
t\geq 0
, we verify their validity for time
t+1
.

The statement
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}\right]=0
in
Item

1
(for time
t+1
)
holds since

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t+1}({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}+{\mathfrak{b}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{B}}_{*};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}}){\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right]-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1}\rho=0,

(G.56)

by the definition of
{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}
in
Equation

G.14o
and the definition of
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1}
in
Equation

G.14q
.

After the updates for time
t
, the covariance matrices
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
have been computed.
We write the first two in block form:

\displaystyle\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}&\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}\\
(\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})^{\top}&s_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(t+2)\times(t+2)},

\displaystyle\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}&\sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}\\
(\sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t})^{\top}&s_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(t+2)\times(t+2)},

(G.57)

where we have introduced the notation
\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t},\sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{t+1}
and
s_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},s_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Notationally, we index the elements of
\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t},\sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}
using the set
\left\{0,1,\cdots,t\right\}
, which is consistent with the indexing of
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
.
We verify that
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
is strictly positive definite.
Recalling the definition
Equation

G.14r
, given
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}
being non-singular due to the induction hypothesis in
Item

2
,
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
is singular if and only if there exist coefficients
\alpha_{0},\cdots,\alpha_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, such that almost surely,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\alpha_{s}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{s}.

However, this is excluded by
Item

(A30)
.
So
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
is invertible.
A similar argument applies to
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
.
This proves part of
Item

2
for time
t+1
.

Induction step for
q_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
.

We collect some past iterates into matrices
U
and
V
:

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}q_{\mathrm{in},2}&q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&\cdots&q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t}&f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}&\cdots&f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times(1+2(t+1))},

(G.58)

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&\cdots f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t}&p_{\mathrm{in}}^{1}&\cdots&p_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times(1+2(t+1))}.

(G.59)

According to the update rules of the abstract VAMP iteration
Equation

G.5
, the distribution of
q_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
given
w_{p,\mathrm{in}},w_{p,\mathrm{out}},w_{q,\mathrm{in}},w_{q,\mathrm{out}}
,
(q_{\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
(q_{\mathrm{out}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
(f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
(f_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
(p_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
(p_{\mathrm{out}}^{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
(f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t+1}
,
(f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t+1}
is the same as the law of
q_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
conditioned on the event
U=Q^{\top}V
.
Here we have used the orthogonality of
Q
which implies that the condition
p_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1}=Qf_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{s}
given by the first update in
Equation

G.5c
is identical to
Q^{\top}p_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1}=f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{s}
for all
0\leq s\leq t
.
For all sufficiently large
d
, the latter conditional distribution equals

\displaystyle\left.q_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\,|\,\left\{U=Q^{\top}V\right\}\right.

\displaystyle=\left.Q^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\,|\,\left\{U=Q^{\top}V\right\}\right.\overset{d}{=}U(U^{\top}U)^{-1}V^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}+\Pi_{U^{\perp}}\widetilde{Q}^{\top}\Pi_{V^{\perp}}^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times 2},

(G.60)

where the last step follows from
Proposition

K.6
(which contains the definitions of the projectors
\Pi_{U^{\perp}},\Pi_{V^{\perp}}
) and
\widetilde{Q}\sim\haar({\mathbb{O}}(d-(1+2(t+1))))
is independent of everything else.
We claim that
U^{\top}U
in the above display is invertible for all sufficiently large
d
and will justify this claim in
Equation

G.69
.
(We remark that across different passages of the induction proof, the notation
U,V,\widetilde{O},\widetilde{Q}
will be overloaded.)
To analyze the first term on the right in
Equation

G.60
, we compute the limits of
d^{-1}U^{\top}U
and
d^{-1}V^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}
.

Let us start with the computation of the limit of
d^{-1}U^{\top}U
.
Note that by the induction hypothesis
Equation

G.15
, for any
0\leq r,s\leq t
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},2},q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\left\langle\beta_{*},\beta_{*}\right\rangle\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}^{2}\right]=\rho,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},2},q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}\right]=0,

(G.61a)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r},q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}\right]=(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})_{r,s},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{r},f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{r}{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{s}\right]=(\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1})_{r,s}.

(G.61b)

We will then show

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r},f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},2},f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0.

(G.62)

The first term is:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r},f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r},\diag(\phi_{s})(q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{s}q_{\mathrm{in},2})\right\rangle

(G.63)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r},\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\phi}_{s})^{\top}(q_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{s}\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2})\right\rangle

(G.64)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{{\mathfrak{b}}_{s+1}}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r},q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle,

(G.65)

where, by the induction hypotheses
Equations

G.15
and
G.16
in
Item

4
, the right-hand sides have the following limits:

Equation

G.63

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{s}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2})\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{s}\right]=0,

(G.66)

Equation

G.64

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r},\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\phi}_{s})^{\top}q_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}\right\rangle+\frac{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{s}}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r},\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\phi}_{s})^{\top}\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{s}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{s}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]=0,

(G.67)

Equation

G.65

\displaystyle=-\frac{{\mathfrak{b}}_{s+1}}{d}\left\langle Q^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{1},Q^{\top}\beta_{*}\right\rangle=-\frac{{\mathfrak{b}}_{s+1}}{d}\left\langle f_{r}(p_{\mathrm{in}}^{r}+{\mathfrak{b}}_{r}\beta_{*};\beta_{*},\Theta)-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{r}\beta_{*},\beta_{*}\right\rangle\to 0.

(G.68)

The equality in
Equation

G.66
follows from independence between
({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t},{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{s}
and the zero mean of
{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{s}
in
Item

(A29)
.
The equality in
Equation

G.67
follows from independence between
({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
and
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2},({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
.
The equality in
Equation

G.68
follows from the definition of
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{r}
in
Equation

G.14q
.
Therefore, the first statement in
Equation

G.62
holds.

Turning to the second statement in
Equation

G.62
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},2},f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},2},\diag(\phi_{s})(q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}q_{\mathrm{in},2})\right\rangle+\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},2},\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\phi}_{s})^{\top}(q_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{s}\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2})-{\mathfrak{b}}_{s+1}q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle.

The first term has limit
0
due to similar reasoning leading to
Equation

G.66
.
For the second term, by manipulations similar to those leading to
Equation

G.67
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},2},\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\phi}_{s})^{\top}(q_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{s}\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2})\right\rangle-\frac{{\mathfrak{b}}_{s+1}}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},2},q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},2},\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\phi}_{s})^{\top}q_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}\right\rangle+\frac{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{s}}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},2},\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\phi}_{s})^{\top}\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle-\frac{{\mathfrak{b}}_{s+1}}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},2},q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{s}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{s}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}^{2}\right]-{\mathfrak{b}}_{s+1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{s}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{s}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\rho-{\mathfrak{b}}_{s+1}\rho=0,

where the last step follows from the definition of
{\mathfrak{b}}_{s+1}
in
Equation

G.14k
.
Therefore the second equality in
Equation

G.62
also holds.
Taking
Equations

G.62
and
G.61
collectively gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}U^{\top}U

\displaystyle\to\begin{bmatrix}\rho&0_{1\times(t+1)}&0_{1\times(t+1)}\\
0_{(t+1)\times 1}&\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}&0_{(t+1)\times(t+1)}\\
0_{(t+1)\times 1}&0_{(t+1)\times(t+1)}&\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(1+2(t+1))\times(1+2(t+1))}.

(G.69)

Note that the matrix on the right is strictly positive definite by
\rho>0
in
Item

(A1)
and the induction hypothesis in
Item

2
.

Next, consider
d^{-1}V^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
.
By the induction hypothesis
Equation

G.15
in
Item

4
, for any
0\leq s\leq t
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle f_{p,\mathrm{in},2},f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}\right]=0,

(G.70a)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{s},f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{s}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}\right]=(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1})_{s,t+1}=(\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})_{s},

(G.70b)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle f_{p,\mathrm{in},2},f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}^{2}\right]=\rho,

(G.70c)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{s},f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{s}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}\right]=0.

(G.70d)

Equation

G.70a
follows from
Equation

G.56
, and

Equation

G.70d
follows from the induction hypothesis in
Item

1
.
Furthermore, we study the limit of

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}(p_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1})^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{d}\left\langle p_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1},f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}\right\rangle&\frac{1}{d}\left\langle p_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1},f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle\end{bmatrix}.

(G.71)

The second entry of
Equation

G.71
can be easily seen vanishing:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle p_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1},\beta_{*}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right]=0,

(G.72)

since
{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1}
and
{\mathsf{B}}_{*}
are independent.
As for the first entry of
Equation

G.71
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle p_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1},f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\left\langle p_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1},f_{t+1}(p_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}+{\mathfrak{b}}_{t+1}\beta_{*};\beta_{*},\Theta)\right\rangle-\frac{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1}}{d}\left\langle p_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1},\beta_{*}\right\rangle.

We have shown in
Equation

G.72
that the second term on the right vanishes.
By
Item

(A27)
and
Proposition

K.2
, the first term converges to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle p_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1},f_{t+1}(p_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}+{\mathfrak{b}}_{t+1}\beta_{*};\beta_{*},\Theta)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1}f_{t+1}({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}+{\mathfrak{b}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{B}}_{*};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right].

(G.73)

Since
({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1},{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1})
are jointly Gaussian and independent of
{\mathsf{B}}_{*}
, the above expression is zero by Stein’s lemma
[
49
, Theorem 2.1]
and divergence-freeness of
f_{t+1}
(see
Item

(A8)
).
Putting
Equations

G.72
and
G.73
together, we see that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}(p_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1})^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle\to\begin{bmatrix}0&0\end{bmatrix}.

(G.74)

Combining
Equations

G.70
and
G.74
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}V^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle\to\begin{bmatrix}0&\rho\\
\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}&0_{t+1}\\
0_{t+1}&0_{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(1+2(t+1))\times 2}.

(G.75)

We are now in a position to compute the right-hand side of
Equation

G.60
.
Using
Equations

G.69
and
G.75
, we have

\displaystyle(U^{\top}U)^{-1}V^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=(d^{-1}U^{\top}U)^{-1}\cdot d^{-1}V^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\to\begin{bmatrix}\rho^{-1}&0_{1\times(t+1)}&0_{1\times(t+1)}\\
0_{(t+1)\times 1}&(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})^{-1}&0_{(t+1)\times(t+1)}\\
0_{(t+1)\times 1}&0_{(t+1)\times(t+1)}&(\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1})^{-1}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0&\rho\\
\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}&0_{t+1}\\
0_{t+1}&0_{t+1}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}&0_{t+1}\\
0_{t+1}&0_{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(1+2(t+1))\times 2}.

By
Proposition

K.5
, the second term in
Equation

G.60
has a Gaussian limit in Wasserstein distance, denoted by
\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&0\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1\times 2}
, independent of
{\mathsf{A}}
,
({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
({\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
({\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t}
,
({\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t+1}
,
({\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t+1}
.
Note that the second entry is zero.
This follows since every column of
\Pi_{V^{\perp}}
is orthogonal to the first column of
V
which equals
f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}
, implying that
\Pi_{V^{\perp}}^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}
vanishes.
Using this together with the above display, recalling that

\displaystyle U\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}\end{bmatrix}

by the induction hypothesis
Equation

G.15
in
Item

4
, and applying
Proposition

K.4
, we conclude

\displaystyle\left.q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}\,|\,\left\{U=Q^{\top}V\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t}\end{bmatrix}(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}+{\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}=\sum_{s=0}^{t}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}\left((\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}\right)_{s}+{\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},

(G.76a)

\displaystyle\left.q_{\mathrm{in},2}\,|\,\left\{U=Q^{\top}V\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}.

(G.76b)

We claim that the right side of the two equations above is precisely
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}
.
To justify this, note that
({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t+1}
is jointly Gaussian and is independent of
{\mathsf{A}},({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t},({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t}
.
So by the definition
Equation

G.14f
, it remains to verify that the right-hand side of
Equation

G.76a
, denoted by
\widetilde{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
, has the desired covariance structure with
({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
.
For any
0\leq r\leq t
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}\right]\left((\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}\right)_{s}+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{r}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})_{r,s}\left((\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}\right)_{s}+0=\left(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}\right)_{r}=(\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})_{r}.

(G.77a)

Note also that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\left\|q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\left\|f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1})^{2}\right]=s_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}.

(G.77b)

Moreover,
\widetilde{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}
is independent of
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}
since
({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
and
{\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
are all independent of
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}
.

From this and
Equation

G.77
, we see that
\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}
has the desired covariance structure with
({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
which we identify as
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
, and therefore

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}q_{\mathrm{in},2}&{\bm{q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&{\bm{f}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}&{\bm{p}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&{\bm{f}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&w_{p,\mathrm{in}}&w_{q,\mathrm{in}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}&\bm{{\mathsf{P}}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}.

(G.78)

We check that the variance of
{\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
is strictly positive.
Since
({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{t+1},\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{t}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}\left((\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}\right)_{s}

\displaystyle\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(0,\left((\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}\right)^{\top}\cdot\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}\cdot\left((\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}\right)\right)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{N}}\left(0,(\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})^{\top}(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}\right).

(G.79)

According to
Equation

G.76
, the variance of
{\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=s_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}-(\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})^{\top}(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t},

where the two terms on the right come from
Equations

G.77b
and
G.79
.
By the Schur complement formula,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left[(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1})^{-1}\right]_{t+1,t+1}}=\left(e_{t+1}^{\top}(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1})^{-1}e_{t+1}\right)^{-1}>0,

(G.80)

where the strict inequality follows from strict positive definiteness of
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
shown around
Equation

G.57
.
This proves the first statement of
Equation

G.41
in
Item

3
.

Induction step for
q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
.

Redefine the matrices
U,V
as

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}q_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}&\cdots&q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t}&f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{0}&\cdots&f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times(1+2(t+1))},

(G.81)

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}&\cdots&f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t}&p_{\mathrm{out},2}&p_{\mathrm{out},1}^{1}&\cdots&p_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times(1+2(t+1))}.

(G.82)

The law of
q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
given all previous iterates (including the initializers and side information) is the same as the law of
q_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
given the condition
U=O^{\top}V
.
By
Proposition

K.6
, the latter conditional law is

\displaystyle\left.q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\,|\,\left\{U=O^{\top}V\right\}\right.=\left.O^{\top}f_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\,|\,\left\{U=O^{\top}V\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}U(U^{\top}U)^{-1}V^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}+\Pi_{U^{\top}}\widetilde{O}^{\top}\Pi_{V^{\perp}}^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},

(G.83)

where
\widetilde{O}\sim\haar({\mathbb{O}}(n-(1+2(t+1))))
is independent of everything else.
To analyze the first term on the right, we compute the limits of
n^{-1}U^{\top}U
and
n^{-1}V^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
.
In particular,
Equation

G.87
below shows that
U^{\top}U
on the right of
Equation

G.83
is invertible for every sufficiently large
n
.

Starting with
n^{-1}U^{\top}U
, for any
0\leq r,s\leq t
, by induction hypotheses
Items

1
and
4
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{out}}^{r},q_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t})_{r,s},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle f_{q,\mathrm{out},2},f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\left\langle z,z\right\rangle\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}^{2}\right]=\sigma^{2},

(G.84a)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle f_{q,\mathrm{out},2},f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{r},f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to(\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1})_{r+1,s+1}.

(G.84b)

Note that the second equation in
Equation

G.84a
implies
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}^{2}\right]=\sigma^{2}
.
We then examine

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{out}}^{r},f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{s}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{out}}^{r},f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}\right\rangle.

(G.85)

The second term is easily seen to be vanishing:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{out}}^{r},f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left\langle O^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{r},\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{n}\left\langle g_{r}(p_{\mathrm{out},1}^{r}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{r}z;z,\varepsilon,\Xi)-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{r}z,z\right\rangle\to 0,

(G.86)

by the definition of
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{r}
in
Equation

G.14q
.
Turning to the first term in
Equation

G.85
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{out}}^{r},f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{s}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{n}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{out}}^{r},\diag(\psi_{s})(q_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{s}\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2})+\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\psi}_{s})(q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{s}q_{\mathrm{in},2})-{\mathfrak{a}}_{s+1}\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{out}}^{r},\diag(\psi_{s})q_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}\right\rangle+\frac{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{s}}{n}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{out}}^{r},\diag(\psi_{s})\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{n}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{out}}^{r},\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\psi}_{s})(q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{s}q_{\mathrm{in},2})\right\rangle-\frac{{\mathfrak{a}}_{s+1}}{n}\left\langle O^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{r},\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle.

The limit of the first term is zero since
({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r},{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s})
is independent of
{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{s}
which has zero mean (see
Item

(A29)
).
The limit of the second term is also zero since the Wasserstein limits
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r},{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}
of
q_{\mathrm{out}}^{r},q_{\mathrm{in},2}
are independent of one another.
The third term again converges to zero due to the independence between
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}
and
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s}
.
We have just shown in
Equation

G.86
that the fourth term converges to zero.
Therefore,
\frac{1}{n}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{out}}^{r},f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{s}\right\rangle\to 0
.

We have shown that both terms in
Equation

G.85
have limit zero.
By this and
Equation

G.84
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}U^{\top}U

\displaystyle\to\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}&0_{(t+1)\times 1}&0_{(t+1)\times(t+1)}\\
0_{1\times(t+1)}&\sigma^{2}&0_{1\times(t+1)}\\
0_{(t+1)\times(t+1)}&0_{(t+1)\times 1}&\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(1+2(t+1))\times(1+2(t+1))}.

(G.87)

The matrix on the right is invertible by the induction hypothesis in
Item

2
and the positivity of
\sigma^{2}=\overline{\kappa}_{2}\rho
implied by
Items

(A2)
and
(A1)
.

Next, we compute the limit of
n^{-1}V^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
.
By the induction hypothesis
Equation

G.16
in
Item

4
, for any
0\leq s\leq t
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{s},f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1})_{s,t+1}=(\sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t})_{s}.

(G.88)

We then examine

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle p_{\mathrm{out},2},f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle p_{\mathrm{out},1}^{s+1},f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\right\rangle.

(G.89)

The first term equals
n^{-1}\left\langle z,g_{t+1}(p_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+1}z;z,\varepsilon)-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1}z\right\rangle
which converges to zero, by the definition of
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1}
in
Equation

G.14q
.
As for the second term in
Equation

G.89
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle p_{\mathrm{out},1}^{s+1},f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left\langle p_{\mathrm{out},1}^{s+1},g_{t+1}(p_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}+{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+1}p_{\mathrm{out},2};p_{\mathrm{out},2},w_{p,\mathrm{out}})\right\rangle-\frac{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1}}{n}\left\langle p_{\mathrm{out},1}^{s+1},p_{\mathrm{out},2}\right\rangle.

(G.90)

Applying
Item

(A27)
and
Proposition

K.2
, noting that
({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{s+1},{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1},{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2})
is jointly Gaussian independent of
{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}
, using Stein’s lemma
[
49
, Theorem 2.1]
and the divergence-freeness of
g_{t+1}
(see
Item

(A8)
), the first term converges to zero.
We claim that the second term in
Equation

G.90
also has limit zero.
To show this, note that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle p_{\mathrm{out},1}^{s+1},p_{\mathrm{out},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left\langle f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{s},f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}\right\rangle\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{s}{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}\right]=0,

by the induction hypothesis in
Item

1
, as claimed.
Thus via
Equation

G.90
, we see that the second term in
Equation

G.89
has limit zero.

We have shown that both terms in
Equation

G.89
converge to zero.
Using this and
Equation

G.88
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}V^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle\to\begin{bmatrix}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}\\
0\\
0_{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2(t+1)}.

Further combining the above display with
Equation

G.87
, and recalling that

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out}.1}^{1}\end{bmatrix}

by the induction hypothesis
Equation

G.16
in
Item

4
, we obtain the Wasserstein limit of the first term on the right-hand side of
Equation

G.83
:

\displaystyle U\cdot(n^{-1}U^{\top}U)^{-1}\cdot n^{-1}V^{\top}f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}&0_{(t+1)\times 1}&0_{(t+1)\times(t+1)}\\
0_{1\times(t+1)}&\sigma^{2}&0_{1\times(t+1)}\\
0_{(t+1)\times(t+1)}&0_{(t+1)\times 1}&\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}\\
0\\
0_{t+1}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}\left((\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}\right)_{s}.

Moreover, by
Proposition

K.5
the second term on the right side of
Equation

G.83
admits a Gaussian distributional limit, denoted by
{\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, independent of everything else.
So overall, the conditional empirical distribution of
q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
converges to that of the random variable

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}=\sum_{s=0}^{t}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}\left((\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}\right)_{s}+{\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}

(G.91)

which is independent of
{\mathsf{A}}
and
({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t},({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t}
.
We will next verify that this random variable
\widetilde{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
is precisely
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
by noting that
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0},\cdots,{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t},\widetilde{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
are jointly Gaussian and checking their covariance structure.

For any
0\leq s\leq t
, it is easy to compute:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{r}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t})_{r,s}\left((\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}\right)_{s}=(\sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t})_{r},

(G.92a)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left\|q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left\|f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1})^{2}\right]=s_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},

(G.92b)

thereby verifying the desired covariance structure of
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{0},\cdots,{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t},\widetilde{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
.
This allows us to identify
\widetilde{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
with
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
and conclude

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&{\bm{f}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t}&p_{\mathrm{out},2}&{\bm{p}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}&{\bm{f}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&w_{p,\mathrm{out}}&w_{q,\mathrm{out}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}\bm{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{P}}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}.

(G.93)

We also check that the variance of
{\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
is strictly positive:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1})^{2}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{s=0}^{t}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}\left((\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}\right)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=s_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}-\left((\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}\right)^{\top}\cdot\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}\cdot\left((\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=s_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}-(\sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t})^{\top}(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t}=\frac{1}{e_{t+1}^{\top}(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1})^{-1}e_{t+1}}>0,

(G.94)

by the Schur complement formula and strict positive definiteness of
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
shown around
Equation

G.57
.
This proves the second statement of
Equation

G.41
in
Item

3
.

Induction step for
f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},f_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
.

As a direct consequence of
Equations

G.78
and
G.93
shown in the previous two steps, the update rules in
Equation

G.5b
of the abstract VAMP algorithm, and
Proposition

K.2
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}q_{\mathrm{in},2}&{\bm{q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&{\bm{f}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&{\bm{p}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&{\bm{f}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&w_{p,\mathrm{in}}&w_{q,\mathrm{in}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&\bm{{\mathsf{P}}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

(G.95)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&{\bm{f}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}&p_{\mathrm{out},2}&{\bm{p}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}&{\bm{f}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&w_{p,\mathrm{out}}&w_{q,\mathrm{out}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}\bm{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{P}}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}.

(G.96)

We verify that the induction hypothesis in
Item

1
continues to hold for
{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}
.
By the convergence result
Equation

G.96
above,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}\right]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left\langle f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1},f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\diag(\psi_{t+1})(q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1}\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2})+\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\psi}_{t+1})(q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1}q_{\mathrm{in},2})-{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+2}\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2},\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\diag(\psi_{t+1})(q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1}\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2}),\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle+\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\psi}_{t+1})(q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1}q_{\mathrm{in},2}),\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle-{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+2}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left\langle z,z\right\rangle.

(G.97)

The last term above equals
-{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+1}\sigma^{2}
.
The first term can be computed as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\diag(\psi_{t+1})(q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1}\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2}),\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\Lambda^{\top}\diag(\psi_{t+1})q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle+\frac{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1}}{n}\left\langle\Lambda^{\top}\diag(\psi_{t+1})\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2},q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0+\frac{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1}}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}^{2}\right]=\frac{\rho}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1},

(G.98a)

where the last line follows from independence of
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}
.
Finally, we turn to the second term in
Equation

G.97
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\psi}_{t+1})(q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}+\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1}q_{\mathrm{in},2}),\Lambda q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\Lambda^{\top}\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\psi}_{t+1})q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1},q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle+\frac{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1}}{n}\left\langle\Lambda^{\top}\diag_{n\times d}(\widetilde{\psi}_{t+1})q_{\mathrm{in},2},q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0+\frac{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1}}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{t+1}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}^{2}\right]=\frac{\rho}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1},

(G.98b)

since
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}
and
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}
are independent.
From
Equation

G.98
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1},f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to\frac{\rho}{\delta}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1}+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1}\right)-{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+2}\sigma^{2}=0,

(G.99)

by the definition of
{\mathfrak{a}}_{t+2}
in
Equation

G.14k
.
Here it helps to recall
{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n},{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}
from
Equation

3.8
.
This establishes
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}\right]=0
, which, together with
Equation

G.56
, confirms the validity of
Item

1
for time
t+1
.

Given the new random variables
{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
, the covariance matrices
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+2},\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}
can be computed according to
Equation

G.14l
.
We write these matrices in block form:

\displaystyle\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&\sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\\
(\sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1})^{\top}&s_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(t+2)\times(t+2)},

\displaystyle\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&\sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\\
(\sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1})^{\top}&s_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(t+2)\times(t+2)},

(G.100)

where we have introduced the notation
\sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},\sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{t+1}
,
s_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+2},s_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Notationally, we index the elements of
\sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},\sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
using the set
\left\{1,2,\cdots,t+1\right\}
, which is consistent with the indexing of
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+2},\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}
.
We now verify the invertibility of
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}
.
Recalling the definition
Equation

G.14l
, given the invertibility of
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
guaranteed by the induction hypothesis in
Item

2
,
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}
is singular if and only if there exist
\alpha_{0},\cdots,\alpha_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, such that almost surely,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\alpha_{s}{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{s}.

By the definition
Equation

G.14h
of
{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
, the above condition is equivalent to

\displaystyle{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}+\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\alpha_{s}{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{s}-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1}\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}+{\mathfrak{b}}_{t+2}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}.

(G.101)

Item

(A29)
ensures that at least one of the random variables
{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t+1},\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t+1}
is not almost surely a constant.
Suppose that this is the case for
{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t+1}
.
Using the representations of
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}
identified in
Equation

G.76a
, we can further write
Equation

G.101
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\alpha_{s}{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{s}-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}-\widetilde{{\mathfrak{a}}}_{t+1}\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}+{\mathfrak{b}}_{t+2}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}\\
&\quad-\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}-{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t+1}\sum_{s=0}^{t}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}\left((\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})^{-1}\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}\right)_{s}.\end{split}

(G.102)

Then we condition on a realization
(\phi,f)
of
{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t+1}
and the right-hand side of the above equation such that
\phi\neq 0
.
Such
\phi
exists since we assumed that
{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t+1}
is non-constant.
The conditioning does not change the distribution of
{\mathsf{U}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}
since it is independent of other random variables in
Equation

G.102
.
Since
{\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
is Gaussian with strictly positive variance as shown in
Equation

G.80
, we have
\mathbb{P}\left({\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\neq f/\phi\right)>0
.
This implies that
Equation

G.102
cannot hold almost surely and therefore
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}
must be non-singular.
On the other hand, if
{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t+1}
is constant,
Item

(A29)
ensures that
\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t+1}
is non-constant.
We can run the same argument but now use the representation of
{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
identified in
Equation

G.91
and leave
\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
on the left-hand side of
Equation

G.102
.
Positivity of the variance of
{\mathsf{U}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
shown in
Equation

G.94
allows us to draw the same conclusion of invertibility of
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}
.
An analogous proof applies to
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}
whose details we omit.

Induction step for
p_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}
.

We redefine
U,V
to be

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}q_{\mathrm{in},2}&q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&\cdots&q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{0}&\cdots&f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times 2(t+2)},

(G.103)

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&\cdots&f_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&p_{\mathrm{in}}^{1}&\cdots&p_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times 2(t+2)}.

(G.104)

The conditional law of
p_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}
given all past iterates and side information is the same as that of
p_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}
given the condition
V=QU
.
By
Proposition

K.6
, for every sufficiently large
d
, the latter conditional law can be written as

\displaystyle\left.p_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}\,|\,\left\{V=QU\right\}\right.=\left.Qf_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\,|\,\left\{V=QU\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}V(U^{\top}U)^{-1}U^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}+\Pi_{V^{\perp}}\widetilde{Q}\Pi_{U^{\perp}}^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1},

(G.105)

where
\widetilde{Q}\sim\haar({\mathbb{O}}(d-2(t+2)))
is independent of everything else.
To proceed, we compute the limits of
d^{-1}U^{\top}U
and
d^{-1}U^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
.
In particular,
Equation

G.106
below shows that
U^{\top}U
on the right of
Equation

G.105
is invertible for every sufficiently large
d
.

The steps for computing the limit of
d^{-1}U^{\top}U
are similar to those for
Equation

G.69
.
Compared to
Equation

G.58
,
U
defined in
Equation

G.103
above has one additional column
q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}
.
So we only need to check the inner product between
q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}
and other columns of
U
.
By the convergence result
Equation

G.78
shown above, for
0\leq s\leq t
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1},q_{\mathrm{in},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1},q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to(\sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t})_{s},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1},q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to s_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}.

Furthermore, by an analysis similar to that for the first term in
Equation

G.62
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1},f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0.

Combining these facts with
Equation

G.69
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}U^{\top}U

\displaystyle\to\begin{bmatrix}\rho&0_{1\times(t+2)}&0_{1\times(t+1)}\\
0_{(t+2)\times 1}&\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&0_{(t+2)\times(t+1)}\\
0_{(t+1)\times 1}&0_{(t+1)\times(t+2)}&\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2(t+2)\times 2(t+2)}.

(G.106)

The matrix on the right-hand side is strictly positive definite by the positivity of
\rho
guaranteed by
Item

(A1)
, the positive definiteness of
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
shown around
Equation

G.57
, and the positive definiteness of
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
due to the induction hypothesis in
Item

2
.

Moving to
d^{-1}U^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}
,
by the convergence result
Equation

G.95
shown above and analysis similar to that for the two terms in
Equation

G.62
, for every
0\leq s\leq t+1
and
0\leq r\leq t
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},2},f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{in},1}^{s},f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{r},f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to(\sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1})_{r}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}U^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle\to\begin{bmatrix}0\\
0_{t+2}\\
\sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2(t+2)\times 1}.

(G.107)

Plugging
Equations

G.106
and
G.107
in the right-hand side of
Equation

G.105
, using the fact that the second term in
Equation

G.105
has a Gaussian limit due to
Proposition

K.5
, denoted by
{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}
, independent of everything else, recalling from the induction hypothesis
Equation

G.15
in
Item

4
that

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{1}&\cdots&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix},

and applying
Proposition

K.4
, we have that the conditional law of
p_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}
converges in Wasserstein to the law of

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{1}&\cdots&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\rho^{-1}&0_{1\times(t+2)}&0_{1\times(t+1)}\\
0_{(t+2)\times 1}&(\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1})^{-1}&0_{(t+2)\times(t+1)}\\
0_{(t+1)\times 1}&0_{(t+1)\times(t+2)}&(\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1})^{-1}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
0_{t+2}\\
\sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}+{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{t+1}{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s}\left((\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1})^{-1}\sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}\right)_{s}+{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}.

(G.108)

The random variable above is jointly Gaussian with
({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t}
and they are independent of
{\mathsf{A}}
and
({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t+1},({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t+1}
.
It can be verified in a way similar to
Equation

G.77
that the above random variable has the desired covariance matrix
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}
with
({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t}
and therefore is precisely the random variable
{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}
.
Furthermore, using the Schur complement formula, positive definiteness of
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}
shown around
Equation

G.100
, the independence between the two terms in
Equation

G.108
and applying arguments similar to
Equation

G.79
, the variance of
{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}
is strictly positive.
We omit these details to avoid repetition.
This proves the first statement of
Equation

G.42
in
Item

3
.

The above reasoning establishes the convergence result:

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}q_{\mathrm{in},2}&{\bm{q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&{\bm{f}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&{\bm{p}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}&f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&{\bm{f}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&w_{p,\mathrm{in}}&w_{q,\mathrm{in}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&\bm{{\mathsf{P}}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}.

(G.109)

Induction step for
p_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}
.

Redefine
U,V
to be

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}q_{\mathrm{out}}^{0}&\cdots&q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{0}&\cdots&f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times 2(t+2)},

(G.110)

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}&\cdots&f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&p_{\mathrm{out},2}&p_{\mathrm{out},1}^{1}&\cdots&p_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times 2(t+2)}.

(G.111)

The conditional law of
p_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}
given all past iterates and side information is the same as that of
p_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}
given the condition
V=OU
.
We then use
Proposition

K.6
to characterize the latter conditional distribution: for all sufficiently large

,

\displaystyle\left.p_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}\,|\,\left\{V=OU\right\}\right.=\left.Of_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\,|\,\left\{V=OU\right\}\right.\overset{d}{=}V(U^{\top}U)^{-1}U^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}+\Pi_{V^{\perp}}\widetilde{O}\Pi_{U^{\perp}}^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},

(G.112)

where
\widetilde{O}\sim\haar({\mathbb{O}}(n-2(t+2)))
is independent of everything else.
Again, we compute the limits
n^{-1}U^{\top}U
and
n^{-1}U^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
.
In particular,
Equation

G.113
below justifies, for all sufficiently large
n
, the invertibility of
U^{\top}U
which shows up on the right of
Equation

G.112
.

First consider
n^{-1}U^{\top}U
.
Note that
U
defined in
Equation

G.110
has one additional column
q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
compared to
U
in
Equation

G.81
.
So to compute the limit of
n^{-1}U^{\top}U
, we only need to examine the inner product between
q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
and other columns of
U
.
By the convergence result
Equation

G.93
already shown, for any
0\leq s\leq t
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},q_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to(\sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t})_{s},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to s_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle q_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1},f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0,

where the last two limits can be treated similarly to
Equation

G.85
.
Combining these limits with
Equation

G.87
yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}U^{\top}U

\displaystyle\to\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&0_{(t+2)\times 1}&0_{(t+2)\times(t+1)}\\
0_{1\times(t+2)}&\sigma^{2}&0_{1\times(t+1)}\\
0_{(t+1)\times(t+2)}&0_{(t+1)\times 1}&\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2(t+2)\times 2(t+2)}.

(G.113)

The matrix on the right is strictly positive definite by the positive definiteness of
\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
shown around
Equation

G.57
, positivity of
\sigma^{2}
noted in
Equation

3.9
and the positive definiteness of
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
from the induction hypothesis in
Item

2
.

Next consider
n^{-1}U^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}
.
For any
0\leq s\leq t+1
and
0\leq r\leq t
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1},q_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1},f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1},f_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{r}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to(\sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1})_{r},

(G.114)

where the first limit can be treated similarly to the first term in
Equation

G.85
.
The second limit follows from
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}\right]=0
shown in
Equation

;
the third one directly follows from the convergence result
Equation

G.96
shown previously.
Moreover, it can be established similarly to the second term in
Equation

G.85
that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\langle f_{q,\mathrm{out},2},q_{\mathrm{out}}^{s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\to 0.

(G.115)

Recall also the second equation in
Equation

G.84a
.
This, together with
Equations

G.114
and
G.115
, implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}U^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle\to\begin{bmatrix}0_{t+2}&0_{t+2}\\
0&\sigma^{2}\\
\sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&0_{t+2}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2(t+2)\times 2}.

(G.116)

By
Proposition

K.5
, the second term in
Equation

G.112
admits a Gaussian limit, denoted by
\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}&0\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1\times 2}
.
The second entry vanishes since every column of
\Pi_{U^{\perp}}
is orthogonal to the column

in

and therefore
\Pi_{U^{\perp}}^{\top}f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}=0_{n-2(t+2)}
.
Combining this observation with
Equations

G.113
and
G.116
, recalling from the induction hypothesis
Equation

G.16
in
Item

4
that

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{1}&\cdots&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix},

and applying
Proposition

K.4
give us the Wasserstein limit of the right-hand side of
Equation

G.112
:

\displaystyle\left.p_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}\,|\,\left\{V=OU\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{0}&\cdots&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{1}&\cdots&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&0_{(t+2)\times 1}&0_{(t+2)\times(t+1)}\\
0_{1\times(t+2)}&\sigma^{2}&0_{1\times(t+1)}\\
0_{(t+1)\times(t+2)}&0_{(t+1)\times 1}&\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\end{bmatrix}^{-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\begin{bmatrix}0_{t+2}&0_{t+2}\\
0&\sigma^{2}\\
\sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&0_{t+2}\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{t}{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{s+1}\left((\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1})^{-1}\sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}\right)_{s+1}+{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}\end{bmatrix}.

We claim that the first entry on the right-hand side is precisely
{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+2}
.
To justify this, we take notice of the following facts.
The random variable in the first entry is jointly Gaussian with
({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t}
, with covariance matrix
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}
(following calculations similar to
Equation

G.92
).
These jointly Gaussian random variables are independent of
{\mathsf{A}}
and
({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t+1},({\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{s})_{0\leq s\leq t+1}
.
Furthermore, they are also independent of
{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}
since both
({\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{s+1})_{0\leq s\leq t}
and
{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}
are independent of
{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}
.
As before, the variance of
{\mathsf{U}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}
is strictly positive, where we use the positive definiteness of
\Sigma_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}
shown around
Equation

G.100
.
This proves the second statement of
Equation

G.42
in
Item

3
.

Therefore, we have shown
