\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&{\bm{f}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t}&p_{\mathrm{out},2}&{\bm{p}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+2}&{\bm{f}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&w_{p,\mathrm{out}}&w_{q,\mathrm{out}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}\bm{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{P}}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+2}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}.

(G.117)

Induction step for
f_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{t+2},f_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}
.

By the update rules
Equation

G.5d
, the convergence results
Equations

G.109
and
G.117
shown in the last two steps, and
Proposition

K.2
, we immediately get

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}q_{\mathrm{in},2}&{\bm{q}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&{\bm{f}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&{\bm{p}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}&f_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&{\bm{f}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+2}&w_{p,\mathrm{in}}&w_{q,\mathrm{in}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Q}}_{\mathrm{in},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{in},1}^{t+1}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{t+1}&\bm{{\mathsf{P}}}_{\mathrm{in}}^{t+2}&{\mathsf{F}}_{p,\mathrm{in},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{p,\mathrm{in},1}^{t+2}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{in}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{q}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&f_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&{\bm{f}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}&p_{\mathrm{out},2}&{\bm{p}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+2}&{\bm{f}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}&w_{p,\mathrm{out}}&w_{q,\mathrm{out}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{W}\begin{bmatrix}\bm{{\mathsf{Q}}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{F}}_{q,\mathrm{out},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{q,\mathrm{out},1}^{t+1}&{\mathsf{P}}_{\mathrm{out},2}&\bm{{\mathsf{P}}}_{\mathrm{out},1}^{t+2}&\bm{{\mathsf{F}}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{t+2}&{\mathsf{W}}_{p,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{W}}_{q,\mathrm{out}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

which finally completes the whole induction argument.
∎

G.2
Proof of
Theorem

G.2

We perturb the random matrices
\Phi_{t}(X^{\top}X),\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}(X),\Psi_{t}(XX^{\top}),\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(X)
and the denoising functions
f_{t+1},g_{t+1}
in the original GVAMP iteration
Equation

4.19
and show that the perturbed version satisfies
Items

(A30)
,
(A27)
and
(A29)
so that
Theorem

G.3
is applicable.
We then show that these two iterations and also their associated state evolution parameters are close to each other and conclude the proof by sending the perturbation to zero.

Fix a small constant
\zeta>0
and an arbitrary integer
\Gamma\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
.
Let
M_{t}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\GOE(d)
,
N_{t}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\GOE(n)
,
\widetilde{M}_{t}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\Gin(n,d)
,
\widetilde{N}_{t}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\Gin(n,d)
,
s^{t}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}{\mathcal{N}}(0_{d},I_{d})
,
q^{t}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}{\mathcal{N}}(0_{n},I_{n})
(for all
t\in\left\{0,1,\cdots,\Gamma+1\right\}
) be independent of each other and of everything else.
3
3

3

Slightly abuse terminology in
Section

D.2.1
, we denote by
\Gin(n,d)
the (rectangular) Ginibre ensemble which is the probability measure on
n\times d
real rectangular matrices
M
such that
M_{i,j}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}{\mathcal{N}}(0,1/d)
for all
(i,j)\in[n]\times[d]
.

Define the augmented side information matrices
\underline{\Theta}=\begin{bmatrix}\Theta&s^{0}&\cdots&s^{\Gamma+1}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times(d^{\prime}+\Gamma+2)}
and
\underline{\Xi}=\begin{bmatrix}\Xi&q^{0}&\cdots&q^{\Gamma+1}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times(n^{\prime}+\Gamma+2)}
.
Let the perturbed initializers be given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{r}_{\zeta}^{0}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{r}^{0}+\zeta s^{0},

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}_{\zeta}^{0}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{p}^{0}+\zeta q^{0},

(G.118)

where
\widetilde{r}^{0},\widetilde{p}^{0}
are the initializers of
Equation

4.19
.
Defining

\displaystyle f_{0}^{\zeta}(b,\theta,\begin{bmatrix}s_{0}&\cdots&s_{\Gamma+1}\end{bmatrix})

\displaystyle=f_{0}(b,\theta)+\zeta s_{0},

\displaystyle g_{0}^{\zeta}(z,e,\xi,\begin{bmatrix}q_{0}&\cdots&q_{\Gamma+1}\end{bmatrix})

\displaystyle=g_{0}(z,e,\xi)+\zeta q_{0},

it is clear that the perturbed initializers satisfy
Item

(A28)
in which the Wasserstein limits of
\widetilde{r}_{\zeta}^{0},\widetilde{p}_{\zeta}^{0}
are given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}^{\zeta}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}+\zeta{\mathsf{S}}_{0},

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}^{\zeta}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}+\zeta{\mathsf{Q}}_{0},

(G.119)

where
{\mathsf{S}}_{0},{\mathsf{Q}}_{0}
are i.i.d. standard Gaussians independent of
{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{Z}},{\mathsf{E}},{\mathsf{\Theta}},{\mathsf{\Xi}}
.
For each
0\leq t\leq\Gamma
, define the perturbed denoising functions
f^{\zeta}_{t+1}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{2+d^{\prime}+\Gamma+2}\to{\mathbb{R}}
and
g^{\zeta}_{t+1}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{3+n^{\prime}+\Gamma+2}\to{\mathbb{R}}
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}f^{\zeta}_{t+1}(r_{\zeta};b,\begin{bmatrix}\theta&s_{0}&\cdots&s_{\Gamma+1}\end{bmatrix})&=f_{t+1}(r_{\zeta};b,\theta)+\zeta s_{t+1}-\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t+1}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{t+1}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]r_{\zeta},\\
g^{\zeta}_{t+1}(p_{\zeta};z,e,\begin{bmatrix}\xi&q_{0}&\cdots&q_{\Gamma+1}\end{bmatrix})&=g_{t+1}(p_{\zeta};z,e,\xi)+\zeta q_{t+1}-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{t+1}^{\prime}({\mathsf{P}}_{t+1}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{Z}},{\mathsf{E}},{\mathsf{\Xi}})\right]p_{\zeta},\end{split}

(G.120)

where
r_{\zeta},p_{\zeta},b,z,e,s_{0},\cdots,s_{\Gamma+1},q_{0},\cdots,q_{\Gamma+1}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d^{\prime}}
,
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n^{\prime}}
, and
f_{t+1},g_{t+1}
are the denoising functions in
Equation

4.19
.
The random variables
{\mathsf{R}}_{t+1}^{\zeta},{\mathsf{P}}_{t+1}^{\zeta}
are given in
Equation

G.126
below.
In particular, the negative correction terms in the definition
Equation

G.120
ensures that the divergence-free condition required in
Item

(A8)
is satisfied by
f_{t+1}^{\zeta},g_{t+1}^{\zeta}
when the expectation of their derivatives with respect to
r_{\zeta},p_{\zeta}
are evaluated using the laws of
{\mathsf{R}}_{t+1}^{\zeta},{\mathsf{P}}_{t+1}^{\zeta}
, respectively.
Since
f_{t+1},g_{t+1}
satisfy
Item

(A10)
,
f_{t+1}^{\zeta},g_{t+1}^{\zeta}
satisfy
Item

(A27)
.
For any
\beta_{0},\cdots,\beta_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, suppose

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t+1}^{\zeta}-\widetilde{b}_{t+1}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\beta_{s}\left(\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{s}^{\zeta}-\widetilde{b}_{s}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right).

(G.121)

Rearranging terms, this is equivalent to

\displaystyle\zeta{\mathsf{S}}_{t+1}

\displaystyle=-f_{t+1}({\mathsf{R}}_{t+1}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})+\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t+1}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{t+1}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]+\widetilde{b}_{t+1}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}+\sum_{s=0}^{t}\beta_{s}\left(\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{s}^{\zeta}-\widetilde{b}_{s}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right).

(G.122)

Since
\zeta>0
and
{\mathsf{S}}_{t+1}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
is independent of all terms on the right of
Equation

G.122
,
Equation

G.121
cannot hold almost surely.
A similar argument applies to
\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t+1}^{\zeta}
.
Therefore
f_{t+1}^{\zeta},g_{t+1}^{\zeta}
satisfy
Item

(A30)
.

Using the above quantities, we construct a perturbed GVAMP iteration.
Recall from
Item

(A2)
that
X=O\Lambda Q^{\top}
.
Initialized with
\widetilde{r}_{\zeta}^{0},\widetilde{p}_{\zeta}^{0}
in
Equation

G.118
, for every
0\leq t\leq\Gamma
, the perturbed GVAMP iterates are updated as follows:

\displaystyle r_{\zeta}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=Q(\Phi_{t}(\Lambda^{\top}\Lambda)+\zeta M_{t})Q^{\top}\widetilde{r}_{\zeta}^{t}+Q(\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}(\Lambda)+\zeta\widetilde{M}_{t})^{\top}O^{\top}\widetilde{p}_{\zeta}^{t},

\displaystyle\widetilde{r}_{\zeta}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=f^{\zeta}_{t+1}(r_{\zeta}^{t+1};\beta_{*},\underline{\Theta}),

(G.123a)

\displaystyle p_{\zeta}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=O(\Psi_{t}(\Lambda\Lambda^{\top})+\zeta N_{t})O^{\top}\widetilde{p}_{\zeta}^{t}+O(\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(\Lambda)+\zeta\widetilde{N}_{t})Q^{\top}\widetilde{r}_{\zeta}^{t},

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}_{\zeta}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=g^{\zeta}_{t+1}(p_{\zeta}^{t+1};z,\varepsilon,\underline{\Xi}).

(G.123b)

Let
m_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},n_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be the vectors of eigenvalues of
M_{t},N_{t}
, respectively.
Let
\widetilde{m}_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\widetilde{n}_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be the vectors of singular values of
\widetilde{M}_{t},\widetilde{N}_{t}
, respectively, appended with zeros if needed, as in
Equation

G.6
.
Then we identify

\displaystyle\phi^{\zeta}_{t}

\displaystyle=\Phi_{t}(\lambda_{d}\circ\lambda_{d})+\zeta m_{t},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}^{\zeta}_{t}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}(\lambda_{d})+\zeta\widetilde{m}_{t},

\displaystyle\psi^{\zeta}_{t}

\displaystyle=\Psi_{t}(\lambda_{n}\circ\lambda_{n})+\zeta n_{t},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}^{\zeta}_{t}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(\lambda_{n})+\zeta\widetilde{n}_{t}.

(G.124)

Using orthogonal invariance and the convergence of empirical spectral distributions of GOE and the Ginibre ensemble,
Item

(A7)
and
Proposition

K.2
, we obtain
Equation

G.7
in which the limiting random variables are given by

\displaystyle{\mathsf{\Phi}}^{\zeta}_{t}

\displaystyle=\Phi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})+\zeta{\mathsf{M}}_{t},

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}^{\zeta}_{t}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})+\zeta\widetilde{{\mathsf{M}}}_{t},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{\zeta}_{t}

\displaystyle=\Psi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})+\zeta{\mathsf{N}}_{t},

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{\zeta}_{t}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})+\zeta\widetilde{{\mathsf{N}}}_{t},

(G.125)

where
{\mathsf{M}}_{t},{\mathsf{N}}_{t}
follow the semicircle distribution,
\widetilde{{\mathsf{M}}}_{t},\widetilde{{\mathsf{N}}}_{t}
follow the (square root pushforward of) Marchenko–Pastur distribution with aspect ration
\delta
, moreover, they are independent of each other and of
{\mathsf{\Lambda}}
for all
t\geq 0
.
Since the semicircle distribution has mean zero, so do
{\mathsf{\Phi}}^{\zeta}_{t},{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{\zeta}_{t}
.
Since
\zeta>0
and the variances of
{\mathsf{M}}_{t},\widetilde{{\mathsf{M}}}_{t},{\mathsf{N}}_{t},\widetilde{{\mathsf{N}}}_{t}
are all positive, it holds that
\min\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t}^{\zeta})^{2}\right],\mathrm{Var}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t}^{\zeta}\right]\right\}>0
,
\min\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{\Psi}}_{t}^{\zeta})^{2}\right],\mathrm{Var}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{t}^{\zeta}\right]\right\}>0
.
Therefore,
Item

(A29)
is satisfied by the vectors in
Equation

G.124
and the associated random variables in
Equation

G.125
.

Now
Theorem

G.3
is applicable to the perturbed GVAMP iteration
Equation

G.123
.
Its state evolution parameters

\displaystyle a_{t+1}^{\zeta},

\displaystyle b_{t+1}^{\zeta},

\displaystyle\widetilde{a}^{\zeta}_{t},

\displaystyle\widetilde{b}^{\zeta}_{t},

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}^{\zeta}_{t+1},

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}^{\zeta}_{t+1},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{J}}^{\zeta}_{t+1},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{K}}^{\zeta}_{t+1},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{R}}_{t+1}^{\zeta},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{P}}_{t+1}^{\zeta},

\displaystyle\forall 0\leq t\leq\Gamma,

(G.126)

can be defined through
Equation

4.29
with the modification that
\Phi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}),\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}),\Psi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}),\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})
,
f_{t+1}({\mathsf{R}}_{t+1};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})
,
g_{t+1}({\mathsf{P}}_{t+1};{\mathsf{Z}},{\mathsf{E}},{\mathsf{\Xi}})
are replaced with the perturbed versions
{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t}^{\zeta},\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t}^{\zeta},{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{t}^{\zeta},\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{t}^{\zeta}
,
f_{t+1}^{\zeta}({\mathsf{R}}_{t+1}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},\underline{{\mathsf{\Theta}}})
,
g_{t+1}^{\zeta}({\mathsf{P}}_{t+1}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{Z}},{\mathsf{E}},\underline{{\mathsf{\Xi}}})
introduced in
Equations

G.120
and
G.125
.

We will inductively show the following statements.
For every
t\geq 0
and every
1\leq r,s\leq t
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{d\to\infty}d^{-1}\left\|r^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle<\infty,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|p^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle<\infty,

(G.127)

\displaystyle\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\limsup_{d\to\infty}d^{-1}\left\|r^{t}-r_{\zeta}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\limsup_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|p^{t}-p_{\zeta}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=0,

(G.128)

\displaystyle\lim_{\zeta\to 0}a_{t}^{\zeta}

\displaystyle=a_{t},

\displaystyle\lim_{\zeta\to 0}b_{t}^{\zeta}

\displaystyle=b_{t},

(G.129)

\displaystyle\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{r}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{J}}_{s}^{\zeta}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{r}{\mathsf{J}}_{s}\right],

\displaystyle\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{r}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{K}}_{s}^{\zeta}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{r}{\mathsf{K}}_{s}\right],

(G.130)

\displaystyle\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{t}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\mathbb{E}\left[g_{t}^{\prime}({\mathsf{P}}_{t}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{Z}},{\mathsf{E}},{\mathsf{\Xi}})\right]

\displaystyle=0,

(G.131)

\displaystyle\limsup_{d\to\infty}d^{-1}\left\|\widetilde{r}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle<\infty,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|\widetilde{p}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle<\infty,

(G.132)

\displaystyle\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\limsup_{d\to\infty}d^{-1}\left\|\widetilde{r}^{t}-\widetilde{r}_{\zeta}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\limsup_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|\widetilde{p}^{t}-\widetilde{p}_{\zeta}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=0,

(G.133)

\displaystyle\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\widetilde{a}_{t}^{\zeta}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{a}_{t},

\displaystyle\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\widetilde{b}_{t}^{\zeta}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{b}_{t},

(G.134)

where
Equations

G.127
,
G.128
,
G.131
,
G.129
and
G.130
are vacuous for
t=0
.
Furthermore, for every
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}r^{1}&\cdots&r^{t}&\widetilde{r}^{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{r}^{t}&\beta_{*}&\Theta\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W_{2}}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{R}}_{1}&\cdots&{\mathsf{R}}_{t}&\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t}&{\mathsf{B}}_{*}&{\mathsf{\Theta}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

(G.135)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}p^{1}&\cdots&p^{t}&\widetilde{p}^{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{p}^{t}&z&\varepsilon&\Xi\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W_{2}}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{P}}_{1}&\cdots&{\mathsf{P}}_{t}&\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t}&{\mathsf{Z}}&{\mathsf{E}}&{\mathsf{\Xi}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

(G.136)

where for
t=0
, these are just
\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{r}^{0}&\beta_{*}&\Theta\end{bmatrix}\xrightarrow{W_{2}}\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}&{\mathsf{B}}_{*}&{\mathsf{\Theta}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}
,
\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{p}^{0}&z&\varepsilon&\Xi\end{bmatrix}\xrightarrow{W_{2}}\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}&{\mathsf{Z}}&{\mathsf{E}}&{\mathsf{\Xi}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}
.
This will imply
Theorem

G.2
.

Base case.

For
t=0
, the Wasserstein convergence in
Equation

G.135
is given in
Item

(A28)
.
It also implies
d^{-1}\left\|\widetilde{r}^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}\to\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}^{2}\right]<\infty
almost surely.
By the choice of the perturbed initializer
Equation

G.118
,
d^{-1}\left\|\widetilde{r}_{\zeta}^{0}-\widetilde{r}^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\zeta^{2}d^{-1}\left\|s^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}\to 0
almost surely as
d\to\infty,\zeta\to 0
in that order.
According to the definition
Equation

4.29e
and the identification of
\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}^{\zeta}
in
Equation

G.119
,
\widetilde{b}_{0}^{\zeta}=\rho^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}+\zeta{\mathsf{S}}_{0}){\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right]=\rho^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right]=\widetilde{b}_{0}
.
The same arguments apply to
\widetilde{p}^{0},\widetilde{p}^{0}_{\zeta},\widetilde{a}_{0}^{\zeta}
.
This justifies
Equations

G.132
,
G.133
and
G.134
.

Induction step.

Assuming that
Equations

G.127
,
G.128
,
G.132
,
G.133
,
G.131
,
G.129
and
G.130
hold up to time
t
, we verify their validity for time
t+1
.

Recalling the update rule
Equation

4.19a
and using the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\left\|r^{t+1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Phi_{t}(X^{\top}X)\right\|_{2}\left\|\widetilde{r}^{t}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}(X)\right\|_{2}\left\|\widetilde{p}^{t}\right\|_{2}.

By
Item

(A9)
and the induction hypothesis
Equation

G.132
, we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{d\to\infty}d^{-1}\left\|r^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}<\infty.

(G.137)

The same argument implies
\limsup_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|p^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}<\infty
, thereby establishing
Equation

G.127
for
t+1
.

Comparing the update rules for
r^{t+1},r^{t+1}_{\zeta}
in
Equations

G.123a
and
4.19a
and applying the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\left\|r^{t+1}-r^{t+1}_{\zeta}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Phi_{t}(\Lambda^{\top}\Lambda)\right\|_{2}\left\|\widetilde{r}^{t}-\widetilde{r}^{t}_{\zeta}\right\|_{2}+\zeta\left\|M_{t}\right\|_{2}\left\|\widetilde{r}^{t}_{\zeta}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}(\Lambda)\right\|_{2}\left\|\widetilde{p}^{t}-\widetilde{p}^{t}_{\zeta}\right\|_{2}+\zeta\left\|\widetilde{M}_{t}\right\|_{2}\left\|\widetilde{p}^{t}_{\zeta}\right\|_{2}.

Combining
Item

(A9)
, the induction hypotheses
Equations

G.132
and
G.133
, and the facts
\left\|M_{t}\right\|_{2}\to 2,\left\|\widetilde{M}_{t}\right\|_{2}\to 1+\sqrt{\delta}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\limsup_{d\to\infty}d^{-1}\left\|r^{t+1}-r^{t+1}_{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}<\infty.

(G.138)

An analogous argument shows
\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\limsup_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|p^{t+1}-p^{t+1}_{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}<\infty
, thereby justifying
Equation

G.128
for
t+1
.

By the definition
Equation

4.29f
and the identification of
\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t}
in
Equation

G.125
,

\displaystyle b_{t+1}^{\zeta}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})+\zeta\widetilde{{\mathsf{M}}}_{t}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\widetilde{a}_{t}^{\zeta}=\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\widetilde{a}_{t}^{\zeta}\to b_{t+1},

(G.139)

where the convergence in the
\zeta\to 0
limit is by the induction hypothesis
Equation

G.134
.
A similar argument shows
a_{t+1}^{\zeta}\to a_{t+1}
as
\zeta\to 0
, thereby establishing
Equation

G.129
for
t+1
.

For any
0\leq s\leq t
, recalling
Equation

4.29d
and using the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{s+1}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{K}}_{t+1}^{\zeta}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{s+1}{\mathsf{K}}_{t+1}\right]\right|\\
&\leq\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{s}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t}^{\zeta}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{s}^{\zeta}\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t}^{\zeta}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\Phi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{s}\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t}\right]\right|+\rho\left|b_{s+1}^{\zeta}b_{t+1}^{\zeta}-b_{s+1}b_{t+1}\right|\\
&+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{s}^{\zeta}\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t}^{\zeta}\right]\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{s}^{\zeta}\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t}^{\zeta}\right]-\widetilde{a}_{s}^{\zeta}\widetilde{a}_{t}^{\zeta}\sigma^{2}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})\right]\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{s}\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t}\right]-\widetilde{a}_{s}\widetilde{a}_{t}\sigma^{2}\right)\right|\\
&+\rho\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{s}^{\zeta}\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]\widetilde{a}_{s}^{\zeta}\widetilde{a}_{t}^{\zeta}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]\widetilde{a}_{s}\widetilde{a}_{t}\right|\\
&+\rho\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{s}^{\zeta}\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\widetilde{a}_{s}^{\zeta}\widetilde{b}_{t}^{\zeta}-\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\widetilde{a}_{s}\widetilde{b}_{t}\right|+\rho\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t}^{\zeta}\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{s}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\widetilde{a}_{t}^{\zeta}\widetilde{b}_{s}^{\zeta}-\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\widetilde{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\widetilde{a}_{t}\widetilde{b}_{s}\right|.\end{split}

(G.140)

By
Equation

G.125
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{s}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t}^{\zeta}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\Phi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\zeta^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{M}}_{t}^{2}\right]=0,

where inequality becomes an equality if and only if
s=t
, and the last equality holds since the semicircle distribution has finite variance.
Similarly,

\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{s}^{\zeta}\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t}^{\zeta}\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})\right]
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{s}^{\zeta}\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]
,

\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{s}^{\zeta}\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{t}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]
,

\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Phi}}_{t}^{\zeta}\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Phi}}}_{s}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\widetilde{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]
,
as
\zeta\to 0
.
Moreover, recalling
Equation

G.120
and using the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{s}^{\zeta}\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t}^{\zeta}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{s}\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t}\right]\right|

(G.141)

\displaystyle\leq\left|\mathbb{E}\left[f_{s}(b_{s}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}+{\mathsf{K}}_{s}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})f_{t}(b_{t}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}+{\mathsf{K}}_{t}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[f_{s}(b_{s}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}+{\mathsf{K}}_{s};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})f_{t}(b_{t}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}+{\mathsf{K}}_{t};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\zeta^{2}+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[f_{s}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{s}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]\right|\cdot\left|\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{t}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]\right|\cdot\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{R}}_{s}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{R}}_{t}^{\zeta}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[f_{s}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{s}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]\right|\cdot\left|\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t}({\mathsf{R}}_{t}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}}){\mathsf{R}}_{s}^{\zeta}\right]\right|+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{t}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]\right|\cdot\left|\mathbb{E}\left[f_{s}({\mathsf{R}}_{s}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}}){\mathsf{R}}_{t}^{\zeta}\right]\right|.

By the Lipschitz property of
f_{t}
from
Item

(A10)
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t}(b_{t}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}+{\mathsf{K}}_{t}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})-f_{t}(b_{t}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}+{\mathsf{K}}_{t};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left|f_{t}\left(b_{t}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}+\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{K}}_{t}^{\zeta})^{2}\right]^{1/2}{\mathsf{G}};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}}\right)-f_{t}\left(b_{t}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{t}^{2}\right]^{1/2}{\mathsf{G}};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}}\right)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left[\left|(b_{t}^{\zeta}-b_{t}){\mathsf{B}}_{*}+\left(\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{K}}_{t}^{\zeta})^{2}\right]^{1/2}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{t}^{2}\right]^{1/2}\right){\mathsf{G}}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left|b_{t}^{\zeta}-b_{t}\right|\rho^{1/2}+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{K}}_{t}^{\zeta})^{2}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{t}^{2}\right]\right|^{1/2}\right)\xrightarrow[\zeta\to 0]{}0,

(G.142)

where
{\mathsf{G}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
is independent of
{\mathsf{B}}_{*}
and the last line follows from the induction hypotheses
Equations

G.129
and
G.130
.
Combining
Equation

G.142
and the hypotheses
Equations

G.129
,
G.130
and
G.131
, we have that
Equation

G.141
converges to
0
as
\zeta\to 0
.
Using all the above observations in
Equation

G.140
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{s+1}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{K}}_{t+1}^{\zeta}\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{s+1}{\mathsf{K}}_{t+1}\right].

(G.143)

An analogous argument shows
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{s+1}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{J}}_{t+1}^{\zeta}\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{s+1}{\mathsf{J}}_{t+1}\right]
as
\zeta\to 0
for every
0\leq s\leq t
.
Now
Equation

G.130
is established for
t+1
.

Next, by
Equations

G.139
and
G.143
(with
s=t
) shown above,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t+1}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{t+1}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t+1}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{t+1};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t+1}^{\prime}(b_{t+1}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}+{\mathsf{K}}_{t+1}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t+1}^{\prime}(b_{t+1}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}+{\mathsf{K}}_{t+1};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]\to 0,

as
\zeta\to 0
.
Since
\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t+1}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{t+1};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]=0
by
Item

(A8)
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t+1}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{t+1}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]

\displaystyle=0.

(G.144)

A similar argument allows us to prove the other limit in
Equation

G.131
for
t+1
.

For
\widetilde{r}^{t+1}
, by the Lipschitz property of
f_{t+1}
in
Item

(A10)
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(f_{t+1}(r^{t+1}_{i};\beta_{*,i},\Theta_{i,:})-f_{t+1}(0;\beta_{*,i},\Theta_{i,:})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}\left\|r^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Combining this with
Equations

G.137
and
G.138
shown above, we obtain

\displaystyle\limsup_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|\widetilde{r}^{t+1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\limsup_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left(\left\|f_{t+1}(r^{t+1};\beta_{*},\Theta)\right\|_{2}+\zeta\left\|s^{t+1}\right\|_{2}+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t+1}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{t+1}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]\right|\left\|r^{t+1}_{\zeta}\right\|_{2}\right)<\infty.

(G.145)

The same argument implies
\limsup_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|\widetilde{p}^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}<\infty
, thereby establishing
Equation

G.132
.

Recalling the design of the perturbed denoiser
Equation

G.120
and applying the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{r}^{t+1}-\widetilde{r}^{t+1}_{\zeta}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|f_{t+1}(r^{t_{+}1};\beta_{*},\Theta)-f_{t+1}(r^{t+1}_{\zeta};\beta_{*},\Theta)\right\|_{2}+\zeta\left\|s^{t+1}\right\|_{2}+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t+1}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{t+1}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*};{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]\right|\left\|r^{t+1}_{\zeta}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|r^{t+1}-r^{t+1}_{\zeta}\right\|_{2}+\zeta\left\|s^{t+1}\right\|_{2}+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t+1}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{t+1}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*};{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]\right|\left(\left\|r^{t+1}\right\|_{2}+\left\|r^{t+1}-r^{t+1}_{\zeta}\right\|_{2}\right),

where the last line is by the Lipschitz property of
f_{t+1}
in
Item

(A10)
.
Using
Equations

G.138
,
G.137
and
G.144
shown above,
d^{-1}\left\|s^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}\to 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\limsup_{d\to\infty}d^{-1}\left\|\widetilde{r}^{t+1}-\widetilde{r}^{t+1}_{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}=0.

(G.146)

A similar argument implies
\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\limsup_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|\widetilde{p}^{t+1}-\widetilde{p}^{t+1}_{\zeta}\right\|_{2}^{2}=0
, thereby verifying
Equation

G.133
for
t+1
.

From the definition
Equation

4.29e
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{b}_{t+1}^{\zeta}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t+1}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right]=\rho^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t+1}(b_{t+1}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}+{\mathsf{K}}_{t+1}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}}){\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right]-\rho^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t+1}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{t+1}^{\zeta};{\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{\Theta}})\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{R}}_{t+1}^{\zeta}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right]\to\widetilde{b}_{t+1},

(G.147)

where the convergence follows from
Equations

G.144
,
G.139
and
G.143
.
Similarly,
\widetilde{a}_{t+1}^{\zeta}\to\widetilde{a}_{t+1}
, establishing
Equation

G.134
for
t+1
.

Next, consider
Equation

G.135
for
t+1
.
Denote by
R^{t+1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times(2(t+2)+d^{\prime})}
and
\bm{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t+1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1\times(2(t+2)+d^{\prime})}
the left-/right-hand sides of
Equation

G.135
for
t+1
.
Denote also their perturbed versions by

\displaystyle R^{t+1}_{\zeta}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{bmatrix}r_{\zeta}^{1}&\cdots&r_{\zeta}^{t+1}&\widetilde{r}_{\zeta}^{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{r}_{\zeta}^{t+1}&\beta_{*}&\Theta\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times(2(t+2)+d^{\prime})},

\displaystyle\bm{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t+1}^{\zeta}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{R}}^{\zeta}_{1}&\cdots&{\mathsf{R}}^{\zeta}_{t+1}&\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}^{\zeta}_{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}^{\zeta}_{t+1}&{\mathsf{B}}_{*}&{\mathsf{\Theta}}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1\times(2(t+2)+d^{\prime})}.

For any
h\in\mathrm{PL}_{(2(t+2)+d^{\prime})\to 1}^{2}
, we apply the triangle inequality to bound

\displaystyle\limsup_{d\to\infty}\left|\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}h(R_{i,:}^{t+1})-h(\bm{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t+1})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\limsup_{d\to\infty}\left|\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left[h(R^{t+1}_{i,:})-h((R^{t+1}_{\zeta})_{i,:})\right]\right|

(G.148)

\displaystyle\quad+\limsup_{d\to\infty}\left|\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}h((R^{t+1}_{\zeta})_{i,:})-\mathbb{E}\left[h(\bm{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t+1}^{\zeta})\right]\right|

(G.149)

\displaystyle\quad+\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[h(\bm{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t+1}^{\zeta})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[h(\bm{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t+1})\right]\right|.

(G.150)

Using the pseudo-Lipschitz property of
h
, the triangle inequality and Cauchy–Schwarz, we upper bound the first term in
Equation

G.148
by

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left[h(R^{t+1}_{i,:})-h((R^{t+1}_{\zeta})_{i,:})\right]\right|\leq\frac{C}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(1+\left\|R^{t+1}_{i,:}\right\|_{2}+\left\|(R^{t+1}_{\zeta})_{i,:}\right\|_{2}\right)\left\|R^{t+1}_{i,:}-(R^{t+1}_{\zeta})_{i,:}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\frac{3}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(1+4\left\|R^{t+1}_{i,:}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|R^{t+1}_{i,:}-(R^{t+1}_{\zeta})_{i,:}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)}\sqrt{\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left\|R^{t+1}_{i,:}-(R^{t+1}_{\zeta})_{i,:}\right\|_{2}^{2}},

which vanishes as
d\to\infty,\zeta\to 0
in that order, using the induction hypotheses
Equations

G.127
,
G.128
,
G.132
and
G.133
along with their counterparts
Equations

G.137
,
G.138
,
G.145
and
G.146
for
t+1
shown above.
The second term in
Equation

G.149
equals zero by the Wasserstein convergence result in
Theorem

G.3
applied to
R^{t+1}_{\zeta}
for any fixed
\zeta>0
.
The third term in
Equation

G.150
is zero due to the convergence of perturbed state evolution parameters to the original ones guaranteed by the induction hypotheses
Equations

G.129
,
G.130
,
G.131
and
G.134
together with their counterparts
Equations

G.139
,
G.143
,
G.144
and
G.147
for
t+1
.
Taking
Equations

G.148
,
G.149
and
G.150
collectively, we conclude
Equation

G.135
for
t+1
.
The convergence result
Equation

G.136
for
t+1
follows from a similar argument which is omitted.
This completes the induction and therefore the proof of
Theorem

G.2
.

G.3
Proof of
Theorem

4.3

Fix a large constant
\Gamma\geq 1
.
Consider an auxiliary GVAMP iteration:

\displaystyle\underline{r}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\underline{\Phi}_{t}(X^{\top}X)\widetilde{\underline{r}}^{t}+\widetilde{\underline{\Phi}}_{t}(X)^{\top}\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{t},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\underline{r}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\underline{f}_{t+1}(\underline{r}^{t+1}),

(G.151a)

\displaystyle\underline{p}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\underline{\Psi}_{t}(XX^{\top})\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{t}+\widetilde{\underline{\Psi}}_{t}(X)\widetilde{\underline{r}}^{t},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\underline{g}_{t+1}(\underline{p}^{t+1};y).

(G.151b)

For
0\leq t\leq\Gamma-1
, we choose

\displaystyle\underline{\Phi}_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\underline{\Phi}}_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}x,

\displaystyle\underline{\Psi}_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}x-1,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\underline{\Psi}}_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\underline{f}_{t}(\underline{r})

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\underline{g}_{t}(\underline{p};y)

\displaystyle=\overline{g}(y)\underline{p},

(G.151c)

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\underline{r}^{t+1}&=\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}X^{\top}\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{t},\\
\underline{p}^{t+1}&=\left(\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}XX^{\top}-I_{n}\right)\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{t},\qquad\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{t+1}=\overline{G}\underline{p}^{t+1},\end{split}

(G.151d)

where
\overline{G}=\diag(\overline{g}(y))
.
For
t\geq\Gamma
, we choose

\displaystyle\underline{\Phi}_{t}

\displaystyle=\Phi_{t-\Gamma},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\underline{\Phi}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Phi}_{t-\Gamma},

\displaystyle\underline{\Psi}_{t}

\displaystyle=\Psi_{t-\Gamma},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\underline{\Psi}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Psi}_{t-\Gamma},

(G.151e)

and

\displaystyle\underline{f}_{t}(\underline{r})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}f_{0}(c_{r}\,\underline{r}),&t=\Gamma\\
f_{t-\Gamma}(\underline{r}),&t>\Gamma\end{cases},

\displaystyle\underline{g}_{t}(\underline{p};y)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}g_{0}(c_{p}\,\underline{p};y),&t=\Gamma\\
g_{t-\Gamma}(\underline{p};y),&t>\Gamma\end{cases},

(G.151f)

where the constants
c_{r},c_{p}
are defined in
Equation

4.21
.
We initialize
Equation

G.151d
with

\displaystyle\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{0}=\gamma^{-\Gamma}w_{3}\,\overline{G}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\rho+w_{2}}}z+\underline{j}^{0}\right),

(G.151g)

where
\underline{j}^{0}\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(0_{n},\frac{w_{1}}{\rho+w_{2}}I_{n}\right)
is independent of everything else.
Note that
\widetilde{r}^{0}
is not needed to initialize
Equation

G.151d
.

Lemma

G.4
below offers a state evolution result for the auxiliary GVAMP iteration and is a direct consequence of the general result of
Theorem

G.2
.

Recall
{\mathsf{Z}},{\mathsf{Y}}
from
Equation

3.10
.
Let

\displaystyle\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}}_{0}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{-\Gamma}w_{3}\,\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\rho+w_{2}}}{\mathsf{Z}}+\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{0}\right),

(G.152a)

where
\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{0}\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(0,\frac{w_{1}}{\rho+w_{2}}\right)
is independent of
{\mathsf{Z}},{\mathsf{Y}}
.
Let
(\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{t+1})_{t\geq 0},(\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{t+1})_{t\geq 0}
be two centered Gaussian processes that are independent of each other and of
({\mathsf{B}}_{*},{\mathsf{Z}},{\mathsf{E}},{\mathsf{\Lambda}})
with covariance structures given below.

1.

For
0\leq r,s\leq\Gamma-1
and
1\leq t\leq\Gamma
,

\displaystyle\underline{a}_{t}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{-(\Gamma-t)}\frac{w_{3}}{\sqrt{\rho+w_{2}}},\qquad\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{t}^{2}\right]=\gamma^{-2(\Gamma-t)}\frac{w_{3}^{2}w_{1}}{\rho+w_{2}},

(G.152b)

\displaystyle\underline{b}_{t}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{-(\Gamma-t)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\rho+w_{2}}},\qquad\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{t}^{2}\right]=\gamma^{-2(\Gamma-t)}\frac{w_{2}}{\rho+w_{2}},

(G.152c)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{r+1}\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{s+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{r}\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{s}\right]+\delta\rho\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]+\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]^{2}\right\}\underline{a}_{r}\underline{a}_{s},

(G.152d)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{r+1}\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{s+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}+\delta\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{r}\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{s}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\rho\left\{\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}+\delta\overline{\kappa}_{2}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]+\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{6}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{2}}-\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}^{2}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{3}}+\delta\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{4}}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]^{2}\right\}\underline{a}_{r}\underline{a}_{s}.

(G.152e)

We also define
\underline{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t}=\underline{b}_{t}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}+\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{t},\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t}=\underline{a}_{t}{\mathsf{Z}}+\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{t}
for every
1\leq t\leq\Gamma
, and
\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}}_{t}=\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t},\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{t}=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{g}({\mathsf{Y}})\overline{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{2}\right]\underline{a}_{t}
for every
1\leq t\leq\Gamma-1
.
Note that due to the choice of denoisers in
Equation

G.151c
for the first phase, we do not need to define
\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{R}}}}_{t},\widetilde{\underline{b}}_{t}
for
0\leq t\leq\Gamma-1
.

2.

For
r,s\geq\Gamma
, the correlations
\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{r+1}\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{s+1}\right],\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{r+1}\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{s+1}\right]
together with the coefficients
\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{t},\widetilde{\underline{b}}_{t},\underline{a}_{t+1},\underline{b}_{t+1}
and the random variables
\underline{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t+1},\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t+1},\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{R}}}}_{t},\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}}_{t}
(for
t\geq\Gamma
) formally follow the same recursion
Equation

4.29
where the denoisers in
Equations

G.151e
and
G.151f
are used.

3.

For
0\leq r\leq\Gamma-1<s
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{r+1}\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{s+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{\Psi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}}_{r}\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}}_{s}\right]-2\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{\Psi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{r}\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{s}\rho+\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{\Psi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}\right]\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{r}\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{s}\rho

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[(\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}-1)\widetilde{\underline{\Psi}}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right]\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{r}\widetilde{\underline{b}}_{s}\rho-\underline{a}_{r+1}\underline{a}_{s+1}\sigma^{2},

(G.152f)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{r+1}\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{s+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\underline{\Phi}}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}}_{r}\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}}_{s}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[(\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}-1)\widetilde{\underline{\Phi}}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{r}\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{s}\rho

\displaystyle\quad+\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{\Phi}_{s}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{r}\widetilde{\underline{b}}_{s}\rho-\underline{b}_{r+1}\underline{b}_{s+1}\rho.

(G.152g)

Lemma G.4
.

Consider the auxiliary GVAMP iteration
Equation

G.151
.
Then for any fixed
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\underline{r}^{1}&\cdots&\underline{r}^{t+1}&\widetilde{\underline{r}}^{\Gamma}&\cdots&\widetilde{\underline{r}}^{t+1}&\beta_{*}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W_{2}}\begin{bmatrix}\underline{{\mathsf{R}}}_{1}&\cdots&\underline{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t+1}&\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{R}}}}_{\Gamma}&\cdots&\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{R}}}}_{t+1}&{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\underline{p}^{1}&\cdots&\underline{p}^{t+1}&\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{t+1}&z&\varepsilon\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W_{2}}\begin{bmatrix}\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}_{1}&\cdots&\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t+1}&\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}}_{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}}_{t+1}&{\mathsf{Z}}&{\mathsf{E}}\end{bmatrix},

where the random variables on the right are defined in
Equation

G.152
.

Proof.

The lemma follows directly from the general result of
Theorem

G.2
.
In particular, specializing the state evolution recursion
Equation

4.29
to the first phase
Equation

G.151c
and using the moment-cumulant relation in
Appendix

A
yield
Equations

G.152b
,
G.152c
,
G.152d
and
G.152e
which are consistent with
Equations

C.28
,
C.30
,
C.31
and
C.38
obtained from a reduction to
[
VKM
, Theorem 1]
.
We leave out these tedious calculations but only make the clarification that for
1\leq t\leq\Gamma
, the notation

\displaystyle\underline{p}^{t},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{t},

\displaystyle\underline{a}_{t},

\displaystyle\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{t},

\displaystyle\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t},

\displaystyle\underline{r}^{t},

\displaystyle\underline{b}_{t},

\displaystyle\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{t},

\displaystyle\underline{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t}

in
Lemma

G.4
corresponds to the notation

\displaystyle u^{t},

\displaystyle g^{t},

\displaystyle\nu_{t},

\displaystyle\tau_{t}{\mathsf{N}}_{t},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Z}}_{t},

\displaystyle v^{t},

\displaystyle\chi_{t},

\displaystyle\omega_{t}{\mathsf{M}}_{t},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{t}

in
Lemma

C.1
.
Finally, we explain how to obtain the expressions
Equations

G.152b
and
G.152c
.
Note that the only difference between the initial conditions
Equations

G.151g
and
B.2
is in the additional
\gamma^{-\Gamma}w_{3}
factor in
Equation

G.151g
.
Inspecting the proof of
Lemma

B.1
, this does not change the validity of the following properties of the state evolution parameters: for every
2\leq t\leq\Gamma
,

\displaystyle\frac{\underline{a}_{t}}{\underline{a}_{t-1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\underline{b}_{t}}{\underline{b}_{t-1}}=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{t}^{2}\right]^{1/2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{t-1}^{2}\right]^{1/2}}=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{t}^{2}\right]^{1/2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{t-1}^{2}\right]^{1/2}}=\gamma,

and for every
1\leq t\leq\Gamma
,

\displaystyle\frac{\underline{a}_{t}}{\underline{b}_{t}}

\displaystyle=w_{3},

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{t}^{2}\right]^{1/2}}{\underline{b}_{t}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{w_{2}}.

(G.153)

By induction on
1\leq t\leq\Gamma
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\underline{a}_{t},\underline{b}_{t},\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{t}^{2}\right]^{1/2},\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{t}^{2}\right]^{1/2}\right)=\gamma^{t}\left(\underline{a}_{0},\underline{b}_{0},\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{0}^{2}\right]^{1/2},\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{0}^{2}\right]^{1/2}\right).

(G.154)

According to the initial condition
Equation

G.152a
,

\displaystyle\underline{a}_{0}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{-\Gamma}w_{3}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\rho+w_{2}}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{0}^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{-\Gamma}w_{3}\sqrt{\frac{w_{1}}{\rho+w_{2}}}.

Plugging this in
Equation

G.154
and using
Equation

G.153
, we conclude that
Equations

G.152b
and
G.152c
hold.
∎

Theorem

4.3
immediately follows from combining
Equations

G.159
and
G.160
in the lemma below with
Lemma

G.4
.

Lemma G.5
.

For every
\tau\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\left|\underline{a}_{\Gamma}-\frac{a_{0}}{c_{p}}\right|=\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\left|\underline{a}_{\Gamma+\tau+1}-a_{\tau+1}\right|

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\left|\underline{b}_{\Gamma}-\frac{b_{0}}{c_{r}}\right|=\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\left|\underline{b}_{\Gamma+\tau+1}-a_{\tau+1}\right|

\displaystyle=0,

(G.155)

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\left|\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{\Gamma+\tau}-\widetilde{a}_{\tau}\right|

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\left|\widetilde{\underline{b}}_{\Gamma+\tau}-\widetilde{b}_{\tau}\right|

\displaystyle=0.

(G.156)

For every
\varsigma,\tau\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma}^{2}\right]-\frac{1}{c_{p}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{0}^{2}\right]\right|=\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma}\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma+\tau}\right]-\frac{1}{c_{p}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{0}{\mathsf{J}}_{\tau}\right]\right|=\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma+\varsigma}\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma+\tau}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{\varsigma}{\mathsf{J}}_{\tau}\right]\right|

\displaystyle=0,

(G.157)

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{\Gamma}^{2}\right]-\frac{1}{c_{r}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{0}^{2}\right]\right|=\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{\Gamma}\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{\Gamma+\tau}\right]-\frac{1}{c_{r}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{0}{\mathsf{K}}_{\tau}\right]\right|=\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{\Gamma+\varsigma}\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{\Gamma+\tau}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{\varsigma}{\mathsf{K}}_{\tau}\right]\right|

\displaystyle=0.

(G.158)

For any fixed
\tau\geq 0
and any
\phi_{r}\in\mathrm{PL}^{2}_{2(\tau+1)+1\to 1},\phi_{p}\in\mathrm{PL}^{2}_{2(\tau+2)\to 1}
, almost surely

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\left|\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(\phi_{r}(c_{r}\underline{r}^{\Gamma}_{i},\cdots,\underline{r}^{\Gamma+\tau}_{i},\widetilde{\underline{r}}^{\Gamma}_{i},\cdots,\widetilde{\underline{r}}^{\Gamma+\tau}_{i},\beta_{*,i})-\phi_{r}(r^{0}_{i},\cdots,r^{\tau}_{i},\widetilde{r}^{0}_{i},\cdots,\widetilde{r}^{\tau}_{i},\beta_{*,i})\right)\right|

\displaystyle=0,

(G.159)

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\phi_{p}(c_{p}\underline{p}^{\Gamma}_{i},\cdots,\underline{p}^{\Gamma+\tau}_{i},\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{\Gamma}_{i},\cdots,\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{\Gamma+\tau}_{i},z_{i},\varepsilon_{i})-\phi_{p}(p^{0}_{i},\cdots,p^{\tau}_{i},\widetilde{p}^{0}_{i},\cdots,\widetilde{p}^{\tau}_{i},z_{i},\varepsilon_{i})\right)\right|

\displaystyle=0.

(G.160)

Proof.

The proof is by induction on time.

Base case.

We take time
\Gamma
in the auxiliary GVAMP iteration
Equation

G.151
as the base case.
By
Equation

G.152b
and the definition
Equation

4.21
of
c_{p}
,

\displaystyle c_{p}\,\underline{a}_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{\rho+w_{2}}}{w_{1}w_{3}}\frac{w_{3}}{\sqrt{\rho+w_{2}}}=\frac{1}{w_{1}}\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:a0_b0}}}{=}a_{0}.

(G.161)

It can be shown similarly that

\displaystyle c_{r}\,\underline{b}_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=b_{0}.

(G.162)

This proves the first equalities in
Equation

G.155
.
Again by
Equation

G.152b
,

\displaystyle c_{p}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho+w_{2}}{(w_{1}w_{3})^{2}}\frac{w_{3}^{2}w_{1}}{\rho+w_{2}}=\frac{1}{w_{1}}\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:a0_b0}}}{=}a_{0}.

(G.163)

A similar calculation shows

\displaystyle c_{r}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{\Gamma}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=b_{0},

(G.164)

thereby establishing the first equalities in
Equations

G.157
and
G.158
.
By the design of
\underline{g}_{\Gamma}
in
Equation

G.151f
along with
Equations

G.161
and
G.163
shown above,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{-2}\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{g}_{\Gamma}\left(c_{p}\left(\underline{a}_{\Gamma}{\mathsf{Z}}+\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma}^{2}\right]^{1/2}{\mathsf{G}}\right);{\mathsf{Y}}\right){\mathsf{Z}}\right]=\sigma^{-2}\mathbb{E}\left[g_{0}\left(a_{0}{\mathsf{Z}}+\sqrt{a_{0}}{\mathsf{G}};{\mathsf{Y}}\right){\mathsf{Z}}\right]\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:wta0_wtb0}}}{=}\widetilde{a}_{0},

where
{\mathsf{G}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
independent of
{\mathsf{Z}},{\mathsf{Y}}
.
Similarly,
\widetilde{\underline{b}}_{\Gamma}=\widetilde{b}_{0}
.
Therefore,
Equation

G.156
holds for
\tau=0
.

Let
\phi_{r}\in\mathrm{PL}_{3\to 1}^{2}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(\phi_{r}(c_{r}\underline{r}_{i}^{\Gamma},\widetilde{\underline{r}}_{i}^{\Gamma},\beta_{*,i})-\phi_{r}(r_{i}^{0},\widetilde{r}_{i}^{0},\beta_{*,i})\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(1+\left\|\begin{bmatrix}c_{r}\underline{r}_{i}^{\Gamma}&\widetilde{\underline{r}}_{i}^{\Gamma}&\beta_{*,i}\end{bmatrix}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\begin{bmatrix}r_{i}^{0}&\widetilde{r}_{i}^{0}&\beta_{*,i}\end{bmatrix}\right\|_{2}\right)\left\|\begin{bmatrix}c_{r}\underline{r}_{i}^{\Gamma}&\widetilde{\underline{r}}_{i}^{\Gamma}\end{bmatrix}-\begin{bmatrix}r_{i}^{0}&\widetilde{r}_{i}^{0}\end{bmatrix}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{3}\left[1+d^{-1}\left(\left\|c_{r}\underline{r}^{\Gamma}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\widetilde{\underline{r}}^{\Gamma}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|r^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\widetilde{r}^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\left\|\beta_{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right]^{1/2}\left[d^{-1}\left(\left\|c_{r}\underline{r}^{\Gamma}-r^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\widetilde{\underline{r}}^{\Gamma}-\widetilde{r}^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right]^{1/2},

(G.165)

where the last line is by Cauchy–Schwarz.
Inspecting the proof of
Lemma

B.3
, we find that despite of an additional multiplicative factor
\gamma^{-\Gamma}w_{3}
in the initializer
Equation

G.151g
, the following result still holds:

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left|\left\langle\underline{r}^{\Gamma},v_{1}(D)\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\underline{r}^{\Gamma}\right\|_{2}}

\displaystyle=1,

almost surely.
Moreover, due to the rescaling of the initializer
Equation

G.151g
, it holds almost surely that for any fixed
\Gamma
,

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}d^{-1}\left\|\underline{r}^{\Gamma}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\underline{b}_{\Gamma}^{2}\rho+\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}_{\Gamma}^{2}\right]\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:art_first2}}}{=}\frac{1}{\rho+w_{2}}\rho+\frac{w_{2}}{\rho+w_{2}}=1.

(G.166)

Combining the above two results, we have that almost surely

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}d^{-1}\left\|\underline{r}^{\Gamma}-v_{1}(D)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=0.

(G.167)

In view of the choice of the initializer
Equation

4.20
for the original GVAMP, this is equivalent to

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}d^{-1}\left\|c_{r}\,\underline{r}^{\Gamma}-r^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=0,\qquad\textnormal{a.s.}

(G.168)

By the choice of
\underline{f}_{\Gamma}
in
Equation

G.151f
and its Lipschitz property guaranteed by
Item

(A10)
,
Equation

G.168
in turn immediately implies

\displaystyle d^{-1/2}\left\|\widetilde{\underline{r}}^{\Gamma}-\widetilde{r}^{0}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=d^{-1/2}\left\|f_{0}(c_{r}\,\underline{r}^{\Gamma})-f_{0}(r^{0})\right\|_{2}\leq Cd^{-1/2}\left\|c_{r}\,\underline{r}^{\Gamma}-r^{0}\right\|_{2}\to 0,

(G.169)

almost surely in the limit
d\to\infty
followed by
\Gamma\to\infty
.
The state evolution result in
Lemma

G.4
implies for any fixed
\Gamma
, almost surely,

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}d^{-1}\left\|\underline{\widetilde{r}}^{\Gamma}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{f}_{\Gamma}\left(c_{r}\left(\underline{b}_{\Gamma}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}+\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{K}}}^{2}_{\Gamma}\right]^{1/2}{\mathsf{G}}\right)\right)^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[f_{0}\left(b_{0}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}+\sqrt{b_{0}}{\mathsf{G}}\right)^{2}\right]<\infty,

(G.170)

where
{\mathsf{G}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
is independent of
{\mathsf{B}}_{*}
and the last equality follows from
Equations

G.162
and
G.164
.
Since the right-hand side above does not depend on
\Gamma
, finiteness holds uniformly over
\Gamma
.
Combining this with
Equation

G.169
, we have
\lim_{d\to\infty}d^{-1}\left\|\widetilde{r}^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}<\infty
.
Combining
Equations

G.166
and
G.168
, we also have
\lim_{d\to\infty}d^{-1}\left\|r^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}<\infty
.
Using all the above results together with
Item

(A1)
in
Equation

G.165
shows
Equation

G.159
for
\tau=0
.

Let
\phi_{p}\in\mathrm{PL}_{4\to 1}^{2}
.
Similarly to
Equation

G.165
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\phi_{p}(c_{p}\underline{p}^{\Gamma}_{i},\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{\Gamma}_{i},z_{i},\varepsilon_{i})-\phi_{p}(p^{0}_{i},\widetilde{p}^{0}_{i},z_{i},\varepsilon_{i})\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{3}\left[1+n^{-1}\left(\left\|c_{p}\underline{p}^{\Gamma}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{\Gamma}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|p^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\widetilde{p}^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\left\|z\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\left\|\varepsilon\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right]^{1/2}\left[1+n^{-1}\left(\left\|c_{p}\underline{p}^{\Gamma}-p^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{\Gamma}-\widetilde{p}^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right]^{1/2}.

(G.171)

We examine each term on the right individually.
First consider
n^{-1}\left\|c_{p}\underline{p}^{\Gamma}-p^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}
.
From the update rule
Equation

G.151d
in the first phase of the auxiliary GVAMP, we have

\displaystyle\underline{p}^{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=(\overline{\kappa}_{2}^{-1}XX^{\top}-I_{n})\overline{G}\underline{p}^{\Gamma-1}=X\underline{r}^{\Gamma}-\overline{G}\underline{p}^{\Gamma-1},

or equivalently,

\displaystyle\underline{p}^{\Gamma}+\overline{G}\underline{p}^{\Gamma-1}

\displaystyle=X\underline{r}^{\Gamma}.

(G.172)

We claim

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|\underline{p}^{\Gamma}-\gamma\underline{p}^{\Gamma-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=0.

(G.173)

To show this, by the state evolution result in
Lemma

G.4
, almost surely,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|\underline{p}^{\Gamma}-\gamma\underline{p}^{\Gamma-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\underline{a}_{\Gamma}^{2}\sigma^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma}^{2}\right]+\gamma^{2}\underline{a}_{\Gamma}^{2}\sigma^{2}+\gamma^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma-1}^{2}\right]-2\gamma\left(\underline{a}_{\Gamma}\underline{a}_{\Gamma-1}\sigma^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma}\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma-1}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:art_first1}}}{=}2\gamma^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma-1}^{2}\right]-2\gamma\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma}\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma-1}\right].

(G.174)

It was shown in
Equation

E.19
that

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma}\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma-1}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma}^{2}\right]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma-1}^{2}\right]^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:art_first1}}}{=}\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma}\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma-1}\right]}{\gamma\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma-1}^{2}\right]}=1.

(G.175)

Note that
Equation

E.19
was established under the initialization scheme
Equation

B.2
.
Nevertheless, it is easily checked that
Equation

G.175
continues to hold under the initialization scheme
Equation

G.151g
which differs from
Equation

B.2
by a multiplicative factor
\gamma^{-\Gamma}w_{3}
.
Using
Equation

G.175
in
Equation

G.174
allows us to conclude
Equation

G.173
.
Now combining
Equations

G.173
,
G.172
and
G.167
gives

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|(I_{n}+\gamma^{-1}\overline{G})\underline{p}^{\Gamma}-Xv_{1}(D)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=0.

In view of the definition of
p^{0}
in
Equation

4.20
, this implies

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|c_{p}\,\underline{p}^{\Gamma}-p^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=0.

(G.176)

By the state evolution result in
Lemma

G.4
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|\underline{p}^{\Gamma}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\underline{a}_{\Gamma}^{2}\sigma^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma}^{2}\right]\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:art_first1}}}{=}\frac{w_{3}^{2}(\sigma^{2}+w_{1})}{\rho+w_{2}}<\infty.

(G.177)

Moreover, finiteness holds uniformly in
\Gamma
and therefore also in the
\Gamma\to\infty
limit.

Equations

G.176
and
G.177
jointly imply
\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|p^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}<\infty
.
They also imply, by similar reasoning leading to
Equations

G.169
and
G.170
, that

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{\Gamma}-\widetilde{p}^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{\Gamma}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle<\infty.

(G.178)

As a consequence, we further have
\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|\widetilde{p}^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}<\infty
.
Furthermore, by
Items

(A1)
,
(A2)
and
(A3)
,

\displaystyle n^{-1}\left\|z\right\|_{2}^{2}\to\overline{\kappa}_{2}\rho

\displaystyle<\infty,

\displaystyle n^{-1}\left\|\varepsilon\right\|_{2}^{2}\to\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{E}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle<\infty,

(G.179)

almost surely as
n\to\infty
.
Collecting
Equations

G.178
,
G.177
,
G.176
and
G.179
, returning to
Equation

G.171
, we conclude
Equation

G.160
for
\tau=0
.

Induction step.

Now fix any
t\geq 1
.
We put the following statements as induction hypotheses:
Equation

G.155
for every
0\leq\tau\leq t-1
,
Equation

G.156
for every
0\leq\tau\leq t
,
Equations

G.157
and
G.158
for every
1\leq\varsigma,\tau\leq t
,
Equations

G.159
and
G.160
for every
0\leq\tau\leq t
.
The remainder of the proof amounts to verifying the analogous statements for
t+1
.

First consider
\underline{a}_{\Gamma+t+1}
:

\displaystyle\overline{\kappa}_{2}\left|\underline{a}_{\Gamma+t+1}-a_{t+1}\right|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:ab}}}{=}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{\Psi}_{\Gamma+t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{\Gamma+t}-\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\widetilde{a}_{t}+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\underline{\Psi}}_{\Gamma+t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right]\widetilde{\underline{b}}_{\Gamma+t}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right]\widetilde{b}_{t}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\right|\cdot\left|\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{\Gamma+t}-\widetilde{a}_{t}\right|+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right]\right|\cdot\left|\widetilde{\underline{b}}_{\Gamma+t}-\widetilde{a}_{t}\right|\to 0,

(G.180)

as
\Gamma\to\infty
, where the last line uses
the choice of
\underline{\Psi}_{\Gamma+t},\widetilde{\underline{\Psi}}_{\Gamma+t}
in
Equation

G.151e
,
the triangle inequality,
and the induction hypotheses for
\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{\Gamma+t},\widetilde{\underline{b}}_{\Gamma+t}
in
Equation

G.156
.
A similar argument proves
\left|\underline{b}_{\Gamma+t+1}-b_{t+1}\right|\to 0
as
\Gamma\to\infty
, thereby establishing
Equation

G.155
for
t+1
.

Then, for any
0\leq\tau\leq t
, we consider
\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma+t+1}\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma+\tau+1}\right]
:

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma+t+1}\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma+\tau+1}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{t+1}{\mathsf{J}}_{\tau+1}\right]\right|

(G.181)

\displaystyle\overset{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:J_cov}}{\leq}\left|\Psi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\Psi_{\tau}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\right|\left(\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}}_{\Gamma+t}\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}}_{\Gamma+\tau}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t}\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{\tau}\right]\right|+\sigma^{2}\left|\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{\Gamma+t}\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{\Gamma+\tau}-\widetilde{a}_{t}\widetilde{a}_{\tau}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\Psi_{\tau}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\right|\cdot\rho\cdot\left|\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{\Gamma+t}\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{\Gamma+\tau}-\widetilde{a}_{t}\widetilde{a}_{\tau}\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})\widetilde{\Psi}_{\tau}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})\right]\right|\cdot\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{R}}}}_{\Gamma+t}\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{R}}}}_{\Gamma+\tau}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t}\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{\tau}\right]\right|+\sigma^{2}\left|\underline{a}_{\Gamma+t+1}\underline{a}_{\Gamma+\tau+1}-a_{t+1}a_{\tau+1}\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\rho\left|\Psi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\widetilde{\Psi}_{\tau}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right|\left|\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{\Gamma+t}\widetilde{\underline{b}}_{\Gamma+\tau}-\widetilde{a}_{t}\widetilde{b}_{\tau}\right|+\rho\left|\Psi_{\tau}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right|\left|\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{\Gamma+\tau}\widetilde{\underline{b}}_{\Gamma+t}-\widetilde{a}_{\tau}\widetilde{b}_{t}\right|.

Recalling the definition
Equation

G.151f
and using the induction hypotheses
Equations

G.155
and
G.157
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}}_{\Gamma+t}\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}}_{\Gamma+\tau}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t}\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{\tau}\right]\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}\left[g_{t}(\underline{a}_{\Gamma+t}{\mathsf{Z}}+\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma+t};{\mathsf{Y}})g_{\tau}(\underline{a}_{\Gamma+\tau}{\mathsf{Z}}+\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma+\tau};{\mathsf{Y}})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{t}(a_{t}{\mathsf{Z}}+{\mathsf{J}}_{t};{\mathsf{Y}})g_{\tau}(a_{\tau}{\mathsf{Z}}+{\mathsf{J}}_{\tau};{\mathsf{Y}})\right]\right|\to 0,

as
\Gamma\to\infty
.
Similarly,
\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{R}}}}_{\Gamma+t}\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{R}}}}_{\Gamma+\tau}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t}\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{\tau}\right]\xrightarrow[\Gamma\to\infty]{}0
.
Using the above observations and the induction hypotheses
Equations

G.155
,
G.156
and
G.157
, we have that
Equation

G.181
converges to
0
as
\Gamma\to\infty
.
A similar argument can be used to show
\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma}\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma+t+1}\right]-c_{p}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{0}{\mathsf{J}}_{t+1}\right]\right|\xrightarrow[\Gamma\to\infty]{}0
, thereby verifying
Equation

G.157
for
t+1
.
The verification o
Equation

G.158
for
t+1
is analogous and omitted.

Next, consider
\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{\Gamma+t+1}
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\underline{a}}_{\Gamma+t+1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:ab_tilde}}}{=}\sigma^{-2}\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{g}_{\Gamma+t+1}(\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}_{\Gamma+t+1};{\mathsf{Y}}){\mathsf{Z}}\right]=\sigma^{-2}\mathbb{E}\left[g_{t+1}(\underline{a}_{\Gamma+t+1}{\mathsf{Z}}+\underline{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\Gamma+t+1};{\mathsf{Y}})\right]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\Gamma\to\infty]{}\sigma^{-2}\mathbb{E}\left[g_{t+1}(a_{t+1}{\mathsf{Z}}+{\mathsf{J}}_{t+1};{\mathsf{Y}})\right]=\widetilde{a}_{t+1},

where the convergence follows from
Equations

G.180
and
G.181
shown above.
A similar argument shows
\widetilde{\underline{b}}_{\Gamma+t+1}\to\widetilde{\underline{b}}_{t+1}
as
\Gamma\to\infty
, thereby justifying
Equation

G.156
for
t+1
.

Finally, we aim to show
Equation

G.160
for
t+1
.
Denote

\displaystyle\begin{split}\underline{P}^{\Gamma+t+1}&\coloneqq\begin{bmatrix}c_{p}\underline{p}^{\Gamma}&\underline{p}^{\Gamma+1}&\cdots&\underline{p}^{\Gamma+t+1}&\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{\Gamma}&\cdots&\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{\Gamma+t+1}&z&\varepsilon\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times 2(t+3)},\\
P^{t+1}&\coloneqq\begin{bmatrix}p^{0}&p^{1}&\cdots&p^{t+1}&\widetilde{p}^{0}&\cdots&\widetilde{p}^{t+1}&z&\varepsilon\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times 2(t+3)}.\end{split}

(G.182)

Writing
\phi\equiv\phi_{p}
for simplicity, using the pseudo-Lipschitz property of
\phi
and applying Cauchy–Schwarz, we bound the left-hand side of
Equation

G.160
(for
t+1
) as follows:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi(\underline{P}^{\Gamma+t+1}_{i,:})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi(P^{t+1}_{i,:})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\frac{3}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1+\left\|\underline{P}^{\Gamma+t+1}_{i,:}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|P^{t+1}_{i,:}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\underline{P}^{\Gamma+t+1}_{i,:}-P^{t+1}_{i,:}\right\|_{2}^{2}}.

(G.183)

By definition
Equation

G.182
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\underline{P}^{\Gamma+t+1}_{i,:}-P^{t+1}_{i,:}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left(\left\|c_{p}\underline{p}^{\Gamma}-p^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{t+1}\left\|\underline{p}^{\Gamma+i}-p^{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{i=0}^{t+1}\left\|\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{\Gamma+i}-\widetilde{p}^{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right).

(G.184)

The induction hypothesis
Equation

G.160
implies that all terms on the right above converge to
0
as in the limit
n\to\infty
followed by
\Gamma\to\infty
, except two terms
n^{-1}\left\|\underline{p}^{\Gamma+t+1}-p^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}
,
n^{-1}\left\|\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{\Gamma+t+1}-\widetilde{p}^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}
which we separately examine below.
According to the update rules
Equations

G.151b
and
4.19b
, and the choice of denoisers in
Equations

G.151e
and
G.151f
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\underline{p}^{\Gamma+t+1}-p^{t+1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\Psi_{t}(XX^{\top})\right\|_{2}\left\|g_{t}(\underline{p}^{\Gamma+t};y)-g_{t}(p^{t};y)\right\|_{2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(X)\right\|_{2}\left\|f_{t}(\underline{r}^{\Gamma+t})-f_{t}(r^{t})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\Psi_{t}(XX^{\top})\right\|_{2}\left\|\underline{p}^{\Gamma+t}-p^{t}\right\|_{2}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(X)\right\|_{2}\left\|\underline{r}^{\Gamma+t}-r^{t}\right\|_{2},

where the triangle inequality and the Lipschitz property of
f_{t},g_{t}
(see
Item

(A10)
) have been used.
Then
Item

(A9)
and the induction hypotheses
Equations

G.159
and
G.160
ensure that

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\left\|\underline{p}^{\Gamma+t+1}-p^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=0.

(G.185)

By the choice of the denoisers
Equation

G.151f
and the Lipschitz property of
f_{t+1},g_{t+1}
, this immediately implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{\Gamma+t+1}-\widetilde{p}^{t+1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\underline{p}^{\Gamma+t+1}-p^{t+1}\right\|_{2}\to 0,

(G.186)

as
n\to\infty,\Gamma\to\infty
in that order.
Consequently,
Equation

G.184
converges to
0
in the same sequential limit.
We then show that
\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\underline{P}^{\Gamma+t+1}_{i,:}\right\|_{2}^{2}
is finite.
To this end,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\underline{P}^{\Gamma+t+1}_{i,:}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left(c_{p}^{2}\left\|\underline{p}^{\Gamma}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{t+1}\left\|\underline{p}^{\Gamma+i}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{i=0}^{t+1}\left\|\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{\Gamma+i}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|z\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\varepsilon\right\|_{2}^{2}\right).

(G.187)

By the state evolution result in
Lemma

G.4
for the auxiliary GVAMP iteration
Equation

G.151
, for any
\Gamma\geq 0
fixed and any
0\leq i\leq t+1
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\|\underline{p}^{\Gamma+i}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}_{\Gamma+i}^{2}\right]<\infty,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\|\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{\Gamma+i}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\underline{{\mathsf{P}}}}_{\Gamma+i}^{2}\right]<\infty,

almost surely, as
n\to\infty
.
By the induction hypotheses
Equations

G.155
and
G.157
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left\|\underline{p}^{\Gamma+i}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{P}}_{i}^{2}\right]<\infty,

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left\|\widetilde{\underline{p}}^{\Gamma+i}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{i}^{2}\right]<\infty.

(G.188)

Combining this with the induction hypothesis
Equation

G.160
along with
Equations

G.186
and
G.185
shown above, and applying the triangle inequality, we also obtain the finiteness of

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left\|p^{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle<\infty,

\displaystyle\lim_{\Gamma\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left\|\widetilde{p}^{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle<\infty,

(G.189)

for every
0\leq i\leq t+1
.
Taking
Equations

G.189
,
G.188
and
G.179
collectively, we see that both
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\underline{P}_{i,:}^{\Gamma+t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}
and
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|P_{i,:}^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}
are bounded in the double limit.
Finally, combining this with the fact that
Equation

G.184
converges to
0
already shown, we have that the left-hand side of
Equation

G.183
also converges to
0
.
This therefore proves
Equation

G.159
for
t+1
.
The proof for
Equation

G.160
is syntactically symmetric and omitted.
This closes the induction and the desired statements in
Lemma

G.5
follows.
∎

Appendix H
Proof of
Theorem

4.4

We show that the canonical form of Bayes-GVAMP in
Equation

4.39
is indeed a special case of the generic GVAMP in
Equation

4.19
by verifying the trace-free and divergence-free conditions in
Items

(A7)
and
(A8)
.

Lemma H.1
.

The functions
\Phi_{t},\Psi_{t}
in
Equations

4.39c
and
4.39d
are trace-free in the sense of
Item

(A7)
and the functions
f_{t},g_{t}
are divergence-free in the sense of
Item

(A8)
.

Proof.

By the definitions of
v_{2}^{t},c_{2}^{t}
in
Equation

4.36f
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{v_{2}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}}\right]-1=\frac{v_{2}^{t}}{v_{2}^{t}}-1=0,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{c_{2}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}\right]-1=\frac{c_{2}^{t}}{c_{2}^{t}}-1=0.

By the definitions of
v_{1}^{t},c_{1}^{t}
in
Equation

4.36b
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{v_{1}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[g_{x1}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}}_{t};\gamma_{1}^{t})\right]-1=\frac{v_{1}^{t}}{v_{1}^{t}}-1=0,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[g_{t}^{\prime}({\mathsf{P}}_{t};{\mathsf{Y}})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{c_{1}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[g_{z1}^{\prime}({\mathsf{P}}_{t},{\mathsf{Y}};\tau_{1}^{t})\right]-1=\frac{c_{1}^{t}}{c_{1}^{t}}-1=0.

This completes the proof.
∎

Then
Theorem

4.4
follows immediately from the lemma below which states that the scalars
\gamma_{1}^{t},\tau_{1}^{t},v_{1}^{t},c_{1}^{t},\gamma_{2}^{t},\tau_{2}^{t},v_{2}^{t},c_{2}^{t}
are in one-to-one correspondence with the state evolution parameters
\widetilde{a}_{t},\widetilde{b}_{t},a_{t},b_{t},\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{t}^{2}\right],\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{t}^{2}\right]
.

Lemma H.2
.

Let
\gamma_{1}^{t},\tau_{1}^{t},\gamma_{2}^{t},\tau_{2}^{t}
be defined via
Equation

4.36
and let
\widetilde{a}_{t},\widetilde{b}_{t},a_{t},b_{t},\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{t}^{2}\right],\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{t}^{2}\right]
be defined via
Equation

4.29
with
\Phi_{t},\widetilde{\Phi}_{t},\Psi_{t},\widetilde{\Psi}_{t},f_{t},g_{t}
given in
Equations

4.39c
,
4.39d
and
4.39e
.
Then these quantities satisfy

\displaystyle\widetilde{a}_{t}

\displaystyle=\tau_{2}^{t},

\displaystyle\widetilde{b}_{t}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho},

\displaystyle a_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{t+1}^{2}\right]=\tau_{1}^{t+1}-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}},

\displaystyle b_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{t+1}^{2}\right]=\gamma_{1}^{t+1}.

(H.1)

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
t
.
In preparation for performing the induction, we first make a few observations that hold for every
t
.

Preparation.

By the definitions
Equation

4.39d
of
\Psi_{t},\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}
, it holds

\displaystyle\Psi_{t}(x^{2})

\displaystyle=x\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(x)-1.

(H.2)

The trace-free property of
\Psi_{t}
(see
Lemma

H.1
) then implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})\right]

\displaystyle=1.

We use these observations to simplify the expressions of a few expectations involving
\Psi_{t},\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})-1\right)^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]-1,

(H.3a)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})-1\right)^{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]-2\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{3}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})\right],

(H.3b)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})-1\right)\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]-1.

(H.3c)

Plugging in the formula of
\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}
in
Equation

4.39d
, we write some terms on the right-hand side, together with
\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\right]
, more explicitly as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(c_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(c_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right],

(H.4a)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(c_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{6}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{3}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{c_{2}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}\right],

(H.4b)

We then express the expectations on the right-hand side above in terms of
c_{2}^{t}
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right]
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{2}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}-\gamma_{2}^{t}\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}\right]=\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}{\tau_{2}^{t}}-\frac{\gamma_{2}^{t}c_{2}^{t}}{\tau_{2}^{t}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{6}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right]
