\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{2}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}\right]-\frac{\gamma_{2}^{t}}{\tau_{2}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right]=\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}{(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}}-\frac{\gamma_{2}^{t}c_{2}^{t}}{(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}}-\frac{\gamma_{2}^{t}}{\tau_{2}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}-\tau_{2}^{t}\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right]=\frac{c_{2}^{t}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}}-\frac{\tau_{2}^{t}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right].

(H.5)

Putting these back in
Equation

H.4
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}^{t}c_{2}^{t}}-\frac{\tau_{2}^{t}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}(c_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right],

(H.6a)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(c_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right],

(H.6b)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}{(\tau_{2}^{t}c_{2}^{t})^{2}}-\frac{\gamma_{2}^{t}}{(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}c_{2}^{t}}-\frac{\gamma_{2}^{t}}{\tau_{2}^{t}(c_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right],

(H.6c)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{3}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}{\tau_{2}^{t}c_{2}^{t}}-\frac{\gamma_{2}^{t}}{\tau_{2}^{t}}.

(H.6d)

We perform analogous manipulations to several quantities involving
\Phi_{t},\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}
.
First note that by the definitions of both functions in
Equation

4.39c
and the definition of
\gamma_{1}^{t+1}
in
Equation

4.36h
, the following identity holds

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{t}\Phi_{t}(x^{2})+\tau_{2}^{t}x\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{v_{2}^{t}}-\gamma_{2}^{t}=\gamma_{1}^{t+1}.

(H.7)

By rearranging terms and using the trace-free property of
\Phi_{t}
(see
Lemma

H.1
), this implies

\displaystyle\Phi_{t}(x^{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}^{t}}\left(\gamma_{1}^{t+1}-\tau_{2}^{t}x\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}(x)\right),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{1}^{t+1}}{\tau_{2}^{t}}.

We use the above observations to compute a few expectations involving these
\Phi_{t},\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}^{t}}\left(\gamma_{1}^{t+1}-\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})\right)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{(\gamma_{1}^{t+1})^{2}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}}+\frac{(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}\right]-2\frac{\gamma_{1}^{t+1}\tau_{2}^{t}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}}\cdot\frac{\gamma_{1}^{t+1}}{\tau_{2}^{t}}=\frac{(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]-\frac{(\gamma_{1}^{t+1})^{2}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}},

(H.8a)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}^{t}}\left(\gamma_{1}^{t+1}-\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})\right)\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{(\gamma_{1}^{t+1})^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}\tau_{2}^{t}}-\frac{\tau_{2}^{t}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right].

(H.8b)

We then derive more explicit expressions for
\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]
that shows up on the right-hand side and
\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}\right]
.
By the definition
Equation

4.39c
of
\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(v_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}}\right],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(v_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}}\right],

By elementary algebra, the expectations of the fractions on the right-hand side can be written in terms of
v_{2}^{t}
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}}\right]
.
Specifically, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{2}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}}-\gamma_{2}^{t}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}}\right]=\frac{v_{2}^{t}}{\tau_{2}^{t}}-\frac{\gamma_{2}^{t}}{\tau_{2}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}}\right],

(H.9)

and

\displaystyle(\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(1-\frac{\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right]=1+(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}}\right]-2\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=1+(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}}\right]-2\left(1-\gamma_{2}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}}\right]+2\gamma_{2}^{t}v_{2}^{t}-1,

which implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{(\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}}{(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}}\right]-\frac{2\gamma_{2}^{t}v_{2}^{t}}{(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}}+\frac{1}{(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}}.

(H.10)

Dividing both terms in
Equations

H.9
and
H.10
by
(v_{2}^{t})^{2}
brings us to the following expressions of
\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}\right],\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{2}^{t}v_{2}^{t}}-\frac{\gamma_{2}^{t}}{\tau_{2}^{t}(v_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}}\right],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{(\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}}{(\tau_{2}^{t}v_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}}\right]-\frac{2\gamma_{2}^{t}}{(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}v_{2}^{t}}+\frac{1}{(\tau_{2}^{t}v_{2}^{t})^{2}}.

We also make an observation regarding
v_{1}^{t},c_{1}^{t}
.
By their definitions in
Equation

4.36b
, the expressions of
g_{x1}^{\prime},g_{z1}^{\prime}
in
Equation

4.34
and the tower property of conditional expectation, we have

\displaystyle v_{1}^{t}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Var}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\,|\,{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\right]\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}^{2}\,|\,{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\,|\,{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\right]^{2}\right]=\rho-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\,|\,{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\right]\right],

(H.12)

\displaystyle c_{1}^{t}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Var}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\,|\,{\mathsf{P}}_{t},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}^{2}\,|\,{\mathsf{P}}_{t},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\,|\,{\mathsf{P}}_{t},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]^{2}\right]=\sigma^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\,|\,{\mathsf{P}}_{t},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]\right],

(H.13)

implying

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\,|\,{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\rho-v_{1}^{t},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\,|\,{\mathsf{P}}_{t},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}-c_{1}^{t}.

(H.14)

We are now ready to present the induction proof.
We first perform the induction step since the verification of the base case
t=0
follows similar arguments in which the initial condition
Equation

4.38
instead of the induction hypotheses is used.

Induction step.

Now assuming the validity of the conclusion
Equation

H.1
for time up to
t-1
, we verify its validity for time
t
.

Let us start with
\widetilde{a}_{t}
:

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\widetilde{a}_{t}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:ab_tilde}}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t}{\mathsf{Z}}\right]\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:wtP_wtR}}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[g_{t}({\mathsf{P}}_{t};{\mathsf{Y}}){\mathsf{Z}}\right]\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:B_fg}}}{=}\frac{1}{c_{1}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[g_{z1}({\mathsf{P}}_{t},{\mathsf{Y}};\tau_{1}^{t}){\mathsf{Z}}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{P}}_{t}{\mathsf{Z}}\right]\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{eqn:gz1} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{eqn:PR}}}{=}\frac{1}{c_{1}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\,|\,{\mathsf{P}}_{t},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]{\mathsf{Z}}\right]-a_{t}\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{c_{1}^{t}}(\sigma^{2}-c_{1}^{t})-\left(\tau_{1}^{t}-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\right)\sigma^{2}=\sigma^{2}\left(\frac{1}{c_{1}^{t}}-\tau_{1}^{t}\right)\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:B_gamma2_tau2}}}{=}\sigma^{2}\tau_{2}^{t}.

implying

\displaystyle\widetilde{a}_{t}

\displaystyle=\tau_{2}^{t},

(H.15)

as desired.
In the above display, the first equality in the second line uses the observation
Equation

H.14
and the induction hypothesis
a_{t}=\tau_{1}^{t}-\sigma^{-2}
.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\rho\widetilde{b}_{t}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:ab_tilde}}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right]\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:wtP_wtR}}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t}({\mathsf{R}}_{t}){\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right]\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:B_fg}}}{=}\frac{1}{v_{1}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[g_{x1}({\mathsf{R}}_{t};\gamma_{1}^{t}){\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{R}}_{t}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right]\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{eqn:PR} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{eqn:gx1}}}{=}\frac{1}{v_{1}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\,|\,{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\right]{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\right]-b_{t}\rho,

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{v_{1}^{t}}(\rho-v_{1}^{t})-\gamma_{1}^{t}\rho=\rho\left(\frac{1}{v_{1}^{t}}-\gamma_{1}^{t}\right)-1\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:B_gamma2_tau2}}}{=}\rho\gamma_{2}^{t}-1,

where the first equality in the second line uses
Equation

H.14
and the induction hypothesis
b_{t}=\gamma_{1}^{t}
.
This implies

\displaystyle\widetilde{b}_{t}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}.

(H.16)

We then consider
b_{t+1}
:

\displaystyle b_{t+1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:ab}}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\widetilde{a}_{t}\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{eqn:B_Phi} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{eqn:ind_wta}}}{=}\frac{1}{v_{2}^{t}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{v_{2}^{t}}\left(1-\gamma_{2}^{t}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}}\right]\right)\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:B_v2_c2}}}{=}\frac{1}{v_{2}^{t}}\left(1-\gamma_{2}^{t}\cdot v_{2}^{t}\right)=\frac{1}{v_{2}^{t}}-\gamma_{2}^{t}\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:B_gamma1_tau1}}}{=}\gamma_{1}^{t+1},

(H.17)

as desired.
Next, move to
a_{t+1}
:

\displaystyle a_{t+1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:ab}}}{=}\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right]\widetilde{b}_{t}+\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\widetilde{a}_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{eqn:ind_wta}, \lx@cref{refnum}{eqn:ind_wtb}, \lx@cref{refnum}{eqn:B_Psi} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{eqn:Psi_wtPsi}}}{=}\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{c_{2}^{t}}\cdot\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}\right]\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{c_{2}^{t}}\cdot\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}-1\right){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\tau_{2}^{t}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:B_v2_c2}}}{=}\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\left\{\frac{1}{c_{2}^{t}}\cdot c_{2}^{t}\cdot\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)+\frac{\tau_{2}^{t}}{c_{2}^{t}}\cdot\frac{1}{\tau_{2}^{t}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]-\gamma_{2}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}\right]\right)-\tau_{2}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:B_v2_c2}}}{=}\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\left\{\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}+\frac{1}{c_{2}^{t}}\left(\overline{\kappa}_{2}-\gamma_{2}^{t}c_{2}^{t}\right)-\tau_{2}^{t}\overline{\kappa}_{2}\right\}=\frac{1}{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}\left\{\frac{1}{c_{2}^{t}}\overline{\kappa}_{2}-\tau_{2}^{t}\overline{\kappa}_{2}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right\}\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:sigma2}}}{=}\frac{1}{c_{2}^{t}}-\tau_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:B_gamma1_tau1}}}{=}\tau_{1}^{t+1}-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}.

(H.18)

Before proceeding to
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{t+1}^{2}\right],\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{t+1}^{2}\right]
, using the induction hypotheses, we derive expressions of
\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t}^{2}\right],\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t}^{2}\right]
in terms of
\tau_{2}^{t},\gamma_{2}^{t}
.
By the tower property of conditional expectation,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{c_{1}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\,|\,{\mathsf{P}}_{t},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]-{\mathsf{P}}_{t}\right)^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{(c_{1}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\,|\,{\mathsf{P}}_{t},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{P}}_{t}^{2}\right]-\frac{2}{c_{1}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{P}}_{t}{\mathsf{Z}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(c_{1}^{t})^{2}}(\sigma^{2}-c_{1}^{t})+\left[\left(\tau_{1}^{t}-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\right)^{2}\sigma^{2}+\left(\tau_{1}^{t}-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\right)\right]-\frac{2}{c_{1}^{t}}\left(\tau_{1}^{t}-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\right)\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\left(\frac{1}{c_{1}^{t}}-\tau_{1}^{t}\right)\left(\frac{1}{c_{1}^{t}}-\tau_{1}^{t}+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\right)\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:B_gamma2_tau2}}}{=}\sigma^{2}\tau_{2}^{t}\left(\tau_{2}^{t}+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\right),

(H.19)

where the second line follows from the induction hypotheses
a_{t}=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{t}^{2}\right]=\tau_{1}^{t}-\sigma^{-2}
.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{v_{1}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\,|\,{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\right]-{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\right)^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{(v_{1}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\,|\,{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\right]\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{R}}_{t}^{2}\right]-\frac{2}{v_{1}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(v_{1}^{t})^{2}}(\rho-v_{1}^{t})+\left[(\gamma_{1}^{t})^{2}\rho+\gamma_{1}^{t}\right]-\frac{2}{v_{1}^{t}}\cdot\gamma_{1}^{t}\rho=\frac{\rho}{v_{1}^{t}}\left(\frac{1}{v_{1}^{t}}-\gamma_{1}^{t}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{v_{1}^{t}}-\gamma_{1}^{t}\right)-\rho\gamma_{1}^{t}\left(\frac{1}{v_{1}^{t}}-\gamma_{1}^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\rho\gamma_{2}^{t}\left(\frac{1}{v_{1}^{t}}-\gamma_{1}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:B_gamma2_tau2}}}{=}\rho\gamma_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right),

(H.20)

where the second line follows from the induction hypotheses
b_{t}=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{t}^{2}\right]=\gamma_{1}^{t}
.

Equipped with the expressions
Equations

H.19
and
H.20
, we now verify
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{t+1}^{2}\right]=\tau_{1}^{t+1}-\sigma^{-2},\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{t+1}^{2}\right]=\gamma_{1}^{t+1}
.
First consider
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{t+1}^{2}\right]
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{t+1}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:J_cov}}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}\right]\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t}^{2}\right]-\widetilde{a}_{t}^{2}\sigma^{2}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]\widetilde{a}_{t}^{2}\rho+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t}^{2}\right]-a_{t+1}^{2}\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}\right]\widetilde{a}_{t}\widetilde{b}_{t}\rho,

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]-1\right)\left[\sigma^{2}\tau_{2}^{t}\left(\tau_{2}^{t}+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\right)-(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}\sigma^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]-2\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{3}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})\right]\right)(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}\rho

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]\rho\gamma_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)-\left(\tau_{1}^{t+1}-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\right)^{2}\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\left(\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]-1\right)\tau_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)\rho

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:B_gamma1_tau1}}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]\left(\tau_{2}^{t}+2\tau_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)\rho\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]-2\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{3}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})\right]\right)(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}\rho+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]\rho\gamma_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\tau_{2}^{t}+2\tau_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)\rho+\left(\frac{1}{c_{2}^{t}}-\tau_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\right)^{2}\sigma^{2}\right).

(H.21)

In the above display, the second equality uses the expressions
Equation

H.3
of several expectations involving
\Psi_{t},\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}
, the expressions

H.19
and

of

just proved in

,
H.16
and

Let us first examine terms involving expectations, excluding the last line

.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]\left(\tau_{2}^{t}+2\tau_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)\rho\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]+\overline{\kappa}_{2}-2\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{3}\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})\right]\right)(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}\rho+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n})^{2}\right]\rho\gamma_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:expect_Psi_explicit}}}{=}\left(\frac{1}{(c_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right]\right)\left(\tau_{2}^{t}+2\tau_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)\rho\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}{(\tau_{2}^{t}c_{2}^{t})^{2}}-\frac{\gamma_{2}^{t}}{(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}c_{2}^{t}}-\frac{\gamma_{2}^{t}}{\tau_{2}^{t}(c_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right]+\overline{\kappa}_{2}-2\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}{\tau_{2}^{t}c_{2}^{t}}-\frac{\gamma_{2}^{t}}{\tau_{2}^{t}}\right)\right)(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}\rho

(H.22)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}^{t}c_{2}^{t}}-\frac{\tau_{2}^{t}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}(c_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right]\right)\rho\gamma_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right).

(H.23)

Elementary algebra shows that the coefficients in front of
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right]
add up to zero:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(c_{2}^{t})^{2}}\left(\tau_{2}^{t}+2\tau_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)\rho\right)-\frac{\gamma_{2}^{t}}{\tau_{2}^{t}(c_{2}^{t})^{2}}(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}\rho-\frac{\tau_{2}^{t}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}(c_{2}^{t})^{2}}\rho\gamma_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)=0.

Therefore all terms involving that expectation are cancelled.
Moreover, the remaining terms in
Equations

H.22
,
H.23
and
H.21
that do not involve
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{4}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2})^{2}}\right]
add up to

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}{(\tau_{2}^{t}c_{2}^{t})^{2}}-\frac{\gamma_{2}^{t}}{(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}c_{2}^{t}}+\overline{\kappa}_{2}-2\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}_{2}}{\tau_{2}^{t}c_{2}^{t}}-\frac{\gamma_{2}^{t}}{\tau_{2}^{t}}\right)\right)(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}\rho+\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}^{t}c_{2}^{t}}\cdot\rho\gamma_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)

\displaystyle-\left(\tau_{2}^{t}+2\tau_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)\rho+\left(\frac{1}{c_{2}^{t}}-\tau_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\right)^{2}\sigma^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{c_{2}^{t}}-\tau_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:B_gamma1_tau1}}}{=}\tau_{1}^{t+1}-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}.

So we have shown

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{t+1}^{2}\right]=\tau_{1}^{t+1}-\sigma^{-2}.

(H.24)

Finally, we turn to
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{t+1}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{t+1}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:K_cov}}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t}^{2}\right]-b_{t+1}^{2}\rho+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}\right]\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t}^{2}\right]-\widetilde{a}_{t}^{2}\sigma^{2}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]\widetilde{a}_{t}^{2}\rho

\displaystyle\quad+2\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}){\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}\right]\widetilde{a}_{t}\widetilde{b}_{t}\rho

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]-\frac{(\gamma_{1}^{t+1})^{2}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}}\right)\rho\gamma_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)-(\gamma_{1}^{t+1})^{2}\rho

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}\right]\left(\sigma^{2}\tau_{2}^{t}\left(\tau_{2}^{t}+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\right)-(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}\sigma^{2}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right](\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}\rho

\displaystyle\quad+2\left(\frac{(\gamma_{1}^{t+1})^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}\tau_{2}^{t}}-\frac{\tau_{2}^{t}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]\right)\tau_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)\rho

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eqn:B_gamma1_tau1}}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]\left(\frac{(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}}\rho\gamma_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)+(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}\rho-2\frac{\tau_{2}^{t}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}}\tau_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)\rho\right)+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}\right]\tau_{2}^{t}

(H.25)

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\frac{(1/v_{2}^{t}-\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}}\rho\gamma_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)+(1/v_{2}^{t}-\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}\rho-2\frac{(1/v_{2}^{t}-\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}\tau_{2}^{t}}\tau_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)\rho\right),

(H.26)

where the second equality uses the expressions of
\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{t}^{2}\right],\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{t}^{2}\right]
in

and

,

and

in

We use

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]\left(\frac{(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}}\rho\gamma_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)+(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}\rho-2\frac{\tau_{2}^{t}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}}\tau_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)\rho\right)+\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}\right]\tau_{2}^{t}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{(\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}}{(\tau_{2}^{t}v_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d})^{2}}\right]-\frac{2\gamma_{2}^{t}}{(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}v_{2}^{t}}+\frac{1}{(\tau_{2}^{t}v_{2}^{t})^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}}{(\gamma_{2}^{t})^{2}}\rho\gamma_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)+(\tau_{2}^{t})^{2}\rho-2\frac{\tau_{2}^{t}}{\gamma_{2}^{t}}\tau_{2}^{t}\left(\gamma_{2}^{t}-\frac{1}{\rho}\right)\rho\right)

(H.27)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{2}^{t}v_{2}^{t}}-\frac{\gamma_{2}^{t}}{\tau_{2}^{t}(v_{2}^{t})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{(\gamma_{2}^{t}+\tau_{2}^{t}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2})^{2}}\right]\right)\tau_{2}^{t}.

H.19
and
H.20
for
t=0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\tau_{2}^{0}(\tau_{2}^{0}+\sigma^{-2}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{{\mathsf{R}}}_{0}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\rho\gamma_{2}^{0}(\gamma_{2}^{0}-\rho^{-1}).

(H.34)

Using
Equations

H.31
,
H.33
and
H.34
shown above together with other observations in the
Preparation
step (which do not require the induction hypotheses) in the calculations leading to
Equations

H.24
and
H.29
, we obtain the desired
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{J}}_{1}^{2}\right]=\tau_{1}^{1}-\sigma^{-2},\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{K}}_{1}^{2}\right]=\gamma_{1}^{1}
.
This completes the verification for the
t=0
case of
Equation

H.1
and thereby concludes the whole proof.
∎

Appendix I
Proof of
Theorem

4.6

Before diving in the proof, we first note from the definition
Equation

4.42
of
Q_{z}
and the definition
Equation

3.9
of
\sigma^{2}
that

\displaystyle Q_{z}

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}\right]}{\delta}=\rho\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}\right]=\rho\overline{m}_{2}=\rho\overline{\kappa}_{2}=\sigma^{2}.

(I.1)

Therefore
\widehat{Q}_{z}=\sigma^{-2}
.

To prove the result, we will argue that under the change of variables in
Equation

4.45
, the fixed point equations for the variables in
Equation

4.44
coincide with the replica saddle point equations
Equation

4.41
.
The former equations can be obtained by removing the time indices from the recursion for
(v_{1}^{t},c_{1}^{t},\gamma_{2}^{t},\tau_{2}^{t},v_{2}^{t},c_{2}^{t},\gamma_{1}^{t+1},\tau_{1}^{t+1})
in
Equation

4.36
:

\displaystyle v_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[g_{x1}^{\prime}({\mathsf{R}};\gamma_{1})\right],

\displaystyle c_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[g_{z1}^{\prime}({\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{Y}};\tau_{1})\right],

(I.2a)

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{v_{1}}=\frac{1}{v_{2}},

\displaystyle\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{c_{1}}=\frac{1}{c_{2}},

(I.2b)

\displaystyle v_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\tau_{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}+\gamma_{2}}\right],

\displaystyle c_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}{\tau_{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}+\gamma_{2}}\right],

(I.2c)

We will check the equivalence between
Equations

4.41
and
I.2
equation by equation.

Given the observation
Equation

I.1
, the equivalences between
Equation

4.41c
and the first equation in
Equation

I.2b
, between
Equation

4.41d
and the second equation in
Equation

I.2b
, between
Equation

4.41e
and the first equation in
Equation

I.2c
, between
Equation

4.41f
and the second equation in
Equation

I.2c
are apparent.
The rest of the proof is devoted to checking the equivalences between
Equation

4.41a
and the first equation in
Equation

I.2a
, between
Equation

4.41b
and the second equation in
Equation

I.2a
.

First consider
Equation

4.41a
.
Recalling the definition of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}(b,a)
in
Equation

4.40
, we compute the partial derivative of the log partition function evaluated at
(\sqrt{\widehat{q}_{x}}\xi,\widehat{q}_{x})
:

\displaystyle\partial_{b}\log{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}\left(\sqrt{\widehat{q}_{x}}\xi,\widehat{q}_{x}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}\left(\sqrt{\widehat{q}_{x}}\xi,\widehat{q}_{x}\right)}\int xP_{{\mathsf{B}}_{*}}(x)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{q}_{x}x^{2}+\sqrt{\widehat{q}_{x}}\xi x\right)\,\mathrm{d}x.

This allows us to evaluate the expectation on the right-hand side of
Equation

4.41a
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}\left(\sqrt{\widehat{q}_{x}}\,{\mathsf{\Xi}},\widehat{q}_{x}\right)\left(\partial_{b}\log{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}\left(\sqrt{\widehat{q}_{x}}\,{\mathsf{\Xi}},\widehat{q}_{x}\right)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}\left(\sqrt{\widehat{q}_{x}}{\mathsf{\Xi}},\widehat{q}_{x}\right)}\left(\int xP_{{\mathsf{B}}_{*}}(x)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{q}_{x}x^{2}+\sqrt{\widehat{q}_{x}}{\mathsf{\Xi}}x\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\xi^{2}\right)}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{\left(\int xP_{{\mathsf{B}}_{*}}(x)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{q}_{x}x^{2}+\sqrt{\widehat{q}_{x}}\xi x\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{2}}{\int P_{{\mathsf{B}}_{*}}(x)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{q}_{x}x^{2}+\sqrt{\widehat{q}_{x}}\xi x\right)\,\mathrm{d}x}\,\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\widehat{q}_{x}}}\int\frac{\left(\int xP_{{\mathsf{B}}_{*}}(x)\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi/\widehat{q}_{x}}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{q}_{x}\left(x-\frac{\xi}{\sqrt{\widehat{q}_{x}}}\right)^{2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{2}}{\int P_{{\mathsf{B}}_{*}}(x)\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi/\widehat{q}_{x}}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{q}_{x}\left(x-\frac{\xi}{\sqrt{\widehat{q}_{x}}}\right)^{2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x}\,\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\,|\,{\mathsf{R}}\right]^{2}\right],\qquad\frac{{\mathsf{R}}}{\widehat{q}_{x}}={\mathsf{B}}_{*}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\widehat{q}_{x}}}\overline{{\mathsf{K}}},\ ({\mathsf{B}}_{*},\overline{{\mathsf{K}}})\sim P_{{\mathsf{B}}_{*}}\otimes{\mathcal{N}}(0,1),

where we made the change of variable
{\mathsf{R}}=\sqrt{\widehat{q}_{x}}{\mathsf{\Xi}}
in the last step.

On the other hand, from
Equation

H.12
, we see that the first equation of
Equation

I.2a
is equivalent to

\displaystyle v_{1}

\displaystyle=\rho-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\,|\,{\mathsf{R}}\right]\right].

(I.3)

In view of the change of variable
v_{1}=\rho-q_{x}
and the tower property of conditional expectation, we conclude the equivalence between
Equations

4.41a
and
I.3
.

Next, consider
Equation

4.41b
.
Recalling the definition of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathrm{out}}(y;\xi,v)
, we compute the partial derivative of the log partition function:

\displaystyle\partial_{\xi}\log{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathrm{out}}(y;\xi,v)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathrm{out}}(y;\xi,v)}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{\xi}Q\left(y\,|\,\sqrt{v}{\mathsf{\Omega}}+\xi\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathrm{out}}(y;\xi,v)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{v}}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{z}Q\left(y\,|\,\sqrt{v}{\mathsf{\Omega}}+\xi\right)\right]=\frac{1}{{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathrm{out}}(y;\xi,v)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{v}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Omega}}Q\left(y\,|\,\sqrt{v}{\mathsf{\Omega}}+\xi\right)\right].

With the shorthand
{\mathfrak{a}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\widehat{Q}_{z}+\widehat{q}_{z}}}
and
{\mathfrak{b}}=\sqrt{\frac{\widehat{q}_{z}}{\widehat{Q}_{z}(\widehat{Q}_{z}+\widehat{q}_{z})}}
, we can write the expectation on the right-hand side of
Equation

4.41b
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathrm{out}}\left(y;{\mathfrak{b}}{\mathsf{\Xi}},{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}\right)\left(\partial_{\xi}\log{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathrm{out}}\left(y;{\mathfrak{b}}{\mathsf{\Xi}},{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}\right)\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Omega}}Q\left(y\,|\,{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathsf{\Omega}}+{\mathfrak{b}}{\mathsf{\Xi}}\right)\right]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[Q\left(y\,|\,{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathsf{\Omega}}+{\mathfrak{b}}{\mathsf{\Xi}}\right)\right]}\,\mathrm{d}y\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\iint p_{{\mathsf{\Xi}}}(\xi)\frac{\left(\int\omega p_{{\mathsf{\Omega}}}(\omega)p_{\left.{\mathsf{Y}}\,|\,{\mathsf{\Omega}},{\mathsf{\Xi}}\right.}\left(y\,|\,\omega,\xi\right)\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\omega}{\int p_{{\mathsf{\Omega}}}(\omega)p_{\left.{\mathsf{Y}}\,|\,{\mathsf{\Omega}},{\mathsf{\Xi}}\right.}\left(y\,|\,\omega,\xi\right)\,\mathrm{d}\omega}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\iint p_{{\mathsf{\Xi}}}(\xi)p_{\left.{\mathsf{Y}}\,|\,{\mathsf{\Xi}}\right.}\left(y\,|\,\xi\right)\left(\int\omega\frac{p_{\left.{\mathsf{\Omega}},{\mathsf{Y}}\,|\,{\mathsf{\Xi}}\right.}\left(\omega,y\,|\,\xi\right)}{p_{\left.{\mathsf{Y}}\,|\,{\mathsf{\Xi}}\right.}\left(y\,|\,\xi\right)}\,\mathrm{d}\omega\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\iint p_{{\mathsf{\Xi}},{\mathsf{Y}}}(\xi,y)\left(\int\omega p_{\left.{\mathsf{\Omega}}\,|\,{\mathsf{Y}},{\mathsf{\Xi}}\right.}\left(\omega\,|\,y,\xi\right)\,\mathrm{d}\omega\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{\Omega}}\,|\,{\mathsf{\Xi}},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]^{2}\right],\qquad\textnormal{where }({\mathsf{\Xi}},{\mathsf{\Omega}})\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)\otimes{\mathcal{N}}(0,1),\ {\mathsf{Y}}\sim Q\left(\cdot\,|\,{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathsf{\Omega}}+{\mathfrak{b}}{\mathsf{\Xi}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathsf{M}}-{\mathfrak{b}}{\mathsf{\Xi}})\,|\,{\mathsf{\Xi}},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]^{2}\right],\qquad\textnormal{where }{\mathsf{M}}={\mathfrak{a}}{\mathsf{\Omega}}+{\mathfrak{b}}{\mathsf{\Xi}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{M}}\,|\,{\mathsf{\Xi}},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]^{2}\right]+{\mathfrak{b}}^{2}-2{\mathfrak{b}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{M}}{\mathsf{\Xi}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{M}}\,|\,{\mathsf{\Xi}},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]^{2}\right]-{\mathfrak{b}}^{2}\right),

where the penultimate line is due to the tower property of conditional expectation.

To connect this to the second equation in
Equation

I.2a
, we make the following observation.
By
Theorem

4.4
, at fixed point,
{\mathsf{P}}
has distribution

\displaystyle{\mathsf{P}}

\displaystyle=\left(\tau_{1}-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\right){\mathsf{Z}}+\sqrt{\tau_{1}-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}}\overline{{\mathsf{K}}},

\displaystyle\textnormal{where }({\mathsf{Z}},\overline{{\mathsf{K}}})\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,\sigma^{2})\otimes{\mathcal{N}}(0,1).

In particular
{\mathsf{P}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,(\tau_{1}-\sigma^{-2})\tau_{1}\sigma^{2})
.
From this, one can deduce the reverse channel

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Z}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tau_{1}}}{\mathsf{\Omega}}+\frac{1}{\tau_{1}}{\mathsf{P}},

\displaystyle\textnormal{where }({\mathsf{\Omega}},{\mathsf{P}})\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)\otimes{\mathcal{N}}(0,(\tau_{1}-\sigma^{-2})\tau_{1}\sigma^{2}).

(I.4)

On the other hand, recalling the shorthand
{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}
, using the observation
\widehat{Q}_{z}=\sigma^{-2}
and the change of variables
\widehat{q}_{z}=\tau_{1}-\sigma^{-2}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{M}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\widehat{Q}_{z}+\widehat{q}_{z}}}{\mathsf{\Omega}}+\sqrt{\frac{\widehat{q}_{z}}{\widehat{Q}_{z}(\widehat{Q}_{z}+\widehat{q}_{z})}}{\mathsf{\Xi}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tau_{1}}}{\mathsf{\Omega}}+\sqrt{\sigma^{2}-\frac{1}{\tau_{1}}}{\mathsf{\Xi}}.

(I.5)

Contrasting
Equation

I.5
with
Equation

I.4
, we find that

\displaystyle\left({\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{Z}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}\left(\sqrt{(\tau_{1}-\sigma^{2})\tau_{1}\sigma^{2}}{\mathsf{\Xi}},{\mathsf{M}}\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{M}}\,|\,{\mathsf{\Xi}},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]^{2}\right]-{\mathfrak{b}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\tau_{1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{M}}\,|\,\sqrt{(\tau_{1}-\sigma^{2})\tau_{1}\sigma^{2}}{\mathsf{\Xi}},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]^{2}\right]-\left(\sigma^{2}-\frac{1}{\tau_{1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\tau_{1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\,|\,{\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]^{2}\right]-\left(\sigma^{2}-\frac{1}{\tau_{1}}\right)\right).

(I.6)

With the relation
Equation

I.6
above and the change of variables
q_{z}=\sigma^{2}-c_{1},\widehat{q}_{z}=\tau_{1}-\sigma^{-2}
, we can use the tower property of conditional expectation to write
Equation

4.41b
as

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}-c_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{1}}\left\{\frac{\tau_{1}-\sigma^{-2}}{\sigma^{-2}}+\tau_{1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\,|\,{\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]\right]-\left(\sigma^{2}-\frac{1}{\tau_{1}}\right)\right)\right\}.

Upon rearrangement, this is precisely

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}-c_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\,|\,{\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]\right],

which, in view of
Equation

H.13
, is in turn equivalent to the second equation in
Equation

I.2a
.

This completes the proof of the equivalence between
Equations

I.2
and
4.41
and therefore of
Theorem

4.6
.

Appendix J
Details of examples and numerical experiments

J.1
Replica saddle point equations

For fixed
\gamma_{1}\geq 0,\tau_{1}\geq\sigma^{-2}
, let

\displaystyle({\mathsf{B}}_{*},\overline{{\mathsf{J}}})

\displaystyle\sim P_{{\mathsf{B}}_{*}}\otimes{\mathcal{N}}(0,1),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{R}}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{1}{\mathsf{B}}_{*}+\sqrt{\gamma_{1}}\,\overline{{\mathsf{J}}},

(J.1a)

\displaystyle({\mathsf{Z}},\overline{{\mathsf{K}}})

\displaystyle\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,\sigma^{2})\otimes{\mathcal{N}}(0,1),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{P}}

\displaystyle=(\tau_{1}-\sigma^{-2}){\mathsf{Z}}+\sqrt{\tau_{1}-\sigma^{-2}}\,\overline{{\mathsf{K}}},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}

\displaystyle\sim Q\left(\cdot\,|\,{\mathsf{Z}}\right).

(J.1b)

With respect to the above laws, define functions

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{x}(\gamma_{1})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\,|\,{\mathsf{R}}\right]\right],

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{z}(\tau_{1})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\,|\,{\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]\right].

(J.2)

Recalling the construction of
{\mathsf{\Lambda}}
in
Equation

6.2
and the definition of
{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}
in
Equation

3.8
, define the function

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}(\tau_{2},\gamma_{2})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\left[(\tau_{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}+\gamma_{2})^{-1}\right]=\begin{cases}\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tau_{2}\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}}^{2}+\gamma_{2}\right)^{-1}\right]+(1-\delta)\gamma_{2}^{-1},&\delta\leq 1\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tau_{2}\delta\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}}^{2}+\gamma_{2}\right)^{-1}\right],&\delta>1\end{cases}.

(J.3)

Proposition J.1
.

The fixed point
(v_{1},v_{2},\gamma_{1})
of
Equation

4.36
satisfies
v_{1}=v_{2}=v
and
(v,\gamma_{1})\in[0,\rho]\times[0,\infty)
solves the following system of equations

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=\rho-{\mathcal{E}}_{x}(\gamma_{1}),

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{v}\left[1-\tau_{1}(v,\gamma_{1})\left(\sigma^{2}-{\mathcal{E}}_{z}(\tau_{1}(v,\gamma_{1}))\right)\right],

(J.4)

where
\tau_{1}(v,\gamma_{1})\geq\sigma^{-2}
is the unique solution to

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle={\mathcal{S}}\left(\frac{\tau_{1}\gamma_{1}v}{\delta-\gamma_{1}v},\frac{1}{v}-\gamma_{1}\right).

(J.5)

Furthermore, for the special case of phase retrieval
q(z,\varepsilon)=\left|z\right|
with Gaussian prior
P_{{\mathsf{B}}_{*}}={\mathcal{N}}(0,\rho)
,
v\in[0,\rho]
can be expressed more explicitly as the solution to
v={\mathcal{F}}(v)
, for a function
{\mathcal{F}}
defined as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}(v)

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta\rho v}{\rho-v}\left(1-\tau_{1}(v)\left(\sigma^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Y}}^{2}\tanh({\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right]\right)\right),

(J.6)

where

\displaystyle({\mathsf{P}},\overline{{\mathsf{K}}})

\displaystyle\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,(\tau_{1}(v)-\sigma^{-2})\tau_{1}(v)\sigma^{2})\otimes{\mathcal{N}}(0,1),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{1}{\tau_{1}(v)}{\mathsf{P}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tau_{1}(v)}}\overline{{\mathsf{K}}}\right|,

(J.7)

and
\tau_{1}(v)\geq\sigma^{-2}
(with slight abuse of notation for the solution
\tau_{1}(v,\gamma_{1})
to
Equation

J.5
) is the unique solution to

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle={\mathcal{S}}\left(\frac{(\rho-v)\tau_{1}}{\rho\delta-(\rho-v)},\frac{1}{\rho}\right).

Proof.

The replica saddle point equations
Equation

I.2
read:

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=\rho-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\,|\,{\mathsf{R}}\right]\right],

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\,|\,{\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]\right],

(J.8a)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{c}

\displaystyle=\tau_{1}+\tau_{2},

(J.8b)

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\tau_{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}+\gamma_{2}}\right],

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}{\tau_{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}+\gamma_{2}}\right],

(J.8c)

where we use the notation
v\coloneqq v_{1}=v_{2},c\coloneqq c_{1}=c_{2}
,
and the random variables in the first line have joint laws specified in
Equation

J.1
.

Equation

J.8
is intended to be solved in the domain

\displaystyle(\gamma_{1},\tau_{1},\gamma_{2},\tau_{2},v,c)

\displaystyle\in[0,\infty)\times[\sigma^{-2},\infty)\times[\rho^{-1},\infty)\times[0,\infty)\times[0,\rho]\times[0,\sigma^{2}].

Note that
c
can be expressed by other variables as

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}}{\tau_{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{n}^{2}+\gamma_{2}}\right]=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}}{\tau_{2}{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}+\gamma_{2}}\right]=\frac{1}{\delta\tau_{2}}(1-\gamma_{2}v).

(J.9)

Using
\gamma_{2}=1/v-\gamma_{1},\tau_{2}=1/c-\tau_{1}
from
Equation

J.8b
to eliminate
\gamma_{2},\tau_{2}
in
Equation

J.9
, upon rearrangements, we obtain

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{1}}\left(1-\frac{\gamma_{1}v}{\delta}\right).

(J.10)

Substituting the right-hand side of
Equation

J.10
in the second equation of
Equation

J.8b
for
c
, we have

\displaystyle\tau_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{c}-\tau_{1}=\frac{\tau_{1}\gamma_{1}v}{\delta-\gamma_{1}v}.

(J.11)

A similar substitution in the second equation of
Equation

J.8a
gives, upon rearrangements,

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{v}\left[1-\tau_{1}\left(\sigma^{2}-{\mathcal{E}}_{z}(\tau_{1})\right)\right],

(J.12)

where
{\mathcal{E}}_{z}(\tau_{1})
is defined in
Equation

J.2
.
Further recalling
{\mathcal{E}}_{x}(\gamma_{1})
from
Equation

J.2
and
{\mathcal{S}}(\tau_{2},\gamma_{2})
from
Equation

J.3
, we collect
Equations

J.11
and
J.12
to arrive at the desired system of equations
Equations

J.4
and
J.5
.

For the phase retrieval model
q(z,\varepsilon)=\left|z\right|
with Gaussian prior
P_{{\mathsf{B}}_{*}}={\mathcal{N}}(0,\rho)
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{B}}_{*}\,|\,{\mathsf{R}}=r\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho r}{\gamma_{1}\rho+1},

(J.13)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\,|\,{\mathsf{P}}=p,{\mathsf{Y}}=y\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\int\varphi_{\sigma}(z)\varphi_{\sqrt{\tau_{1}-\sigma^{-2}}}(p-(\tau_{1}-\sigma^{-2})z)\delta(y-\left|z\right|)z\,\mathrm{d}z}{\int\varphi_{\sigma}(z^{\prime})\varphi_{\sqrt{\tau_{1}-\sigma^{-2}}}(p-(\tau_{1}-\sigma^{-2})z^{\prime})\delta(y-\left|z^{\prime}\right|)\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}}=y\tanh(py),

(J.14)

and therefore, by the tower property of conditional expectation,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{x}(\gamma_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{R}}^{2}\right]}{(\gamma_{1}\rho+1)^{2}}=\frac{\rho^{2}\gamma_{1}}{\gamma_{1}\rho+1},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{z}(\tau_{1})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Y}}^{2}\tanh({\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{Y}})^{2}\right],

(J.15)

where the second expectation is taken over the joint law in
Equation

J.7
which is equivalent to
Equation

J.1b
specialized to the phase retrieval model.
Using the expression of
{\mathcal{E}}_{x}(\gamma_{1})
in
Equation

J.15
, we can solve for
\gamma_{1}
in terms of
v
from the first equation in
Equation

J.4
which yields

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho-v}{\rho v}.

(J.16)

This then leads to the more succinct fixed point equation
v={\mathcal{F}}(v)
where
{\mathcal{F}}
is defined as
Equation

J.6
.
∎

J.2
Synthetic data

Phase retrieval.

Under the setting of
Section

6.2
, it is easy to verify that the function
\overline{g}
in
Equation

4.16
is given by

\displaystyle\overline{g}(y)

\displaystyle=y^{2}-1,

(J.17)

where
\gamma
is defined as per
Equation

4.2
.
The corresponding preprocessing function (see
Item

(A5)
) is therefore
{\mathcal{T}}(y)=1-\frac{\gamma}{y^{2}-1+\gamma}
, whereas the preprocessing function from
[
60
]
specializes to
{\mathcal{T}}(y)=1-\frac{1}{y^{2}}
.
The weak recovery condition
Equation

4.18
becomes

\displaystyle\delta

\displaystyle>\frac{\overline{m}_{2}^{\prime 2}}{\overline{m}_{4}^{\prime}}\frac{3}{2}.

The vector denoisers
g_{x1},g_{z1}
(see
Equations

4.32
and
4.33
) used in Bayes-GVAMP admit explicit expressions which have been derived in
Equations

J.13
and
J.14
.

Poisson regression.

In this setting, elementary calculations show that
\overline{g}
in
Equation

4.16
evaluates to

\displaystyle\overline{g}(y)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{3}\left(\frac{1}{2}+y\right)-1.

The preprocessing functions from
Item

(A5)
and
[
60
]
can then be evaluated accordingly.
The criticality condition
Equation

4.18
becomes

\displaystyle\delta

\displaystyle>\frac{\overline{m}_{2}^{\prime 2}}{\overline{m}_{4}^{\prime}}\frac{7}{4}.

For Bayes-GVAMP, though the denoiser
g_{x1}
is still given by the simple Gaussian MMSE estimator
Equation

J.13
,
g_{z1}
in
Equation

4.33
and its second moment (required by
Equation

4.34
) are more tricky to evaluate:

\displaystyle g_{z1}(p,y;\tau_{1})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\,|\,{\mathsf{P}}=p,{\mathsf{Y}}=y\right],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[g_{z1}({\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{Y}};\tau_{1})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{Z}}\,|\,{\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{Y}}\right]^{2}\right],

(J.18)

where

\displaystyle({\mathsf{Z}},\overline{{\mathsf{K}}})

\displaystyle\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,\sigma^{2})\otimes{\mathcal{N}}(0,1),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{P}}

\displaystyle=(\tau_{1}-\sigma^{-2}){\mathsf{Z}}+\sqrt{\tau_{1}-\sigma^{-2}}\,\overline{{\mathsf{K}}},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}\sim\pois({\mathsf{Z}}^{2}).

Under the above law,
Equation

J.18
can be written equivalently as

\displaystyle g_{z1}(p,y;\tau_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{G}}(p;\tau_{1})^{2y+1}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{G}}(p;\tau_{1})^{2y}\right]},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[g_{z1}({\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{Y}};\tau_{1})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{G}}({\mathsf{P}};\tau_{1})^{2{\mathsf{Y}}+1}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathsf{G}}({\mathsf{P}};\tau_{1})^{2{\mathsf{Y}}}\right]}\right)^{2}\right],

(J.19)

where the random variable
{\mathsf{G}}(p;\tau_{1})
is distributed according to

\displaystyle{\mathsf{G}}(p;\tau_{1})

\displaystyle\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(\frac{p}{\tau_{1}+2},\frac{1}{\tau_{1}+2}\right).

The difficulty in evaluating
Equation

J.19
is two-fold:
the ratio in
g_{z1}
involves moments of a non-centered Gaussian whose values can quickly reach machine precision for moderate values of
y\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
;
the second moment of
g_{z1}
involves nested expectations over
3
variables
{\mathsf{Z}},\overline{{\mathsf{K}}},{\mathsf{Y}}
.
To overcome the difficulty, we make use of the following observation.

Proposition J.2
.

Fix
\mu,a,b\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
\sigma>0
.
Let
{\mathsf{G}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(\mu,\sigma^{2})
and denote for any
k\geq 0
,

\displaystyle R_{k+1}(\mu,\sigma^{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[(a+b{\mathsf{G}})^{k+1}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[(a+b{\mathsf{G}})^{k}\right]}.

Then we have
R_{1}(\mu,\sigma^{2})=a+b\mu
; and for
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
,

\displaystyle R_{k+1}(\mu,\sigma^{2})

\displaystyle=a+b\left(\mu+\sigma^{2}kb\frac{1}{R_{k}(\mu,\sigma^{2})}\right).

The proof follows immediately from Stein’s lemma applied to the numerator of
R_{k+1}
; we omit the details.

Proposition

J.2
allows us to evaluate
g_{z1}
recursively (over
y
) without evaluating its numerator and denominator individually.
For the second moment of
g_{z1}
, we further combine
Proposition

J.2
with the
2
-dimensional Gauss–Hermite quadrature
[
85
, Section 4.6.1]
and employ the following approximation scheme (with suitably large
N,Y
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[g_{z1}({\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{Y}};\tau_{1})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\approx\sum_{z=1}^{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}w_{N}(x_{z})w_{N}(x_{k})\sum_{y=0}^{Y}\frac{(\sigma x_{z})^{2y}e^{-(\sigma x_{z})^{2}}}{y!}R_{2y+1}\left(\frac{(\tau_{1}-\sigma^{-2})\sigma x_{z}+\sqrt{\tau_{1}-\sigma^{-2}}\,x_{k}}{\tau_{1}+2},\frac{1}{\tau_{1}+2}\right)^{2},

where the weights
w_{N}(\cdot)
are given by

\displaystyle w_{N}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{N-1}N!}{N^{2}H_{N-1}(x)^{2}},

with
H_{N}
denoting the degree-
N
physicists’ Hermite polynomial and
(x_{i}/\sqrt{2})_{1\leq i\leq N}
its roots.
In the numerics, we find it efficient and accurate to take the degree of the quadrature to be
N=50
and the upper bound
Y
on
y
to be
10
standard deviations larger than the mean of the Poisson.
This approximation scheme is deterministic and avoids stochastic errors of Monte Carlo method which we find non-negligible even with reasonably large sample sizes.
Similar approximation scheme involving the recursion in
Proposition

J.2
and quadrature is used to evaluate analogous quantities in GAMP and its state evolution
[
72
]
.

J.3
Real data

Coded diffraction patterns.

Coded diffraction patterns over the reals used in
Section

6.2
are constructed similarly to their complex analogues in
[
17
, Section 2.2]
.
For
\delta\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
,
X\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\delta\times d}
is defined as

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}C_{d}^{\top}D_{1}&\cdots&C_{d}^{\top}D_{\delta}\end{bmatrix}^{\top},

where
C_{d}\in{\mathbb{O}}(d)
is the discrete cosine transform (DCT) matrix and each
D_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
(for
1\leq i\leq\delta
) is an independent diagonal matrix with diagonal elements i.i.d. according to the law of a certain random variable
{\mathsf{D}}
.
For binary and ternary CDP,
\law({\mathsf{D}})
is taken to be

\displaystyle\law({\mathsf{D}})

\displaystyle=\bern(1/2)

and

\displaystyle\law({\mathsf{D}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Rademacher}+\frac{1}{2}\delta_{0}

respectively.
Recalling
Equation

3.8
, by orthogonality of
C_{d}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{d}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{\delta}{\mathsf{D}}_{i}^{2},

where
({\mathsf{D}}_{i})_{1\leq i\leq\delta}
are i.i.d. copies of
{\mathsf{D}}
.
However, note that
X
is neither left- nor right-orthogonally invariant in law.

GTEx.

The design matrices used in
Section

6.2
are obtained by sequentially applying the following standard preprocessing steps
[
27
]
to the GTEx datasets ‘Skin - Sun Exposed (Lower leg)’ and ‘Muscle - Skeletal’
[
58
]
respectively.

1.

Remove rows consisting of all zeros;

2.

Build a matrix by sequentially including each row from above only if it has an overlap at most
0.3
with all existing rows;

3.

Standardize all rows collected from the previous step.

The resulting matrices are entirely deterministic and, in particular, do not satisfy
Item

(A2)
.
The only randomness involved in the experiments in
Section

6.2
lies in the generation of
\beta_{*}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{d},I_{d})
.

Appendix K
Auxiliary lemmas

Propositions

K.1
,
K.2
,
K.4
and
K.3
are a few facts regarding Wasserstein convergence of empirical measures collected from
[
46
, Appendices E and F.1]
and
[
56
, Appendix G.1]
.

Proposition K.1
.

Fix
p\geq 1,k\geq 1,\ell\geq 0
.
Let
E\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times\ell}
be a deterministic matrix satisfying
E\xrightarrow{W_{p}}{\mathsf{E}}^{\top}
.
If
V\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times k}
have i.i.d. rows according to the law of
{\mathsf{V}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
with finite mixed moments up to order
p
, then
\begin{bmatrix}V&E\end{bmatrix}\xrightarrow{W_{p}}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{V}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{E}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}
where
{\mathsf{V}}
is independent of
{\mathsf{E}}
.

Proposition K.2
.

Fix
p,q\geq 1
and
k,\ell\geq 1
.
Suppose that
V\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times k}
satisfies
V\xrightarrow{W_{p+q}}{\mathsf{V}}^{\top}
.
Let
g\in\mathrm{PG}^{q}_{k\to\ell}
(see
Definition

3.2
) be continuous.
Then
g(V)\xrightarrow{W_{p}}g({\mathsf{V}})^{\top}
, where
g
is applied row-wise to
V
.

Proposition K.3
.

Fix
p\geq 1,k\geq 1
.
Suppose that
V\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times k}
satisfies
V\xrightarrow{W_{p}}{\mathsf{V}}^{\top}
.
Let
f\in\mathrm{PG}^{p}_{k\to 1}
be continuous everywhere except on a set of probability
0
under the law of
{\mathsf{V}}
.
Then almost surely,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(V_{i,:})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[f({\mathsf{V}})\right].

Proposition K.4
.

Fix
p,k,\ell\geq 1
.
Suppose
V\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times k}
,
W\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times\ell}
, and
M_{n},M\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k\times\ell}
satisfy
V\xrightarrow{W_{p}}{\mathsf{V}}^{\top}
,
W\xrightarrow{W_{p}}0_{1\times\ell}
, and
M_{n}\to M
entrywise, almost surely.
Then
VM_{n}+W\xrightarrow{W_{p}}{\mathsf{V}}^{\top}M
.

Proposition

K.5
below is a generalization of
[
46
, Proposition F.2]
from
m=1
to
m\geq 1
fixed.

Proposition K.5
.

Fix
p\geq 1
,
k,\ell\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
.
Let
O\sim\haar({\mathbb{O}}(n-\ell))
.
Let
E\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times k}
,
V\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(n-\ell)\times m}
,
\Pi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times(n-\ell)}
be deterministic matrices satisfying the following:
E\xrightarrow{W_{p}}{\mathsf{E}}^{\top}
,
n^{-1}V^{\top}V\to\Sigma
entry-wise, and
\Pi
has orthonormal columns.
Then
\begin{bmatrix}\Pi OV&E\end{bmatrix}\xrightarrow{W_{p}}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Z}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{E}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}
where
{\mathsf{Z}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{m},\Sigma)
is independent of
{\mathsf{E}}
.

Let
j\geq 0
be fixed, and
d
a dimension that grows jointly with
n
.
Fix a constant
j\geq 0
.
Let
F\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times j}
be deterministic satisfying
F\xrightarrow{W_{p}}{\mathsf{F}}^{\top}
.
Let
U\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times m}
be the first
d
rows of
\Pi OV
if
d\leq n
, or
\Pi OV
appended by
d-n
i.i.d. rows distributed as
{\mathcal{N}}(0_{m},\Sigma)
if
d>n
.
Then
\begin{bmatrix}U&F\end{bmatrix}\xrightarrow{W_{p}}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{U}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{F}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}
where
{\mathsf{U}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{m},\Sigma)
is independent of
{\mathsf{F}}
.

Proof.

Denote
n^{\prime}=n-\ell
and suppose that it is sufficiently large.
Note that left-multiplying
V
by
O
uniformly randomizes the left singular space of
V
while retaining its singular values.
Writing
V
in terms of its SVD we have
V=L\begin{bmatrix}\Lambda\\
0_{(n^{\prime}-m)\times m}\end{bmatrix}R^{\top}
. We can express the law of
OV
as

\displaystyle OV

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}O\begin{bmatrix}\Lambda\\
0_{(n^{\prime}-m)\times m}\end{bmatrix}R^{\top}\overset{d}{=}O\begin{bmatrix}R&0_{m\times(n^{\prime}-m)}\\
0_{(n^{\prime}-m)\times m}&I_{n^{\prime}-m}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\Lambda\\
0_{(n^{\prime}-m)\times m}\end{bmatrix}R^{\top}=O^{\prime}R\Lambda R^{\top}=O^{\prime}(V^{\top}V)^{1/2},

where
O^{\prime}
contains the first
m
columns of
O
.
We use the well-known representation of the law of a partial Haar matrix (see, e.g.,
[
98
, Section I-D]
):
O^{\prime}\overset{d}{=}H(H^{\top}H)^{-1/2}
, where
H\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n^{\prime}\times m}
consists of i.i.d. standard Gaussian entries.
Since
\Pi
has orthonormal columns,
\Pi^{\top}G\overset{d}{=}H
where
G\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times m}
consists of i.i.d. standard Gaussian entries.
Using both observations above, we have

\displaystyle\Pi OV

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}\Pi\Pi^{\top}G(G^{\top}\Pi\Pi^{\top}G)^{-1/2}(V^{\top}V)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=G(G^{\top}\Pi\Pi^{\top}G)^{-1/2}(V^{\top}V)^{1/2}-PG(G^{\top}\Pi\Pi^{\top}G)^{-1/2}(V^{\top}V)^{1/2},

(K.1)

where
P=I_{n}-\Pi\Pi^{\top}
.
By
Proposition

K.1
,

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}G&E\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W_{p}}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{G}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{E}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

(K.2)

where
{\mathsf{G}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{m},I_{m})
is independent of
{\mathsf{E}}
.
Since
\Pi^{\top}G
has i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
entries, we have
n^{-1}G^{\top}\Pi\Pi^{\top}G\to I_{m}
entry-wise, almost surely.
Therefore, recalling the assumption
n^{-1}V^{\top}V\to\Sigma
, we have

\displaystyle(G^{\top}\Pi\Pi^{\top}G)^{-1/2}(V^{\top}V)^{1/2}\to\Sigma^{1/2}

(K.3)

entry-wise, almost surely.

Next, we show that

\displaystyle PG

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W_{p}}0_{m}^{\top}.

(K.4)

The law of
PG
can be written as
PG\overset{d}{=}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\pi_{j}g_{j}^{\top}
where
\pi_{1},\cdots,\pi_{m}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
form an orthonormal basis of the column span of
P
and
g_{1},\cdots,g_{m}
are i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0_{m},I_{m})
vectors.
Write each
\pi_{j}
as
\begin{bmatrix}\pi_{1,j}&\cdots&\pi_{n,j}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}
.
For any fixed
p\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\pi_{i,j}g_{j}^{\top}\right\|_{2}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\left\|\pi_{i,j}g_{j}^{\top}\right\|_{2}\right)^{p}\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\ell^{1-1/p}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\left\|\pi_{i,j}g_{j}^{\top}\right\|_{2}^{p}\right)^{1/p}\right]^{p}

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell^{p-1}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\pi_{i,j}\right|^{p}\left\|g_{j}\right\|_{2}^{p}=\ell^{p-1}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\left\|g_{j}\right\|_{2}^{p}\frac{\left\|\pi_{j}\right\|_{p}^{p}}{n},

where we have used subadditivity of
\ell_{2}
norm and the inequality
\left\|x\right\|_{p}\leq\left\|x\right\|_{r}\leq d^{1/r-1/p}\left\|x\right\|_{p}
for any vector
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
0\leq r\leq p
.
If
p\geq 2
, then
n^{-1}\left\|\pi_{j}\right\|_{p}^{p}\leq n^{-1}\left\|\pi_{j}\right\|_{2}^{p}=n^{-1}
.
If
1\leq p<2
, then
n^{-1}\left\|\pi_{j}\right\|_{p}^{p}\leq n^{-1}\left(n^{1/p-1/2}\left\|\pi_{j}\right\|_{2}\right)^{p}=n^{-p/2}
.
In both cases,
n^{-1}\left\|\pi_{j}\right\|_{p}^{p}\to 0
.
Therefore, almost surely,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|(PG)_{i,:}\right\|_{2}^{p}

\displaystyle\to 0.

(K.5)

Furthermore, for any bounded Lipschitz
f\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\to{\mathbb{R}}
, by
Equation

K.5
, almost surely,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[f((PG)_{i,:})-f(0_{m}^{\top})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}C\left\|(PG)_{i,:}-0_{m}^{\top}\right\|_{2}\to 0.

(K.6)

Taking
Equations

K.5
and
K.6
collectively, we have almost surely,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f((PG)_{i,:})

\displaystyle\to f(0_{m}^{\top}),

for both
f(v)=\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{p}
and any bounded Lipschitz
f
.
Therefore
Equation

K.4
holds.
Combining
Equations

K.4
,
K.3
,
K.1
and
K.2
and applying
Proposition

K.4
yield the first convergence result for
\begin{bmatrix}\Pi OV&E\end{bmatrix}
:

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\Pi OV&E\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}\begin{bmatrix}G&E\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}(G^{\top}\Pi\Pi^{\top}G)^{-1/2}(V^{\top}V)^{1/2}&0_{m\times k}\\
0_{k\times m}&I_{k}\end{bmatrix}-\begin{bmatrix}PG(G^{\top}\Pi\Pi^{\top}G)^{-1/2}(V^{\top}V)^{1/2}&0_{n\times k}\end{bmatrix}\xrightarrow{W_{p}}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathsf{Z}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{E}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

where
{\mathsf{Z}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{m},\Sigma)
is independent of
{\mathsf{E}}
.

Next consider
U
.
Construct
\widetilde{G},W_{1},W_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times m}
as follows:

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}G_{1:d,:},&d\leq n\\
\begin{bmatrix}G\\
G^{\prime}\end{bmatrix},&d>n\end{cases},

\displaystyle W_{1}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\left[PG(G^{\top}\Pi\Pi^{\top}G)^{-1/2}(V^{\top}V)^{1/2}\right]_{1:d,:},&d\leq n\\
\begin{bmatrix}PG(G^{\top}\Pi\Pi^{\top}G)^{-1/2}(V^{\top}V)^{1/2}\\
0_{(d-n)\times m}\end{bmatrix},&d>n\end{cases},

\displaystyle W_{2}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}0_{d\times m},&d\leq n\\
\begin{bmatrix}0_{n\times m}\\
G^{\prime}\left[(G^{\top}\Pi\Pi^{\top}G)^{-1/2}(V^{\top}V)^{1/2}-\Sigma\right]\end{bmatrix},&d>n\end{cases},

where
G^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(d-n)\times m}
(if
d>n
) contains i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
entries, independent of everything else.
By construction, it is easy to verify that

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle=\widetilde{G}(G^{\top}\Pi\Pi^{\top}G)^{-1/2}(V^{\top}V)^{1/2}-W_{1}-W_{2}.

Again by
Proposition

K.1
,

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{G}&F\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{W_{p}}\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{{\mathsf{G}}}^{\top}&{\mathsf{F}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

(K.7)

where
\widetilde{{\mathsf{G}}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{m},I_{m})
is independent of
{\mathsf{F}}
.
The same argument as before shows
W_{1}\xrightarrow{W_{p}}0_{m}^{\top}
.
Also,
W_{2}\xrightarrow{W_{p}}0_{m}^{\top}
.
To verify this, we only need to consider the case
d>n
.
Almost surely,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d-n}\left\|G^{\prime}_{i,:}\left[(G^{\top}\Pi\Pi^{\top}G)^{-1/2}(V^{\top}V)^{1/2}-\Sigma\right]\right\|_{2}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(G^{\top}\Pi\Pi^{\top}G)^{-1/2}(V^{\top}V)^{1/2}-\Sigma\right\|_{2}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d-n}\left\|G^{\prime}_{i,:}\right\|_{2}^{p}\to 0.

Using this, one can then verify
Equation

3.3
for any bounded Lipschitz
f
, thereby justifying the
W_{p}
-convergence of
W_{2}
; the argument is similar to
Equation

K.6
.
Combining these with
Equation

K.7
and applying
Proposition

K.4
then give the convergence result for
\begin{bmatrix}U&F\end{bmatrix}
.
∎

The following result identifies the distribution of a Haar matrix conditioned on it solving a linear system.

Proposition K.6

(
[
86
, Lemma 4]
)
.

Fix
k\geq 1
.
Let
X,Y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times k}
be deterministic matrices with rank
k
such that
QY=X
for some
Q\in{\mathbb{O}}(n)
.
If
O\in\haar({\mathbb{O}}(n))
, then

\displaystyle O\,|\,\left\{OY=X\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}X(X^{\top}X)^{-1}Y^{\top}+\Pi_{X^{\perp}}\widetilde{O}\Pi_{Y^{\perp}}^{\top}=X(Y^{\top}Y)^{-1}Y^{\top}+\Pi_{X^{\perp}}\widetilde{O}\Pi_{Y^{\perp}}^{\top},

where
\widetilde{O}\sim\haar({\mathbb{O}}(n-k))
is independent of everything else and
\Pi_{X^{\perp}},\Pi_{Y^{\perp}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times(n-k)}
are (any) matrices with orthonormal columns spanning
\spn\left\{\left\{X_{:,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{k}\right\}^{\perp},\spn\left\{\left\{Y_{:,i}\right\}_{i=1}^{k}\right\}^{\perp}
, respectively.
